- the initiation of a Binding whose later reliance or breach conditions remain active;
- the establishment of an expectation whose Non-Occurrence leaves persistent residue;
- a Frame change that reorganizes which continuations become visible or authorized;
- a threshold after which repair no longer restores the earlier corridor without residue.

Earlier material may remain relevant as prehistory without becoming part of the claimed Trajectory. Prehistory may explain why the entry condition was available, but it must not be silently converted into a constitutive segment merely because it precedes the selected beginning.

```text
preceding event
≠ constitutive Trajectory beginning automatically

relevant prehistory
≠ included Trajectory segment automatically
```

An entry point chosen only because it makes the later direction appear coherent is under endpoint-selection pressure. The analyst must show why the selected beginning changes the reconstruction of sedimentation or present historical load.

#### Ending, analytical cut, and open continuation

The end of the **recorded interval** is not necessarily the end of the Trajectory object. Chapter 11 distinguishes:

- a **closed Trajectory claim**, where the relevant historical object is reconstructed as concluded within the declared frame and period;
- an **open Trajectory claim**, where the analytical cut is current or provisional and further continuation remains possible;
- an **interrupted Trajectory**, where the carrier is disrupted but residue or re-entry conditions remain;
- a **dormant Trajectory trace**, where historical structure remains recoverable but is not presently constitutive without additional evidence;
- a **transformed continuation**, where reference and lineage remain traceable despite changed surface form or Frame;
- a **terminated claim**, where later continuation is outside the declared scope without a claim that the historical process itself ceased.

```text
end of source window
≠ end of Trajectory automatically

present analytical cut
≠ completed history

open continuation
≠ prediction of continuation
```

Open continuation must be stated without forecasting a specific result. It records unresolved continuation possibilities, not expected destiny.

#### Periodization and segment lineage

A Trajectory may contain phases with different carriers, directions, intensities, or profile combinations. Periodization is therefore permitted where it improves discrimination and remains source-supported.

Possible segments include:

- formation;
- reinforcement;
- redistribution;
- interruption;
- repair;
- dormancy;
- re-entry;
- reversal;
- translation into a changed Frame;
- partial closure.

Segment boundaries must preserve lineage. A later segment may alter the Trajectory without erasing the earlier result. Conversely, the use of a new label or phase name does not prove that a new Trajectory began.

```text
new segment
≠ new reference object automatically

same reference object
≠ one homogeneous segment

changed Frame
≠ erased historical lineage
```

Where a prior Trajectory claim exists, the new boundary declaration must state whether it:

- extends the earlier interval;
- narrows it;
- subdivides it;
- revises its entry or endpoint;
- preserves it as a parallel construction;
- or replaces a failed claim with a new testable claim.

No boundary revision retroactively repairs an unsupported historical-load claim.

#### Boundary sensitivity

A boundary is analytically load-bearing when a relevant change to its start, endpoint, or included segments alters at least one warranted conclusion concerning:

- the historical carrier;
- cumulative change;
- persistent residue;
- profile-specific accumulation;
- Changed Action Corridors;
- directionality;
- repair or reversal;
- open continuation;
- uncertainty or loss;
- claim scope.

If substantially different boundaries leave every substantive conclusion unchanged, the selected periodization may be merely presentational. If small arbitrary shifts radically change the label while the source trace remains weak, the Trajectory claim may be unstable or overfit.

```text
boundary sensitivity
≠ permission to choose the most dramatic interval
```

The purpose is to test claim dependence on periodization, not to maximize narrative contrast.

#### C11-BOUND-01 — two defensible boundaries

A distributed review system develops through several phases:

1. an early voluntary coordination practice;
2. a formal rule that adopts the practice;
3. repeated delayed reviews under the formal rule;
4. a later repair that restores review but retains centralized records and unequal conversion costs.

Two source-responsible Trajectory constructions are proposed.

**Construction A** begins with the voluntary practice. It claims that lowered coordination friction and shared-format reliance were already sedimenting before formal adoption.

**Construction B** begins with the formal rule. It claims that the relevant Trajectory for authorization burden and review residue starts only when the institutional expectation and review window become explicit.

Both use the same present analytical cut. Their differences are material:

```text
Construction A:
  longer Attractor and Binding lineage
  weaker early authorization claim

Construction B:
  shorter institutional lineage
  clearer Residual and Asymmetry carrier
```

Neither boundary is automatically uniquely correct. Construction A is bounded for a coordination-history claim; Construction B is bounded for a formal-governance claim. They must remain separate because merging them would blur the difference between informal sedimentation and institutionally specified Non-Event residue.

The local result is:

```text
separate bounded constructions
```

This result does not establish that both constructions support the same claim, nor that every alternative beginning is admissible.

#### Boundary failure and no-erasure

Boundary pressure requires claim reduction or Failure where:

- the start is selected only from the known endpoint;
- relevant prehistory is concealed while still doing explanatory work;
- the analytical cut is presented as natural closure;
- later repair or reversal is omitted by ending the interval early;
- an open continuation is narrated as completed direction;
- segment labels replace carrier continuity;
- a new boundary is used to erase a prior failed claim.

A failed boundary may still preserve:

- a warranted Path segment;
- a local accumulation finding;
- a documented Frame change;
- a bounded present constraint;
- an unresolved rival periodization.

```text
failed Trajectory boundary
≠ erased history
≠ erased Path segment
```

<a id="11-11-trajectory-compression"></a>

### 11.11 Trajectory Compression

**Trajectory Compression** is the necessary selective representation of a historically extended object at a coarser narrative or analytical resolution while retaining the structures required to reconstruct its bounded claim.

Compression is unavoidable. A Trajectory cannot reproduce every event, local variation, source fragment, or intermediate configuration. The question is therefore not whether compression occurs, but whether the compressed object remains traceably loaded.

```text
Trajectory Compression
=
selective reduction with retained constitutive trace

Trajectory Compression
≠ lossless summary
≠ macro-label substitution
```

Chapter 11 establishes the object-level preservation duties for representing a Trajectory. Chapter 15 later owns the complete `COMPOSE` procedure, including operation-specific source selection, formation rules, and full compression accounting. Nothing in this section preauthorizes a `COMPOSE` occurrence.

#### Preservation burden

A compressed Trajectory must preserve, where constitutive to the claim:

- the Path reference and actual-traversal lineage;
- load-bearing configurations and transitions;
- temporal ordering and relevant duration;
- central Non-Events and their expectation frames;
- branch points, blocked or lost alternatives, and relevant non-selections;
- Attractor, Asymmetry, Binding, and Residual carriers;
- cumulative changes and Changed Action Corridors;
- reversals, repairs, interruptions, dormancy, and re-entry;
- current-condition contributions and rival explanations;
- open continuation and disputed periodization;
- source uncertainty and irrecoverable loss.

Not every item must appear at equal detail. The analyst must show why an omitted detail is non-constitutive for the declared claim.

```text
less detail
≠ less traceability automatically

more detail
≠ better compression automatically
```

A dense chronology can obscure the carrier just as a macro-label can erase it.

#### Compression declaration

Trajectory Compression uses the canonical Loss structure:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

For a Trajectory, these fields should answer:

- **preserved:** which transitions, Non-Events, profiles, alternatives, repairs, and corridor effects remain directly visible?
- **compressed:** which repeated or parallel structures are represented as clusters, phases, or ranges?
- **excluded:** which materials or subpaths fall outside the declared frame or claim?
- **uncertain:** which chronology, carrier relation, or periodization remains unresolved?
- **irrecoverable:** which local differences cannot be reconstructed after compression or from the available sources?

The declaration must distinguish deliberate analytical compression from information already absent in the sources.

```text
compressed by analysis
≠ unavailable in source
≠ Non-Event
```

#### Load-bearing transitions and Non-Events

A transition is load-bearing where removing or substantially changing it would alter the warranted Trajectory construction. A Non-Event is load-bearing where its framed Non-Occurrence changes residue, alternatives, cost, timing, or continuation conditions.

Compression may group repeated transitions when their relevant relation remains reconstructable. It may not silently replace a heterogeneous sequence with a uniform trend where reversals, unequal effects, or changing Frames matter.

```text
repeated similar transition cluster
→ may be compressed with declared range and variation

heterogeneous phases
→ may not be flattened into one direction without loss disclosure
```

Central Non-Events are especially vulnerable to disappearance because compressed histories often privilege positive events. Their omission can transform a Trajectory of delayed review, blocked repair, or repeated non-decision into a misleading sequence of formal milestones.

#### Reversal, repair, and heterogeneity

Compression must retain counterpressure. A Trajectory may include:

- local reversal without full reset;
- partial repair with persistent residue;
- redistribution rather than monotonic accumulation;
- competing subpaths;
- different effects across roles;
- a changed Frame that translates rather than erases the carrier;
- periods of weak or dormant historical load.

A macro-description such as “continuous centralization,” “steady stabilization,” or “progressive closure” is inadmissible where the source history includes constitutive decentralization, reopening, repair, or role-specific divergence that the label cannot represent.

```text
compressed direction
≠ monotonic history

stable macro-function
≠ homogeneous internal Trajectory
```

#### Counterfactual compression test

A compressed Trajectory should be tested by asking:

> Would restoring an omitted transition, Non-Event, reversal, alternative, or role-specific burden materially alter the warranted claim?

Possible results include:

- the omission is non-constitutive and compression remains admissible;
- the claim remains but requires a bounded qualification;
- a profile or direction claim must be reduced;
- the Trajectory boundary must be revised;
- the compressed object fails because the omitted structure is constitutive;
- available sources do not permit adjudication.

Compression is above the Traceability Ceiling where the same label survives every relevant change to its source history.

#### Macro-label substitution

Macro-label substitution occurs when a large historical term replaces rather than represents the traceable Path and sedimentation relation.

Typical indicators include:

- the label is applied before constitutive transitions are declared;
- no central Non-Event or alternative remains visible;
- internal phases are treated as irrelevant by definition;
- relevant source changes do not alter the label;
- the label performs explanatory work that the source trace does not support;
- the label carries a target function or normative implication without `PROJECT_AS` or application authority.

```text
macro-label
+ selected examples
≠ compressed Trajectory automatically
```

The correct result may be `claim_reduction_required`, `failed_transformation`, or `mandatory_stop`, depending on whether weaker findings remain and whether the failed object is used for stronger derivation.

#### C11-COMP-01 — omitted reversal and central Non-Event

An analyst summarizes a fifteen-year institutional history as a “continuous centralization Trajectory.” The underlying Path includes:

- an early consolidation of review authority;
- a later decentralization pilot that temporarily redistributes access and lowers exit burden;
- a formally expected evaluation of the pilot that never occurs;
- expiration of the pilot’s legal basis during the missing evaluation;
- renewed centralization under a different technical standard;
- persistent local records that make a later decentralization route possible but costly.

The macro-label omits both the decentralization phase and the missing evaluation. As a result, it converts a contingent sequence of consolidation, partial reversal, Non-Event, expiry, and renewed consolidation into a monotonic direction.

A responsible compression must preserve at least:

```text
initial consolidation
→ partial decentralizing reversal
→ expected evaluation does not occur
→ pilot basis expires
→ renewed centralization under changed conditions
→ costly residual reopening possibility
```

The original “continuous centralization” claim fails. A reduced claim may remain:

```text
recurrent centralizing outcome
with interrupted and contingently restored dominance
```

The local result is:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

The Path, centralization episodes, decentralization pilot, Residual Accumulation, and present reopening cost remain preserved. Compression failure does not erase them.

<a id="11-12-competing-trajectory-constructions"></a>

### 11.12 Competing Trajectory Constructions

**Competing Trajectory Constructions** are two or more source-responsible reconstructions of overlapping historical material that differ in boundary, periodization, constitutive selection, profile weighting, directionality, or claim scope.

Their existence is not a defect. It is often a consequence of multiple valid analytical questions, incomplete sources, heterogeneous historical carriers, or disputed periodization.

```text
same material
≠ one uniquely true Trajectory automatically

multiple constructions
≠ all constructions equally admissible
```

Each construction must independently satisfy the Path substrate, sedimentation, present-effect, source-trace, boundary, compression, and claim-ceiling burdens.

#### Sources of competition

Constructions may differ because they select:

- different but defensible beginnings;
- different present analytical cuts;
- different relevant Frames;
- different granularities;
- different load-bearing transitions;
- different central Non-Events;
- different segment boundaries;
- different operator-profile emphasis;
- different present corridor dimensions;
- different source bases or uncertainty treatments;
- different questions and claim scopes.

A difference in construction does not automatically produce contradiction. Two Trajectories may be compatible if one reconstructs coordination friction and another reconstructs authorization burden. They may conflict if they make incompatible claims about the same reference, frame, interval, and dimension.

#### Comparison basis

Competing constructions should be compared across a common declaration:

| Dimension | Construction A | Construction B |
| --- | --- | --- |
| reference object |  |  |
| frame |  |  |
| temporal boundary |  |  |
| granularity |  |  |
| source basis |  |  |
| constitutive transitions |  |  |
| central Non-Events |  |  |
| profile carriers |  |  |
| directionality |  |  |
| present corridor effects |  |  |
| compression and loss |  |  |
| rival explanations |  |  |
| claim scope |  |  |

Comparison must not force commensurability where the constructions answer materially different questions.

#### Relations among competing constructions

Possible relations include:

- **compatible:** both support mutually consistent bounded claims;
- **complementary:** each captures a different constitutive dimension of the same historical object;
- **nested:** one interval or granularity is contained within another while retaining distinct claim scope;
- **partially overlapping:** some segments or carriers are shared, others differ;
- **incomparable:** Frames, granularities, or questions do not support direct ranking;
- **conflicting:** the constructions make incompatible claims under sufficiently comparable conditions;
- **underdetermined:** sources do not decide which constitutive selection or periodization is better supported;
- **failed versus retained:** one construction exceeds the source or claim ceiling while another remains bounded.

These are analytical relations, not new Output Classes. Local results must still map to the canonical inventory.

#### Adjudication discipline

Where constructions are directly comparable, adjudication should test:

- Path evidence;
- boundary rationale;
- Source–Result Dependency;
- preservation of counterpressure;
- compression loss;
- current-condition allocation;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- explanatory discrimination;
- source and claim ceilings.

A construction does not win because it is longer, more detailed, more coherent, more formal, or uses more operator vocabulary.

```text
formal elegance
≠ comparative warrant

broader interval
≠ better Trajectory
```

Where one construction omits constitutive counterpressure, it may require reduction or Failure. Where both remain bounded for different questions, both may be admissible with bounded claims. Where materially conflicting constructions cannot be adjudicated from available sources, Non-Capture remains available.

#### No forced synthesis

Competing constructions must not be merged merely to avoid conflict. A synthetic history can become less discriminating than either rival by combining incompatible boundaries, profile carriers, or direction claims.

```text
rival constructions
+ merged vocabulary
≠ integrated Trajectory automatically
```

Integration would require a new bounded claim and, where multiple source objects are compositionally formed, later operation-specific testing.

#### C11-COMPETE-01 — different questions, both bounded

The same organizational history is reconstructed in two ways.

**Construction A — coordination Trajectory**

- begins with voluntary template sharing;
- tracks Attractor Sedimentation and Binding Accumulation;
- treats formal rule adoption as a later reinforcement;
- claims lower coordination friction and higher switching cost at the present cut.

**Construction B — accountability Trajectory**

- begins with the formal review obligation;
- tracks Residual and Asymmetry Accumulation;
- treats earlier template sharing as prehistory;
- claims delayed review, unequal evidentiary burden, and a narrowed challenge corridor.

The constructions overlap but answer different questions. Their boundaries and constitutive carriers are explicit. Neither claims to exhaust the other.

The local results are:

```text
Construction A:
  admissible_with_bounded_claim

Construction B:
  admissible_with_bounded_claim
```

The coexistence of the two does not create a combined stronger claim or increased authority.

#### C11-COMPETE-02 — undecidable constitutive selection

A long-running inter-organizational arrangement has incomplete records for its first five years. Two later source clusters are reliable but support different reconstructions.

**Construction A** treats an early informal routing practice as the carrier that later formal rules stabilize.

**Construction B** treats the formal rule as a discontinuity that creates a new Trajectory, while earlier practice remains merely analogous and insufficiently traceable.

Both constructions:

- preserve the same later Path;
- disclose the early source gap;
- identify different beginnings;
- produce different Binding and Attractor lineages;
- remain sensitive to the missing early records;
- avoid claiming unique truth.

The available sources cannot determine whether continuity or discontinuity is the better constitutive reconstruction. Merging the constructions would conceal the precise dispute.

The local result is:

```text
non_capture
```

Non-Capture does not validate both constructions as true. It records that responsible adjudication is unavailable under the declared source basis.

<a id="11-13-false-trajectory"></a>

### 11.13 False Trajectory

A **False Trajectory** is a claimed Trajectory whose Path substrate, sedimentation, present historical load, boundary, compression, or non-teleological direction cannot be sustained under the declared sources and claim scope.

False Trajectory is a diagnostic phrase, not a new object class and not an eleventh Output Class.

```text
False Trajectory
=
failed or overclaimed Trajectory construction

False Trajectory
≠ no history
≠ no Path
≠ no recurrence
```

The purpose of the diagnosis is to prevent a stronger historical label from laundering weaker or failed findings.

#### False Trajectory conditions

A Trajectory claim is false or unsustained where one or more constitutive failures remain uncompensated:

- only chronology or duration is present;
- the Path substrate is absent, failed, or silently revised;
- repetition lacks cumulative or sedimented present effect;
- historical interest replaces present praxis relevance;
- direction is inferred only backward from the endpoint;
- alternatives, reversals, repairs, or current-condition explanations are removed;
- a disputed boundary is presented as natural or uniquely true;
- compression hides constitutive transitions or Non-Events;
- a macro-label replaces traceable Path structure;
- operator symbols substitute for occurrence evidence;
- current conditions fully account for the claimed effect while historical attribution remains insensitive;
- a Trajectory is equated with strong Path Dependence;
- a Trajectory is assigned a frame-function, macro-event function, or attractor-function without `PROJECT_AS`;
- formal Record completeness is treated as substantive proof.

No single surface indicator decides every case. The failure must be tied to the claim’s constitutive burden.

#### Preservation of weaker findings

A failed Trajectory claim does not erase every lower-level result. Depending on the sources, the following may remain warranted:

- chronology;
- duration;
- a sequence;
- one or more transitions;
- a Path or Path segment;
- repeated configurations;
- current recurrence;
- local Asymmetry;
- discrete Bindings;
- a framed Non-Event;
- persistent but non-constitutive residue;
- a present action constraint;
- a rival construction;
- unresolved source uncertainty.

```text
failed Trajectory
→ preserve warranted weaker findings
→ reduce or fail only the unsupported claim
```

This no-erasure rule prevents both overclaim and analytical waste.

#### Canonical dispositions

False-Trajectory pressure may map to:

- `admissible_with_bounded_claim` where a narrower Trajectory survives;
- `admissible_but_provisional` where the claim remains source-responsible but materially incomplete;
- `claim_reduction_required` where Path, profile, or present-condition findings survive but the Trajectory label is too strong;
- `failed_transformation` where the delimited Trajectory construction cannot be sustained;
- `mandatory_stop` where a known failed Trajectory is used to derive a stronger claim;
- `non_capture` where responsible competing constructions cannot be adjudicated.

`resolution_neutral` may apply to a finer distinction that changes no warranted Trajectory result, but it must not protect an unsupported claim.

#### C11-FALSE-01 — repetition and coherence without present load

A twenty-five-year professional network holds annual meetings, uses similar language, and preserves a continuous archive. An analyst claims a “deep institutional Trajectory” that determines present coordination.

The source review shows:

- the meetings are recurrent;
- membership and procedures change substantially;
- the archive is rarely used in current decisions;
- present coordination is governed by a recently introduced external platform;
- removing the earlier meeting history does not materially change the reconstruction of current access, cost, authorization, or continuation;
- no persistent Non-Event residue, cumulative Asymmetry, layered Binding, or changed corridor is established.

The materials support duration, recurrence, and a warranted historical Path. They do not support sedimented present load.

The result is:

```text
claim_reduction_required
```

Preserved findings:

```text
long-duration recurring Path
+ archival continuity
+ current platform-produced coordination
```

The False Trajectory diagnosis does not deny the network’s history. It denies the stronger claim that the history presently co-determines coordination in the asserted way.

#### C11-STOP-01 — failed Trajectory used for stronger derivation

After the Trajectory claim in `C11-FALSE-01` has been reduced, a later analysis states:

```text
because the network has a deep Trajectory,
its current coordination is strongly path-dependent
and the Trajectory functions as the governing Frame
```

This chain inherits the failed premise and adds both a Chapter-12 Path-Dependence claim and a RETYPE frame-function claim without separate tests.

```text
known failed Trajectory
→ Path Dependence inference
→ frame-function inference
```

The required result is:

```text
mandatory_stop
```

A new analysis may proceed only through explicit lineage and new evidence:

1. establish a new bounded Trajectory claim or retain only the warranted Path;
2. separately test Path Dependence in Chapter 12 terms;
3. separately test `PROJECT_AS` in the relevant RETYPE context.

Changing the label, boundary, granularity, or target function does not erase the earlier failure.

<a id="11-14-minimal-trajectory-record"></a>

### 11.14 Minimal Trajectory Record

The **Minimal Trajectory Record** is a compact view of the declarations required to inspect a Trajectory claim. It maps into the Chapter-7 Shared Transformation Record and does not create an independent schema.

```yaml
trajectory:
  path_reference:
  temporal_scope:
  sedimented_attractors:
  accumulated_asymmetries:
  persistent_residues:
  binding_changes:
  changed_action_corridors:
  irreversible_transitions:
  open_continuations:
  competing_constructions:
  claim_scope:
```

```text
Minimal Trajectory Record
≠ complete Shared Transformation Record
≠ substantive Trajectory warrant
```

#### Field interpretation

| Field | Minimum function | Must not imply |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `path_reference` | identifies the warranted Path substrate, segments, status lineage, and prior results | that every Path became a Trajectory |
| `temporal_scope` | declares start, endpoint or analytical cut, prehistory, open continuation, and disputed periodization | natural or uniquely true boundary |
| `sedimented_attractors` | records supported `Α + Θ` carriers and changed friction or default accessibility | fused operator, necessity, or attractor-function |
| `accumulated_asymmetries` | records supported `Ω + Θ` dimensions, distribution, exit effects, repair, and current load | moral rank, legitimacy, or person type |
| `persistent_residues` | records expectation-bound `Λ + Θ` carriers, windows, closure, dormancy, and remaining effect | missing information as Non-Event or permanence |
| `binding_changes` | records supported `Ψ + Θ` layering, reliance, breach, reopening, transfer, weakening, or release | inner state, consent, duty, or mandatory continuation |
| `changed_action_corridors` | records bounded present changes in accessibility, visibility, authorization, affordability, coordination, timing, reversibility, expectedness, proof burden, or exposure | prediction, instruction, eliminated agency, or fifth profile |
| `irreversible_transitions` | records source-bound transitions whose relevant costs, residues, or lost alternatives are not restored by later change | absolute metaphysical irreversibility |
| `open_continuations` | records unresolved or still available continuations at the analytical cut | forecast, destiny, or recommendation |
| `competing_constructions` | records rival boundaries, selections, claims, comparison status, and unresolved adjudication | that every rival is admissible or true |
| `claim_scope` | limits reference, frame, level, granularity, temporal reach, source basis, and authority | inherited generalization or application authority |

A field may be empty, absent in substance, or uncertain where the corresponding profile is not constitutive. The Record must distinguish:

```text
not present
≠ not checked
≠ unknown
≠ excluded from scope
```

#### Shared Record mapping

The compact Trajectory view requires the Shared Transformation Record to retain:

- source object, origin typing, frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, and source basis;
- Path lineage and earlier result identity;
- present analytical cut and boundary rationale;
- operation status, including where no operation is yet claimed;
- expected praxeological difference;
- profile-specific carriers and Source–Result Dependency;
- current-condition allocation and rival explanation pressure;
- directionality and anti-teleology declaration;
- compression and canonical Loss fields;
- alternatives and competing constructions;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- Source, Claim, and Authority Ceilings;
- Stop, Non-Capture, re-entry, and no-erasure lineage;
- extension ownership for Trajectory-specific declarations.

The existing Record carriers are sufficient at WP3. No schema expansion is required merely because the compact view is useful.

#### Record completeness and substantive sufficiency

A Record can be syntactically complete while the Trajectory remains unwarranted. Machine-readable validation may establish that fields are present, correctly typed, and linked. It cannot establish that:

- the Path substrate is empirically warranted;
- sedimentation exceeds repetition;
- a carrier remains present-bearing;
- directionality is substantively non-teleological;
- one boundary is better supported;
- compression retains every constitutive element;
- rival constructions can be adjudicated;
- current conditions do not better explain the result;
- Path Dependence or a target function follows.

```text
record completeness
≠ historical-load sufficiency
≠ admissibility
≠ truth proof
```

#### C11-REC-01 — complete Record, unresolved sufficiency

A Trajectory Record is completed for a long-running coordination process. It contains:

- a warranted Path reference;
- declared temporal boundary and analytical cut;
- candidate Attractor and Binding carriers;
- a documented missed review;
- a present switching cost;
- compression and Loss declarations;
- one rival periodization;
- bounded claim scope.

However, the available sources do not show whether the switching cost is inherited from historical coordination or produced entirely by a recently imposed technical standard. The Record correctly marks current-condition allocation as underdetermined.

The Record passes structural completeness but the full historical-load claim remains unresolved.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The result concerns the bounded claim, not the mere field completion. No automatic Trajectory truth is inferred.

#### C11-NC-01 — rival source-responsible Records

Two complete Records describe the same later Path but disagree on whether an early informal practice and a later formal rule constitute one translated carrier or two distinct Trajectories. Each Record:

- declares its boundary and Path lineage;
- identifies its own Source–Result Dependency;
- preserves the rival construction;
- documents the early source gap;
- records compression and uncertainty;
- avoids target-function and Path-Dependence inference.

No available source can determine whether continuity or discontinuity is the more responsible construction. The difference materially changes Binding lineage and Attractor Sedimentation.

The result is:

```text
non_capture
```

The Records remain inspectable; neither receives validation by default.

#### Stop, Non-Capture, and re-entry

The Minimal Trajectory Record must preserve distinct governance outcomes:

```text
incomplete declaration
≠ failed Trajectory
≠ mandatory stop
≠ non_capture
```

- Missing required information may support `admissible_but_provisional`, `claim_reduction_required`, or `failed_transformation`, depending on whether responsible re-entry remains possible and whether a bounded claim survives.
- A known failed Trajectory used for Path Dependence, causality, target function, legitimacy, ranking, or authority requires `mandatory_stop`.
- Rival source-responsible constructions that remain materially undecidable may receive `non_capture`.
- Non-Capture does not convert incomplete sources into support and does not protect a weak claim from reduction.

Re-entry requires:

- explicit reference to the earlier Record and result;
- identification of new source, changed boundary, changed claim, or corrected compression;
- a new Source–Result test;
- preserved no-erasure lineage;
- no inherited operation, Output Class, target function, or authority.

#### Chapter-12 handoff

Chapter 11 supplies a developed Trajectory object. Chapter 12 receives that object for a separate property test:

```text
warranted Trajectory object
→ possible Path-Dependence test

warranted Trajectory object
≠ Path Dependence already established
```

The handoff must include:

- Path reference and boundary;
- sedimentation carriers;
- present historical-load claim;
- changed corridor dimensions;
- current-condition allocation;
- competing constructions;
- uncertainty, Loss, Stop, and Non-Capture status;
- claim ceiling.

Chapter 12 may confirm weak order dependence, strong Path Dependence, a reduced dependence claim, Failure, Stop, or Non-Capture. It may not retroactively repair an unsupported Trajectory.

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 11.10–11.14 canonically establish:

- Trajectory Boundary as a claim-sensitive declaration of beginning, prehistory, analytical cut, terminal status, segment lineage, and open continuation;
- periodization without naturalized or uniquely true boundaries;
- Trajectory Compression with preservation of load-bearing transitions, Non-Events, profile carriers, alternatives, reversals, repairs, current conditions, and canonical Loss;
- rejection of macro-label substitution and monotonic flattening;
- comparison and adjudication of compatible, complementary, nested, overlapping, incomparable, conflicting, underdetermined, and failed constructions without creating new Output Classes;
- Non-Capture where materially rival source-responsible constructions cannot be adjudicated;
- False Trajectory as a diagnostic for a failed or overclaimed construction, with bounded preservation of weaker findings;
- Mandatory Stop where a known failed Trajectory is used for stronger derivation;
- the Minimal Trajectory Record as a view inside the Shared Transformation Record;
- Record completeness without substantive truth inference;
- the explicit Chapter-12 handoff and no-retroactive-repair rule.

The eight assigned WP3 pressure cases are represented:

```text
C11-BOUND-01
C11-COMP-01
C11-COMPETE-01
C11-COMPETE-02
C11-FALSE-01
C11-REC-01
C11-STOP-01
C11-NC-01
```

The local result remains:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP3 defect. It preserves the remaining Chapter-11 WP4 duties:

- integrated redundancy and dependency audit;
- verification of all nineteen Chapter-11 case duties;
- complete Reference and bounded Formal-Model synchronization review;
- local Admissibility, Failure, Stop, Non-Capture, link, schema, fingerprint, package, and roundtrip audits;
- Chapter-11 Provisional Lock.

```text
WP3 completion
≠ Chapter 11 provisional lock
≠ Path-Dependence confirmation
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ target-function assignment
≠ unique periodization
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 11 — WP4:
Synchronization,
Integrated Audit,
and Provisional Lock
```

#### Chapter 11 completion boundary

Chapter 11 has now operationalized the Trajectory object without collapsing it into Path, Path Dependence, `COMPOSE`, a RETYPE target function, causal necessity, or historical verdict. The chapter receives a warranted Path from Chapter 10 and applies a stronger, conjunctive burden: earlier structure must remain source-traceably carried into the present analytical cut through cumulative change, persistent residue, changed praxis conditions, or changed continuation possibilities.

```text
warranted Path
≠ warranted Trajectory automatically

warranted Trajectory
≠ Path Dependence automatically
```

The Chapter 11 completion test is satisfied:

1. a Trajectory requires a warranted Path substrate and cannot be established by chronology, duration, repetition, persistence, institutional visibility, or narrative coherence alone;
2. Historical Sedimentation requires an identifiable carrier, a temporally supported cumulative relation, a present praxis effect, and Source–Result Dependency under declared Source and Claim Ceilings;
3. cumulative change may be non-linear and may include thresholds, erosion, redistribution, interruption, repair, reversal, dormancy, re-entry, or partial restoration without becoming a universal score;
4. persistent residue remains distinct from permanence, current recurrence, archival survival, remembrance, missing information, and unsupported retrospective significance;
5. directionality is dimension-specific and reconstructible without destiny, progress, decline, optimization, original plan, necessary endpoint, or determined future;
6. `Α + Θ`, `Ω + Θ`, `Ψ + Θ`, and `Λ + Θ` remain separate occurrence-level historical-load profiles and do not fuse operators, create a new primitive, or require every Trajectory to instantiate every profile;
7. Changed Action Corridors declare historically altered accessibility, visibility, authorization, affordability, coordination feasibility, temporal availability, reversibility, expectedness, evidentiary burden, or exposure without eliminating agency, predicting conduct, or recommending action;
8. Trajectory Boundary and periodization remain claim-sensitive, source-bounded, revisable, and open to competing constructions rather than naturalized as uniquely true historical seams;
9. Trajectory Compression preserves load-bearing transitions, Non-Events, profile carriers, alternatives, reversals, repairs, current-condition pressure, uncertainty, open continuation, and canonical Loss rather than substituting a macro-label for a traceable Path;
10. competing constructions are compared under independent Path and Trajectory burdens and may remain compatible, complementary, nested, overlapping, incomparable, conflicting, underdetermined, failed, or non-captured without forced synthesis;
11. False Trajectory remains a diagnostic of a failed or overclaimed construction, while warranted weaker findings—such as chronology, duration, transitions, Path segments, recurrence, local Asymmetry, discrete Binding, framed Non-Events, bounded residue, present constraint, or source uncertainty—remain preserved;
12. the Minimal Trajectory Record remains a compact view inside the Shared Transformation Record and cannot convert field presence, schema validity, or machine-readable completeness into historical-load sufficiency, substantive admissibility, or truth;
13. Chapter 12 receives a developed Trajectory object for a separate graded Path-Dependence test, while Chapter 15 retains complete `COMPOSE` mechanics and RETYPE retains contextual target-function assignment;
14. all nineteen prepared Chapter-11 pressure cases are represented in the canonical chapter without being misreported as completed `03_cases/*` artifacts, empirical evidence, or calibration data;
15. Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, and Non-Capture preserve lineage, weaker findings, Loss, uncertainty, rival constructions, and re-entry conditions without authority inheritance.

The integrated audit confirms that the chapter's four historical-load profiles are neither additive scores nor compensatory shortcuts. A rich Attractor profile cannot repair a failed Path substrate; accumulated Asymmetry cannot substitute for missing present effect; Binding history cannot establish consent, obligation, or enforceability; and repeated absence cannot become Residual Accumulation without a warranted expectation frame. Each claim retains its own carrier, temporal trace, present effect, counterpressure, source basis, and ceiling.

```text
profile richness
≠ Trajectory warrant by accumulation of labels

formal completeness
≠ substantive sufficiency
```

The anti-laundering boundary remains active. A failed Trajectory may be reopened only through a new testable claim that identifies new source material, a corrected boundary, a revised compression, or a reduced claim. A new frame, granularity, periodization, label, operation, or target function does not erase the earlier result and cannot inherit its missing support.

```text
new Trajectory construction
= new testable claim

later Path-Dependence success
≠ retroactive Trajectory repair

later PROJECT_AS success
≠ retroactive origin-object repair
```

The chapter-level result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known local Chapter-11 repair requirement. It preserves later pressure from Chapter 12 Path Dependence, Chapter 13 alternatives, Chapter 14 Non-Events, Chapter 15 `COMPOSE`, Chapter 16 PATH Boundary Conditions, Chapter 17 integrated PATH cases and audit, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, Appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, and Model Finalization.

```text
Chapter 11 provisional lock
≠ historical truth proof
≠ Path-Dependence confirmation
≠ COMPOSE authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ unique periodization
≠ causal or predictive authority
≠ higher authority
```

Reopen Chapter 11 only if later work demonstrates a concrete conflict involving the Path-to-Trajectory threshold, sedimentation carrier, Source–Result Dependency, present historical-load effect, profile separation, corridor declaration, boundary, compression, competing construction, False Trajectory routing, Minimal Record mapping, Chapter-12 input requirements, or the unchanged PMS Base. Mere preference for a different periodization, richer narrative, broader label, finer detail, or stronger conclusion is not a reopening ground.

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 12 — Preparation:
Path Dependence and Sedimentation
```

<a id="chapter-12-path-dependence-and-sedimentation"></a>

## 12. Path Dependence and Sedimentation

Chapter 11 established the Trajectory as a warranted Path whose earlier structure remains source-traceably carried into the present analytical cut through cumulative change, persistent residue, altered praxis conditions, or changed continuation possibilities. Chapter 12 asks a stronger and more discriminating question: **how much of the present reconstruction actually depends on the particular prior Path?**

A historical object may carry substantial sedimented load without every current feature being path-dependent in the strong sense. The earlier history may improve interpretation, explain one local credibility difference, or clarify a bounded cost while the present configuration remains largely reconstructible from current conditions. Conversely, two configurations that appear similar at the present cut may differ materially in cost, role, exposure, evidentiary burden, or continuation reachability because they arrived there through different Paths.

```text
historical contribution
≠ historical indispensability
```

Path Dependence therefore names neither a new historical object nor an automatic consequence of Trajectory formation. It is a **graded property claim** about the extent to which current meaning, cost, role, credibility, accessibility, reachability, or continuation possibilities would be materially misdescribed or underdescribed if the relevant prior Path were omitted, materially altered, or replaced by a source-bounded alternative.

```text
warranted Trajectory
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

Sections 12.1–12.3 establish:

- Path Dependence as a property rather than an object, operator, operation, level, or target function;
- the eligible Path and Trajectory inputs for a dependence test;
- bounded dependence dimensions and claim scope;
- weak order dependence as a real but limited historical effect;
- strong Path Dependence as historical indispensability under a current-state sufficiency challenge;
- approximately held-present comparison without false object identity or causal isolation;
- explicit rejection of duration, repetition, narrative richness, Trajectory status, or `Θ` alone as sufficient proof;
- claim reduction, Failure, and lineage preservation where the stronger property is not warranted.

Sections 12.4–12.7 later examine the dependence-bearing occurrence profiles `Α + Θ`, `Ω + Θ`, `Ψ + Θ`, and `Λ + Θ`. Sections 12.8–12.12 later address recontextualization, modifiers without reset, non-determinism, the complete local Path-Dependence test, Failure, Record mapping, Stop, and Non-Capture. Chapter 13 owns the detailed taxonomy of branches and unavailable alternatives. Chapter 15 retains the complete `COMPOSE` procedure. Chapter 24 later owns `DECOMPOSE` analysis of dependence-bearing occurrences or composites. Chapter 46 retains the general Counterfactual-Sensitivity architecture.

<a id="12-1-path-dependence-as-a-property"></a>

### 12.1 Path Dependence as a Property

**Path Dependence** is the graded property by which a present configuration or bounded continuation field depends on its prior Path such that omission or material alteration of the relevant history changes the warranted reconstruction.

```text
Trajectory
=
historically sedimented temporal object

Path Dependence
=
property of historical indispensability
```

The property does not exist independently of a reference object. It qualifies a Path, a Trajectory, or a bounded segment of one with respect to a declared present cut and a declared dependence dimension. It does not create another object beside Path and Trajectory.

#### Property, object, and occurrence

A Trajectory is an analytical object whose identity is carried by the warranted Path, historical carriers, boundary, compression, and present-bearing load established in Chapter 11. Path Dependence asks how indispensable that Path is to one current feature or continuation dimension of the object.

```text
Trajectory object
≠ Path-Dependence property
```

The distinction prevents several category collapses:

- a Trajectory label cannot serve as automatic strong-dependence proof;
- Path Dependence cannot be `DECOMPOSE`d as though it were a PMS operator type;
- Path Dependence is not a fourth STRATA operation;
- Path Dependence does not define a relative level;
- Path Dependence does not assign a target function;
- a dependence finding does not create application authority.

The abstract PMS operator `Θ` supplies temporality, sequence, and trajectory capacity within the Base grammar. It does not instantiate the Path-Dependence property by itself. Likewise, the presence of `Α`, `Ω`, `Ψ`, or `Λ` symbols does not establish a dependence-bearing occurrence relation without a source-supported historical carrier and present effect.

#### Eligible source objects

A local dependence test may begin from:

1. a warranted Path where a bounded order effect is tested without claiming a Trajectory-wide dependency;
2. a warranted Trajectory where weak or strong dependence is tested at the present analytical cut;
3. a bounded segment of a warranted Trajectory where dependence is local rather than whole-object-wide;
4. competing warranted Trajectory constructions tested separately under their own boundaries, source bases, and claim ceilings.

The following are not sufficient strong-dependence inputs:

- a chronology without a warranted Path;
- an isolated endpoint;
- duration alone;
- repeated events without sedimented present load;
- a macro-label that replaces the historical trace;
- a failed or non-captured Trajectory treated as settled;
- a formal Record whose substantive carrier is unsupported;
- an operator formula used as occurrence evidence.

```text
eligible input
≠ dependence conclusion
```

The input remains independently revisable. If a later audit invalidates the Path or Trajectory substrate, the dependence claim loses its load-bearing input. A later successful dependence test cannot retroactively repair a failed Path or Trajectory.

#### Required property declaration

A bounded property claim must declare at least:

```yaml
path_dependence_claim:
  reference_object:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  present_analytical_cut:
  tested_dimension:
  relevant_prior_path_segment:
  historical_carrier:
  current_state_baseline:
  current_condition_contribution:
  omission_or_comparison_pressure:
  source_basis:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
  claim_ceiling:
```

This is a conceptual view inside the Shared Transformation Record, not a second schema. Field presence does not establish substantive passage.

#### Tested dimensions

Path Dependence must be dimension-specific. The property may concern one or more of the following:

- **meaning:** how a present act, statement, rule, or role is interpreted;
- **credibility:** how much evidentiary or practical weight a present signal carries;
- **cost or friction:** effort, delay, uncertainty, negotiation, switching, repair, or exit burden;
- **role allocation:** who is expected, authorized, exposed, or burdened in the present configuration;
- **access or authorization:** which continuations are visible, permitted, affordable, or procedurally available;
- **expectation or default continuation:** what is treated as ordinary, exceptional, overdue, or unlikely;
- **reversibility:** what can be changed without reopening prior commitments, records, losses, or exclusions;
- **evidentiary burden:** what must be shown because of the prior Path;
- **continuation reachability:** which bounded next configurations remain practically reachable and at what cost.

```text
Path Dependence in one dimension
≠ whole-object dependence in every dimension
```

A configuration may be strongly path-dependent in exit cost but only weakly order-dependent in interpretation. It may be path-dependent in evidentiary burden while current role allocation is explained almost entirely by present rules. The claim must not spread from one supported dimension to the whole object.

#### Present analytical cut

The property is assessed at a declared present analytical cut. “Present” here means the current cut of the reconstruction, not necessarily the calendar present. The cut may be historical, retrospective, or intermediate, provided its sources, boundary, and claim scope are explicit.

The analyst must identify:

- what configuration is being reconstructed at the cut;
- which features are treated as current conditions;
- which historical traces remain active at that cut;
- which continuation field is bounded for analysis;
- which later information is excluded to avoid retrospective leakage.

```text
present analytical cut
≠ completed history
```

A later outcome must not be used to make an earlier Path appear more dependent than it was at the tested cut.

#### Graded local findings

Chapter 12 distinguishes at least three local property findings:

1. **no material historical dependence at the tested claim level** — detailed history adds no warranted discriminative difference to the current reconstruction;
2. **weak order dependence** — earlier order changes a bounded local interpretation or cost while the present remains largely reconstructible from current conditions;
3. **strong Path Dependence** — the prior Path is materially indispensable to adequate reconstruction of the tested current dimension or continuation field.

These are not new canonical Output Classes. They map to the fixed vocabulary according to the claim and evidentiary status.

Possible mappings include:

- bounded supported property claim → `admissible_with_bounded_claim`;
- supported but source-limited strong candidate → `admissible_but_provisional`;
- strong claim reduced to weak order dependence → `claim_reduction_required`;
- no additional historical purchase and no active stronger claim → `resolution_neutral`;
- rival source-responsible explanations that remain undecidable → `non_capture`;
- known failed claim used for prediction, projection, diagnosis, or authority → `mandatory_stop`.

```text
local dependence strength
≠ Output Class identity
```

#### Property scope and anti-inheritance

A dependence result is bounded by:

- reference object;
- present cut;
- tested dimension;
- Path segment;
- frame and granularity;
- source basis;
- current-condition allocation;
- uncertainty and Loss;
- claim and authority ceiling.

A result does not transfer automatically across:

- other present cuts;
- other dimensions;
- other Path segments;
- other periodizations;
- other frames or granularities;
- other Trajectory constructions;
- later `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS` occurrences;
- other domains or persons.

```text
supported local Path Dependence
≠ universal historical dependence
≠ authority inheritance
```

#### C12-PROP-01 — one bounded dependence dimension within a warranted Trajectory

A service network has a warranted Trajectory in which access approvals, repeated review delays, and documentation practices have changed over several cycles. At the present cut:

- the current role titles are determined by a recently issued organizational chart;
- current service prices are determined by a new external tariff;
- access to archived records still depends on credentials and classifications accumulated through the earlier Path;
- two current roles with the same formal title have different practical evidentiary access because one role inherits the earlier archive lineage and the other does not.

The property test is limited to **evidentiary access**. It does not claim that the entire current organization is strongly path-dependent.

The bounded finding is:

```text
current role title
→ largely current-state explained

current tariff
→ current-state explained

archive access and evidentiary burden
→ materially history-dependent candidate
```

The Trajectory remains the historical object. Path Dependence qualifies one current dimension. No new object, operator, operation, level, target function, or authority follows.

A suitable local output is `admissible_with_bounded_claim` if the archive carrier and source trace are supported. If the archive lineage is only partially documented, the result may remain `admissible_but_provisional` or `partially_admissible` without expanding to the whole object.

<a id="12-2-weak-order-dependence"></a>

### 12.2 Weak Order Dependence

**Weak order dependence** is present where the order of prior configurations or transitions changes a bounded current interpretation, credibility relation, local cost, or procedural meaning, while the present configuration remains largely reconstructible from current conditions without detailed Path history.

```text
history changes a local reconstruction
+
current conditions remain largely sufficient
→ weak order dependence
```

Weak order dependence is not analytically trivial. A local historical difference may be indispensable to interpreting one statement, deciding whether a delay is ordinary or exceptional, understanding why a procedure carries low trust, or explaining a limited proof burden. The “weak” qualifier concerns the scope and load of the property, not its moral, practical, or historical importance.

#### Minimum burden

A weak-order claim requires:

1. a warranted temporal order relation;
2. an identifiable local feature whose interpretation changes when order is omitted or reversed;
3. a source-supported connection between that order and the local present effect;
4. substantial reconstructibility of the broader current configuration from current conditions;
5. limited or bounded sedimented load;
6. no unsupported extension to whole-object cost, role, accessibility, or reachability;
7. explicit source, claim, Loss, and uncertainty boundaries.

```text
order matters locally
≠ detailed Path is globally indispensable
```

#### Local meaning and credibility

The same present statement or action may carry different meaning because of its position in a prior sequence. Examples include:

- an assurance made after an earlier unfulfilled assurance carrying lower current credibility;
- an identical procedural delay occurring before rather than after a declared deadline;
- a request following a documented repair attempt carrying different meaning than the same request made before any repair;
- a present exception interpreted differently because it follows a prior temporary exemption rather than an initial rule.

The claim must identify the precise local difference. It must not infer a whole-object Path-Dependence property merely because a history-sensitive interpretation exists.

#### Current-state reconstructibility

Weak order dependence preserves a strong rival explanation from current conditions. The broader current configuration may remain adequately reconstructible from:

- current rules;
- current resource distribution;
- current role assignments;
- current prices or technical constraints;
- currently available records;
- present authorization structures;
- current environmental conditions.

The historical order adds a bounded difference rather than carrying the broader object.

```text
current-state reconstructibility
≠ history is irrelevant
```

The distinction avoids two opposite errors:

- **historical inflation:** treating any order-sensitive meaning as strong Path Dependence;
- **presentist erasure:** treating a largely current-state-explainable configuration as wholly independent of history.

#### Limited sedimented load

Weak order dependence may include residue or a small present cost, but the load remains bounded. The claim may show:

- one additional review step;
- a limited credibility discount;
- a local expectation shift;
- a short procedural delay;
- one role-specific clarification burden;
- a reversible interpretive difference.

It does not support a strong claim where:

- exit costs remain materially unchanged;
- continuation reachability is not altered;
- role allocation is still determined by current conditions;
- the historical carrier is no longer active beyond local interpretation;
- omission of the detailed Path does not materially underdescribe the broader present.

#### Weak dependence and duration

A long history may support only weak order dependence. Conversely, a short sequence may create a strong local effect. Duration therefore does not determine the dependence grade.

```text
long history
≠ strong dependence

short history
≠ weak dependence automatically
```

The relevant issue is the present discriminative load carried by the specific order, not the amount of elapsed time.

#### Counterpressure and reduction

Every weak-order claim should face at least two challenges:

1. **order-neutrality challenge:** Would the local reconstruction remain materially the same if the relevant earlier events occurred in another order or were omitted?
2. **current-condition challenge:** Is the local difference already explained by current information, rules, or material conditions?

If neither challenge produces a warranted difference, the result may be `resolution_neutral`. If a strong claim was initially made but only a local order effect survives, the correct disposition is `claim_reduction_required` rather than silent relabeling.

#### C12-WEAK-01 — local credibility change without whole-configuration dependence

A committee issues the same written assurance at two different moments:

- in Path A, the assurance follows a prior assurance that was fulfilled on schedule;
- in Path B, the assurance follows a prior assurance whose promised review did not occur;
- at the present cut, both committees operate under the same current charter, budget, membership rules, deadline, and enforcement procedure;
- the new assurance has lower local credibility in Path B because the earlier sequence changes how the identical text is reasonably interpreted;
- current authorization, cost, and continuation reachability remain determined by the same present conditions in both cases.

The warranted local result is weak order dependence in the dimension of **credibility**.

```text
same present text
+ different preceding sequence
→ different bounded credibility

same current rules, cost, and reachability
→ no strong whole-configuration dependence
```

The claim does not establish motive, deception, guilt, deterministic future non-performance, or a general institutional type. A suitable output is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. If the original claim asserted strong Path Dependence for the whole committee configuration, it must be reduced.

<a id="12-3-strong-path-dependence"></a>

### 12.3 Strong Path Dependence

**Strong Path Dependence** is present where the relevant prior Path and its sedimented carriers are materially indispensable to adequate reconstruction of a declared current dimension or bounded continuation field.

```text
without the relevant prior Path,
the present would be materially misdescribed
or underdescribed
→ strong Path-Dependence candidate
```

The property is “strong” because the historical route does more than improve narrative richness or local interpretation. It changes the warranted account of current cost, role, credibility, access, exposure, reversibility, evidentiary burden, or continuation reachability in a way that current conditions alone do not adequately explain.

#### Conjunctive threshold

A strong candidate requires all of the following:

1. a warranted Path or Trajectory input;
2. a declared present analytical cut;
3. a declared dependence dimension;
4. a relevant prior Path segment;
5. a traceable historical carrier;
6. sedimented relation across time;
7. a present effect with praxeological purchase;
8. a current-state baseline;
9. an explicit current-condition sufficiency challenge;
10. Source–Result Dependency under relevant source variation;
11. a source-bounded omission, actual comparator, or alternative-history pressure test;
12. preserved contingency and no deterministic or teleological conclusion;
13. canonical Loss, uncertainty, claim ceiling, Stop, and Non-Capture controls.

```text
strong Path Dependence
=
conjunctive historical-indispensability claim

not
=
accumulated suggestive indicators
```

No item compensates for another. Rich historical detail does not compensate for missing present effect. Severe present cost does not compensate for absent carrier lineage. A complete Record does not compensate for unsupported comparison. Several operator profiles do not add into a universal score.

#### Historical contribution versus historical indispensability

A Trajectory already establishes historical contribution: earlier structures remain present-bearing through sedimentation, residue, cumulative change, or changed corridors. Strong Path Dependence adds a narrower and heavier burden:

```text
historical contribution
→ history participates in the present reconstruction

historical indispensability
→ adequate reconstruction fails materially without it
```

The distinction is tested by asking whether current rules, resources, roles, technologies, prices, institutions, or external conditions can adequately reconstruct the relevant present effect without detailed Path history.

If current conditions explain the feature sufficiently, the historical account may remain:

- a warranted Trajectory;
- a weak order-dependence claim;
- a contextual historical explanation;
- a source record of development;
- a bounded residue claim.

It does not remain a strong Path-Dependence claim merely because the history is true or important.

#### Current-state sufficiency challenge

Every strong candidate must face the rival hypothesis:

```text
The tested present feature may be adequately reconstructed
from current conditions without the detailed prior Path.
```

The current-state baseline should identify:

- current formal rules;
- current material and technical constraints;
- current resources and prices;
- current role and access assignments;
- current information and records;
- current external events or environmental conditions;
- current enforcement, repair, or exit procedures;
- current continuation alternatives.

The analyst must then ask:

- Which part of the present effect remains unexplained or materially underdescribed?
- Does the historical carrier still operate, or is it only archival?
- Would removing the earlier Path alter the current cost, role, credibility, accessibility, or reachability claim?
- Are current and historical contributions distinguishable with available sources?
- Does the result change under relevant source variation?

The strong claim fails or reduces where current conditions remain sufficient, the carrier is no longer active, or the supposed historical difference does not change the warranted reconstruction.

#### Approximately held-present comparison

The contract-required pressure test holds a declared set of present features approximately constant while varying prior Path history. This is not a claim of identical objects or experimental causal isolation.

The comparison must declare:

- which present features are held approximately similar;
- which present differences remain and are not suppressed;
- which prior transition, sequence, or carrier differs;
- whether the cases are actual, reconstructed, or source-bounded counterfactual;
- which current dependence dimension changes;
- how sources support comparability;
- what Loss and uncertainty remain.

```text
approximately similar present features
≠ identical present objects
≠ controlled experiment
≠ causal proof
```

The comparison is useful because surface similarity can conceal different historical load. Two current agreements may have the same text but different reopening costs. Two roles may have the same title but different access lineage. Two institutions may have the same rule but different evidentiary burdens because one rule emerged through repeated exceptions and the other did not.

#### Same endpoint, different praxis structure

Chapter 10 established that similar endpoints do not imply identical Paths. Chapter 12 adds a separate question: do those different Paths materially change the current praxis structure?

A strong dependence candidate may be supported where approximately similar endpoints differ in:

- sunk coordination or switching cost;
- role credibility;
- practical exit conditions;
- evidentiary access;
- default continuation;
- expectation burden;
- availability of repair;
- reachability of alternatives.

```text
same endpoint label
≠ same current praxis structure
```

But different histories alone do not prove different present structures. The present effect must be source-supported and dimension-specific.

#### Source-bounded omission pressure

At WP1, the minimum omission question is:

```text
What materially disappears from the warranted present reconstruction
if the relevant prior Path segment is omitted?
```

A strong candidate should identify a non-trivial loss such as:

- current exit cost becomes inexplicable;
- role credibility is misassigned;
- access differences appear arbitrary;
- evidentiary burden is underdescribed;
- a continuation appears equally reachable when it is not;
- a present default appears current-rule-based although it is historically stabilized.

The full Historical-Omission and source-bounded Alternative-History test architecture is completed in Section 12.11. WP1 establishes the burden without pre-empting Chapter 13 branch taxonomy or Chapter 46 general counterfactual theory.

#### `Θ` alone is insufficient

Temporality is necessary for any Path-Dependence claim but never sufficient by itself.

```text
Θ
→ sequence and temporal extension capacity

Θ alone
≠ sedimented carrier
≠ current effect
≠ historical indispensability
```

The following do not establish strong Path Dependence:

- long duration;
- repeated occurrence;
- institutional age;
- continuity of label;
- archival density;
- narrative coherence;
- chronological detail;
- persistence without changed present discrimination;
- a statement that “history matters” without a tested dimension.

Dependence-bearing operator profiles are examined in Sections 12.4–12.7, but no profile is independently sufficient and no profile compensates for a failed current-state challenge.

#### Strong dependence without necessity

Strong Path Dependence does not mean:

- the prior Path was the only possible cause;
- the current state could not have arisen by another route;
- all alternatives are now impossible;
- future continuation is determined;
- the object has an essence fixed by history;
- the property applies to every dimension;
- the finding authorizes intervention, sanction, diagnosis, or prediction.

```text
historical indispensability for reconstruction
≠ metaphysical necessity
≠ deterministic causation
```

Section 12.10 later develops this non-determinism boundary in full. WP1 already prohibits any use of the strong label as destiny or forecast.

#### Failure, reduction, and preservation

A strong claim may fail while weaker findings remain warranted. Possible preserved findings include:

- chronology;
- sequence;
- transitions;
- warranted Path;
- warranted Trajectory;
- Historical Sedimentation;
- one local order-sensitive interpretation;
- bounded residue;
- current constraint;
- source uncertainty.

Typical dispositions are:

- strong claim unsupported, weak order effect supported → `claim_reduction_required`;
- no additional historical discrimination at the tested level → `resolution_neutral`;
- materially rival source-responsible explanations remain undecidable → later `non_capture`;
- input Path or Trajectory invalid → `failed_transformation` for the strong property claim;
- known failed claim used for stronger derivation or authority → `mandatory_stop`.

```text
failed strong dependence
≠ erased history
≠ failed Path automatically
≠ failed Trajectory automatically
```

A revised boundary, new source, corrected carrier, or reduced claim requires a new testable claim with explicit lineage. A new frame, granularity, periodization, label, operation, or target function cannot silently repair the failed result.

#### C12-STRONG-01 — approximately similar present, different prior Paths, different exit cost

Two regional service units have approximately the same present surface configuration:

- the same current contract template;
- the same formal role titles;
- the same nominal exit clause;
- the same current tariff;
- the same current reporting deadline.

Their prior Paths differ materially.

**Path A**

- the unit adopted the current template directly;
- no earlier bespoke integration was required;
- records remain portable;
- replacement providers can assume the current contract with ordinary transition work.

**Path B**

- the unit reached the same template after several temporary integrations;
- data were migrated into a proprietary archive;
- staff roles specialized around the temporary arrangement;
- repeated review delays allowed local workarounds to become standard;
- the current formal exit clause remains identical, but practical exit requires archive conversion, replacement training, and reconstruction of undocumented dependencies.

At the present cut, current rules explain the formal clause and tariff but do not adequately explain the difference in practical exit cost. The historical archive, specialization, and workaround carriers remain active.

```text
approximately same current contract surface
+ different warranted Paths
+ different source-traceable practical exit burden
→ bounded strong Path-Dependence candidate in exit cost
```

The claim remains dimension-specific. It does not establish that every current feature of Path B is strongly path-dependent, that exit is impossible, that the earlier Path was illegitimate, or that future continuation is determined. A suitable local result is `admissible_but_provisional` until the operator-profile and full test duties of WP2–WP3 are completed.

#### C12-OBJ-01 — Trajectory label used as automatic strong-dependence proof

An analysis establishes a warranted Trajectory with repeated review delays, persistent residue, and a changed documentation corridor. It then states:

```text
This is a Trajectory;
therefore the present organization is strongly path-dependent.
```

The inference fails. The analysis does not identify:

- a tested dependence dimension;
- a current-state baseline;
- a current-condition sufficiency challenge;
- an omission or source-bounded comparison;
- the feature that becomes materially misdescribed without the Path.

The Trajectory remains warranted. The strong Path-Dependence claim is rejected.

```text
warranted Trajectory preserved
+
automatic dependence inference rejected
→ claim_reduction_required or no strong claim admitted
```

The failed property inference does not invalidate Chapter 11. It prevents the Trajectory label from functioning as inherited proof.

#### C12-THETA-01 — long duration and repetition without additional present discrimination

A procedure has been repeated annually for twenty years. The present configuration is fully specified by:

- the current statutory rule;
- the current software implementation;
- the current role matrix;
- the current tariff;
- the current appeal process.

Historical records show stable repetition, but:

- removing the earlier sequence does not change the warranted account of current cost, role, access, credibility, or reachability;
- no active carrier links earlier repetitions to a distinct present burden;
- current conditions independently reproduce the procedure;
- the history adds context and institutional age but no further discriminative load at the tested claim level.

```text
long duration
+ repetition
+ current-state sufficiency
+ no additional historical discrimination
→ no strong Path Dependence
```

If the analysis made only a strong dependence claim, the result is `claim_reduction_required` or `resolution_neutral`, depending on whether any bounded order effect remains. The historical record is not erased. `Θ` remains relevant to the chronology, but temporality alone does not establish the property.

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 12.1–12.3 now canonically establish:

- Path Dependence as a graded property rather than an object, primitive, operation, level, target function, score, or authority layer;
- the Trajectory/object versus dependence/property distinction;
- eligible Path, Trajectory, segment, and competing-construction inputs without inherited conclusions;
- dimension-specific claims concerning meaning, credibility, cost, role, exposure, access, expectation, reversibility, evidentiary burden, or continuation reachability;
- the present analytical cut and current-state baseline;
- the three local findings of no material historical dependence, weak order dependence, and strong Path Dependence without creating new Output Classes;
- weak order dependence as a bounded historical effect under substantial current-state reconstructibility;
- strong Path Dependence as a conjunctive historical-indispensability burden;
- the distinction between historical contribution and historical indispensability;
- the current-state sufficiency challenge;
- approximately held-present comparison without object identity, experimental isolation, or causal proof;
- Source–Result and omission pressure without pre-empting the full Chapter-12 test, Chapter-13 branch taxonomy, or Chapter-46 counterfactual architecture;
- explicit insufficiency of `Θ`, duration, repetition, institutional age, narrative coherence, archival density, or Trajectory status alone;
- failure and reduction with preservation of warranted Path, Trajectory, sedimentation, residue, or local order findings;
- all five assigned WP1 pressure cases.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP1 defect. It preserves the remaining Chapter-12 burdens:

- dependence-bearing `Α + Θ`, `Ω + Θ`, `Ψ + Θ`, and `Λ + Θ` profiles;
- profile interaction and non-compensation;
- `Φ` under Path Dependence;
- `Χ`, `Σ`, and later `Ψ` as modifiers without reset;
- dependence without determinism;
- the complete Historical-Omission and source-bounded Alternative-History tests;
- failure, Record, Stop, Non-Capture, and re-entry architecture;
- integrated Chapter-12 audit and Provisional Lock.

```text
WP1 completion
≠ Chapter 12 provisional lock
≠ full operator-profile support
≠ completed Path-Dependence test
≠ causal necessity
≠ prediction
≠ COMPOSE authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 12 — WP2:
Dependence-Bearing Operator Profiles
```

<a id="12-4-a-theta-attractor-dependence"></a>

### 12.4 `Α + Θ` — Attractor Dependence

Chapter 11 established **Attractor Sedimentation** where repeated configurations leave traceable carriers that change present local friction, expectation, visibility, coordination, or continuation accessibility. Chapter 12 applies a stronger burden. **Attractor Dependence** is present only where the prior recurrence history is materially indispensable to an adequate reconstruction of a declared current dimension.

```text
Attractor Sedimentation
≠ Attractor Dependence automatically
```

```text
Α occurrence
+ temporal retention under Θ
+ present default or friction effect
+ failed current-only sufficiency
→ bounded Attractor-Dependence candidate
```

This remains an occurrence-level relation. It does not fuse `Α` and `Θ`, create a new operator, define an object class, or establish strong Path Dependence for the entire Trajectory.

#### Dependence-bearing load

A sedimented Attractor becomes dependence-bearing where the present practical field cannot be adequately described without the historical route through which one continuation became easier, more familiar, more visible, or more readily coordinated than alternatives.

Possible dependence-bearing carriers include:

- templates, interfaces, scripts, or routing shortcuts retained from repeated use;
- learned sequencing that reduces the need for renewed negotiation;
