- precedents that make one continuation easier to justify;
- infrastructure built around one repeated route;
- role familiarity that lowers initiation or coordination cost;
- repeated allocation of attention, staff, or resources to one continuation;
- non-use of alternatives that leaves them formally available but practically undeveloped;
- records or classifications that privilege the historically repeated route.

The claim must show more than current recurrence. It must identify how the prior Path generated, retained, or translated the carrier and what material present difference follows from that lineage.

```text
current default
≠ historically dependent default automatically
```

A current rule, technology, price, resource distribution, or explicit instruction may fully explain why one continuation is now preferred. In that case the Attractor may be current-state produced even if similar behavior occurred historically.

#### Current-state sufficiency challenge

The strongest current-only rival must be stated explicitly. Relevant current conditions may include:

- a presently mandatory workflow;
- current incentives or penalties;
- present resource scarcity;
- current interface design;
- a current role mandate;
- a recently adopted standard;
- present demand concentration;
- current technical compatibility.

Attractor Dependence is reduced or fails where these conditions adequately explain the tested present friction, visibility, expectation, or accessibility without detailed Path history.

```text
historical repetition
+ current rule fully explains present default
= Attractor Sedimentation may remain historical context,
  but strong Attractor Dependence is not established
```

The current-state challenge must not erase history by assumption. If the current rule itself arose through, incorporates, or depends on the prior carrier, that lineage must be tested rather than treated as exogenous.

#### Omission and variation pressure

A bounded Attractor-Dependence claim should ask:

1. What present friction, visibility, default expectation, or continuation accessibility becomes materially misdescribed if the repeated prior Path is omitted?
2. Would a relevant source-supported change in recurrence, retention, interruption, or alternative practice alter the present claim?
3. Does the carrier remain active, or has a current condition replaced it?
4. Are alternative continuations unavailable, merely less familiar, or only less visible?
5. Is the claim limited to one route, role, segment, or continuation dimension?

An omission that only removes narrative richness is insufficient. A supported variation must alter a warranted present reconstruction.

#### Dependence is not inevitability

A history-dependent default may remain interruptible. Attractor Dependence may mean that an alternative requires additional coordination, retraining, explanation, or infrastructure. It does not mean that:

- the repeated route is rationally superior;
- the route is legitimate or desirable;
- alternatives are impossible;
- the current default will persist;
- one future continuation is determined;
- deviation should be sanctioned;
- the Trajectory should be projected as an attractor-function.

```text
historically lowered friction
≠ necessary continuation
≠ normative priority
```

#### Profile-specific claim view

A bounded view inside the Shared Transformation Record may declare:

```yaml
attractor_dependence:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  present_analytical_cut:
  tested_dimension:
  repeated_prior_configurations:
  historical_carrier:
  current_default_or_friction:
  current_state_rival:
  omission_result:
  source_bounded_variation:
  alternatives_affected:
  repair_interruption_or_replacement:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
```

This declaration does not add a schema field or prove passage. The governing Record remains Chapter 7.

#### C12-ATTR-01 — same current policy, different historically produced deviation friction

Two service units operate under the same current approval policy, use the same formal request form, and permit the same three review routes.

**Unit A** reached the current arrangement after several years of repeatedly routing exceptions through one reviewer:

- a reusable template was built around that route;
- staff learned one familiar sequence;
- archived precedents are searchable by that reviewer's classifications;
- alternative reviewers receive few cases and maintain no comparable examples;
- the formal alternatives remain available but require manual explanation and additional coordination.

**Unit B** adopted the same current policy recently:

- no route has yet accumulated reusable precedent;
- staff training covers all three routes equally;
- archives use a route-neutral classification;
- switching among reviewers carries approximately equal current effort.

At the present cut, policy text alone does not explain why deviation from the familiar route is materially more costly in Unit A. The retained template, archive lineage, and learned sequence carry the earlier repetition into current coordination.

```text
same current policy surface
+ different recurrence lineage
+ different source-traceable deviation friction
→ bounded Attractor-Dependence candidate
```

The claim is limited to coordination friction and route accessibility. It does not establish that Unit A cannot change, that its dominant route is legitimate, or that every feature of the unit is strongly Path Dependent. Pending WP3's complete Historical-Omission and Alternative-History tests, a suitable local result is `admissible_but_provisional`.

<a id="12-5-o-theta-asymmetry-dependence"></a>

### 12.5 `Ω + Θ` — Asymmetry Dependence

Chapter 11 established **Asymmetry Accumulation** where differential cost, access, exposure, capacity, information, responsibility burden, or exit condition is retained, intensified, redistributed, or hardened across a Path. **Asymmetry Dependence** applies the stronger question: would the declared present asymmetry be materially misdescribed if its prior distribution history were omitted?

```text
Asymmetry Accumulation
≠ Asymmetry Dependence automatically
```

```text
Ω occurrence
+ distribution trace under Θ
+ present differential burden
+ historical indispensability
→ bounded Asymmetry-Dependence candidate
```

The relation remains structural and occurrence-bound. It is not a person ranking, moral hierarchy, legitimacy decision, or automatic responsibility verdict.

#### Accumulated versus current-only Asymmetry

A present difference may be fully explained by current conditions. Examples include:

- a newly assigned role with explicit current authority;
- a present resource imbalance created by an external price change;
- a current legal or technical access rule;
- a recent capacity difference unrelated to the prior Path;
- a current exposure condition produced by present location or infrastructure.

Such conditions may instantiate `Ω` without strong Path Dependence.

A dependence-bearing Asymmetry requires a traceable earlier distribution that still structures the present dimension. Possible carriers include:

- accumulated specialized knowledge or informational exclusion;
- historically unequal access to records, networks, tools, or resources;
- repeated transfer of coordination or repair burden to one role;
- retained exposure produced by earlier placement or sequencing;
- sunk adaptation costs distributed unevenly across roles;
- role hardening through repeated initiation, approval, or dependency;
- asymmetric documentation or proof burdens;
- differential exit costs created by earlier investments or exclusions.

```text
present inequality
≠ accumulated historical Asymmetry automatically
```

The claim must identify both the temporal distribution and the present-bearing carrier.

#### Distribution trace

A source-responsible distribution trace should distinguish:

1. who or what occupied the relevant positions at each tested segment;
2. which cost, access, exposure, information, capacity, or responsibility dimension differed;
3. whether the difference persisted, intensified, transferred, or was repaired;
4. which carrier retained the distribution into the present cut;
5. which current conditions independently reproduce or alter it;
6. whether a different prior distribution would materially change the present claim.

The trace need not assign intention or blame. Distributed Asymmetry may arise through repeated local arrangements, procedural defaults, material constraints, or Non-Events without one controlling actor.

#### Present-effect and current-state challenge

Asymmetry Dependence requires a present differential effect with praxeological purchase, such as:

- unequal practical exit cost despite equal formal exit clauses;
- unequal evidentiary access despite equal formal titles;
- unequal exposure to delay or failure;
- unequal coordination burden despite symmetric task descriptions;
- unequal ability to use alternatives;
- role authority that depends on accumulated archive, precedent, or network access;
- current repair obligations that follow an earlier burden distribution.

The current-state rival must test whether present rules, resources, skills, prices, or formal assignments fully explain the difference. If they do, the claim may remain a current Asymmetry finding without established historical dependence.

```text
strong present differential
+ no source-supported historical carrier
= current Asymmetry,
  not established Asymmetry Dependence
```

Severity does not compensate for missing lineage.

#### Dignity and authority boundary

A structural Asymmetry-Dependence claim must not become:

- a statement about the worth or maturity of persons;
- a diagnosis of incapacity;
- proof of guilt or exploitation;
- a legal or political legitimacy finding;
- an automatic duty allocation;
- a sanction or intervention authorization;
- an irreversible label.

The claim may show that current burdens, access, or exit conditions are history-dependent. Any normative, legal, organizational, or clinical application requires independent authority.

#### Profile-specific claim view

```yaml
asymmetry_dependence:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  present_analytical_cut:
  tested_dimension:
  prior_distribution:
  historical_carrier:
  present_differential_effect:
  current_state_rival:
  omission_result:
  source_bounded_redistribution_variation:
  repair_transfer_or_persistence:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
```

Field completeness remains distinct from substantive warrant.

#### C12-ASYM-01 — equal formal roles, unequal practical exposure from prior burden distribution

Two maintenance teams now have the same formal role matrix, the same staffing level, the same current budget, and the same contractual response time.

Their Paths differ.

**Team A** historically rotated emergency coverage and documented incidents in a shared archive. Knowledge, interruption cost, and after-hours exposure were distributed across the group.

**Team B** repeatedly routed emergencies through one specialist role:

- incident knowledge accumulated in that role's private notes;
- other roles received little exposure or training;
- unresolved cases were repeatedly returned to the same specialist;
- no handover protocol was completed;
- current staffing parity was introduced only after this history.

At the present cut, the formal matrix is symmetric. Practical exposure is not:

- one role remains the only credible responder for several legacy systems;
- taking leave requires undocumented knowledge transfer;
- failure risk and interruption burden remain concentrated;
- other roles possess the same formal mandate but not the accumulated access or familiarity.

```text
same current formal role matrix
+ different prior burden distribution
+ retained knowledge and archive carrier
+ unequal current exposure and exit cost
→ bounded Asymmetry-Dependence candidate
```

The claim does not rank the specialist or infer motive, virtue, exploitation, or legal responsibility. It concerns current exposure and practical substitutability. A suitable local result is `admissible_but_provisional` if the distribution trace is supported. If current technical certification alone fully explains the specialization, the historical claim must reduce accordingly.

<a id="12-6-ps-theta-binding-dependence"></a>

### 12.6 `Ψ + Θ` — Binding Dependence

Chapter 11 established **Binding Accumulation** where commitments, reliance, institutionalization, coordination investments, expectations, or breach costs remain historically carried into the present. **Binding Dependence** is present where those prior binding occurrences are materially indispensable to reconstructing a declared current obligation structure, reopening cost, reliance relation, coordination feasibility, or exit condition.

```text
Binding Accumulation
≠ Binding Dependence automatically
```

```text
Ψ occurrence
+ retention or transfer under Θ
+ present commitment or reopening effect
+ failed current-only sufficiency
→ bounded Binding-Dependence candidate
```

`Ψ` remains the PMS operator type. Chapter 12 tests concrete earlier Binding occurrences and their temporal carriers. It does not decompose the operator type, infer inner intention, or convert structural commitment into moral duty.

#### Concrete binding occurrences

Eligible historical carriers may include:

- signed or recorded commitments;
- repeated reliance on promised continuity;
- investments made because a continuation was treated as stable;
- institutional rules built around an earlier commitment;
- shared records that preserve obligations or release conditions;
- coordination arrangements whose reopening requires multiple parties;
- accumulated breach, switching, or renegotiation costs;
- role identities or procedures stabilized around a prior binding;
- dormant commitments that reactivate under specified conditions.

A general atmosphere of expectation is insufficient unless the relevant expectation is source-supported, frame-bound, and connected to a concrete present effect.

```text
expectation
≠ Binding occurrence automatically

Binding occurrence
≠ moral obligation automatically
```

#### Current enforceability versus historical reliance

The present may contain an enforceable rule, contract, policy, or technical lock that fully explains current constraint. Binding Dependence requires additional discrimination: what present commitment, reliance, cost, or reopening condition cannot be adequately reconstructed from current enforceability alone?

Relevant distinctions include:

- current legal enforceability versus historically accumulated reliance;
- current technical lock-in versus earlier coordination investment;
- present formal membership versus prior commitments that shaped role expectations;
- current exit clause versus sunk transition or breach costs;
- present promise text versus credibility or reliance generated by the prior sequence.

```text
current enforceability
≠ historical Binding Dependence
```

The two may coexist. The Record must allocate their contributions rather than allowing one to absorb the other.

#### Retention, transfer, conflict, release, and dormancy

A binding carrier may:

- persist in the same form;
- transfer into a new contract, role, or institutional record;
- conflict with later commitments;
- be partly released while costs or reliance remain;
- become dormant and later reactivate;
- be formally terminated but practically retained through infrastructure or expectation;
- be repaired through explicit renegotiation;
- lose present load where the carrier is genuinely dissolved.

A new label, renewed agreement, changed role title, or declared restart does not by itself establish either persistence or erasure. WP3 later owns the complete modifier and reset test.

#### Revision and exit remain possible

Binding Dependence may make change costly without making it impossible. The claim must preserve:

- potential renegotiation;
- release conditions;
- alternative coordination;
- repair or integration;
- partial exit;
- changed future commitments;
- source uncertainty.

It must not infer:

- permanent obligation;
- moral blame for exit;
- invalidity of revision;
- deterministic continuation;
- identity essence;
- authority to enforce the binding.

#### Profile-specific claim view

```yaml
binding_dependence:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  present_analytical_cut:
  tested_dimension:
  prior_binding_occurrences:
  reliance_or_coordination_investment:
  historical_carrier:
  present_commitment_or_reopening_effect:
  current_enforceability_contribution:
  current_state_rival:
  omission_result:
  release_repair_transfer_or_dormancy:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
```

The view is descriptive and auditable, not an enforcement instrument.

#### C12-BIND-01 — same present agreement, different reopening cost from prior reliance

Two regional partnerships now use the same standard agreement, have the same nominal termination period, and receive the same current service package.

**Partnership A** entered the standard agreement directly. Its records, staff routines, and supplier interfaces remain portable.

**Partnership B** reached the standard agreement after a sequence of temporary assurances and repeated renewals:

- staff were trained around a bespoke interface;
- local partners invested in compatible equipment after continuity assurances;
- shared records were migrated into the provider's classification system;
- earlier renewal decisions deferred development of an independent alternative;
- the current standard agreement formally replaces the temporary arrangements.

At the present cut, the agreement text explains the formal termination period in both partnerships. It does not fully explain why reopening coordination in Partnership B requires equipment conversion, record translation, retraining, and renegotiation with dependent partners.

```text
same current agreement
+ different prior reliance and coordination investment
+ retained material and record carriers
+ different present reopening cost
→ bounded Binding-Dependence candidate
```

The result concerns reopening and transition cost. It does not prove that the provider acted wrongfully, that continued participation is required, or that exit is impossible. Pending WP3, a suitable local result is `admissible_but_provisional`.

<a id="12-7-lambda-theta-residual-dependence"></a>

### 12.7 `Λ + Θ` — Residual Dependence

Chapter 11 established **Residual Accumulation** where warranted Non-Events, unresolved transitions, missed windows, delayed repair, repeated non-decision, or unintegrated expectations remain carried into the present. **Residual Dependence** is present where that earlier non-realization is materially indispensable to reconstructing a declared current cost, credibility relation, evidentiary burden, role allocation, accessibility condition, or continuation field.

```text
Residual Accumulation
≠ Residual Dependence automatically
```

```text
Λ occurrence
+ expectation-bound non-realization under Θ
+ present-bearing residue
+ historical indispensability
→ bounded Residual-Dependence candidate
```

The claim requires a warranted `Λ` occurrence. Missing information, undocumented events, analytical ignorance, or mere absence cannot be converted into a dependence-bearing Non-Event.

#### Expectation and realization window

Residual Dependence begins from an explicit expectation structure:

- what was expected;
- by whom or by which procedure;
- within which frame;
- during which bounded realization window;
- on what source basis;
- what did not occur;
- what present residue followed.

```text
missing record
≠ Non-Event

mere absence
≠ residual carrier
```

The expectation may be institutional, procedural, relational, contractual, or materially prepared. It need not imply moral duty. It must be sufficiently warranted for the non-occurrence to have structural meaning.

#### Dependence-bearing residue

Possible carriers include:

- unresolved backlogs created by repeated non-decision;
- missed eligibility, repair, or transition windows;
- records that remain incomplete because a required review did not occur;
- credibility burdens created by repeated unfulfilled procedural expectations;
- blocked alternatives that later require exceptional reopening;
- role ambiguity retained because no allocation decision was made;
- costs shifted forward by delayed maintenance or integration;
- unclosed exceptions that become precedent-like constraints;
- reliance or coordination burdens generated by prolonged suspension.

The present effect must remain distinguishable from a current-only rule or resource shortage. A backlog may now be severe, but severity alone does not establish that its current structure depends on the earlier Non-Event.

#### Later closure, repair, and changed frame

A later event may repair, close, translate, or replace the earlier residue. The analysis must test:

- whether the original expectation remains relevant;
- whether the missed window can still be reopened;
- whether later repair removes the present burden or only changes its form;
- whether a new frame renders the old Non-Event irrelevant;
- whether current constraints independently reproduce the same effect;
- which residue, if any, remains at the present cut.

```text
later event
≠ earlier Non-Event erased automatically

historical Non-Event
≠ permanent residue automatically
```

WP3 later owns the full `Φ`, modifier, and reset architecture.

#### Counterpressure and false residual dependence

A Residual-Dependence claim fails or reduces where:

- the expectation frame is retrospective or unsupported;
- the realization window is undefined;
- the alleged non-occurrence is only missing information;
- the current effect is fully explained by current conditions;
- later repair removed the relevant carrier;
- the result remains unchanged under material source variation;
- every delay or absence is inflated into `Λ`;
- the claim assigns guilt, intention, or incapacity without independent warrant.

```text
historical gap
+ no warranted expectation
= no Λ-based dependence claim
```

#### Profile-specific claim view

```yaml
residual_dependence:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  present_analytical_cut:
  tested_dimension:
  expectation_frame:
  realization_window:
  warranted_non_event:
  historical_residual_carrier:
  present_residual_effect:
  current_state_rival:
  omission_result:
  later_closure_repair_or_reframing:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
```

The declaration cannot turn absence into evidence.

#### C12-RES-01 — same current repair procedure, different evidentiary burden after a missed review window

Two grant programs now use the same repair procedure, the same current eligibility rule, and the same review staff.

**Program A** performed the scheduled eligibility review before migrating its records. Disputed classifications were resolved and documented before the new procedure began.

**Program B** repeatedly deferred the scheduled review during migration:

- the review window was formally announced and documented;
- no decision was issued before the legacy system closed;
- unresolved classifications were imported as provisional flags;
- later applicants must reconstruct older evidence to remove those flags;
- the current repair procedure is identical to Program A's, but some Program B cases carry an additional historical proof burden.

At the present cut, current eligibility rules explain what must ultimately be shown. They do not fully explain why otherwise comparable Program B cases must reconstruct evidence from the missed review period.

```text
same current repair procedure
+ warranted missed review window
+ retained provisional-flag carrier
+ different present evidentiary burden
→ bounded Residual-Dependence candidate
```

The claim is limited to evidentiary burden and repair accessibility. It does not infer blame, deliberate obstruction, or permanent exclusion. A suitable local result is `admissible_but_provisional` where the expectation window and record lineage are supported.

#### Cross-profile interaction

The four profiles may coexist and interact:

```text
Α + Θ
→ one continuation becomes easier or more visible

Ω + Θ
→ its burdens or access are distributed unequally

Ψ + Θ
→ reliance and reopening costs become bound to it

Λ + Θ
→ unresolved non-realizations preserve additional residue
```

This sequence is illustrative, not universal. A Path may instantiate the profiles in another order, in parallel, in conflict, or only partially.

```text
profile interaction
≠ operator fusion
≠ universal causal chain
≠ additive dependence score
```

Each profile must retain:

- its own occurrence reference;
- historical carrier;
- temporal trace;
- tested present dimension;
- current-state rival;
- omission or variation pressure;
- uncertainty and Loss;
- claim and authority ceiling.

#### No all-profile requirement

Strong Path Dependence may be carried primarily by one profile. For example:

- archive lineage may establish bounded Asymmetry Dependence without a material Binding claim;
- a missed review window may establish Residual Dependence without a stable Attractor;
- reliance investment may establish Binding Dependence even where present role distribution is symmetric;
- a repeated route may establish Attractor Dependence while no warranted Non-Event is present.

```text
one sufficiently supported dependence-bearing profile
may support one bounded dimension

all four profiles
are not required
```

Conversely, the presence of all four sedimentation profiles does not establish strong Path Dependence if current conditions adequately explain the tested present feature.

#### Non-compensation

No profile can compensate for:

- an invalid Path or Trajectory substrate;
- missing present analytical cut;
- an undeclared dependence dimension;
- absent current-state baseline;
- failed Source–Result Dependency;
- no material present effect;
- unsupported omission or comparison pressure;
- source or claim ceiling breach;
- Type or Reference discontinuity;
- missing Stop or Non-Capture capacity.

Likewise, profile richness cannot compensate for a profile-specific failure:

```text
strong Attractor narrative
≠ supported Asymmetry claim

severe Asymmetry
≠ supported Binding lineage

Binding record
≠ warranted Non-Event

Residual history
≠ current dependence automatically
```

A profile may be `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, another `resolution_neutral`, another `claim_reduction_required`, and another `non_capture` within the same Trajectory. The integrated result must preserve these local differences rather than collapse them into one strength label.

#### C12-PROFILE-01 — rich four-profile history without failed current-state sufficiency

A regional scheduling system has a long documented history:

- one route was repeatedly used (`Α + Θ`);
- senior roles handled most exceptions (`Ω + Θ`);
- partners renewed annual coordination commitments (`Ψ + Θ`);
- several improvement reviews were delayed (`Λ + Θ`).

An analysis infers strong Path Dependence in current scheduling cost merely because all four profiles are present.

The present cut shows, however:

- a newly imposed external platform determines all routing;
- current licensing prices determine switching cost;
- the old reviewer hierarchy no longer controls access;
- annual commitments were terminated before migration;
- delayed reviews left no active backlog or classification residue;
- material variation in the earlier Path would not change the current scheduling-cost reconstruction under the new platform.

The historical profiles remain relevant to the former Trajectory. They do not carry the tested current cost.

```text
four historical sedimentation profiles
+ no active present carrier in tested dimension
+ current conditions sufficient
→ no strong Path Dependence in current scheduling cost
```

The strong claim requires `claim_reduction_required`. Warranted historical findings remain preserved; depending on the tested level, the current dependence result may be `resolution_neutral`. The case demonstrates both non-compensation and the prohibition on additive profile scoring.

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 12.4–12.7 now canonically establish:

- `Α + Θ` Attractor Dependence as historically indispensable present friction, default expectation, visibility, or continuation accessibility rather than mere recurrence or Attractor Sedimentation;
- `Ω + Θ` Asymmetry Dependence as historically indispensable differential cost, access, exposure, capacity, burden, role, or exit condition rather than present inequality alone;
- `Ψ + Θ` Binding Dependence as historically indispensable commitment, reliance, coordination investment, reopening cost, or release condition rather than expectation or current enforceability alone;
- `Λ + Θ` Residual Dependence as historically indispensable present residue from a warranted expectation-bound Non-Event rather than missing information or mere absence;
- current-state sufficiency challenges specific to each profile;
- profile-specific omission and source-variation pressure without pre-empting WP3's complete Path-Dependence test;
- separation of sedimentation support from dependence warrant;
- separation of operator type, occurrence relation, Path-Dependence property, operation, target function, and authority;
- interaction among profiles without operator fusion, universal causal chain, additive score, or all-profile requirement;
- the possibility that one profile supports one bounded dependence dimension while other profiles remain absent, neutral, reduced, provisional, failed, or non-captured;
- strict non-compensation for invalid substrate, missing present effect, failed current-state challenge, unsupported Source–Result Dependency, or source and claim ceiling breach;
- all five assigned WP2 Pressure Cases.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP2 defect. It preserves the remaining Chapter-12 burdens:

- `Φ` under Path Dependence;
- recontextualization versus reset;
- `Χ`, `Σ`, and later `Ψ` as modifiers without erasure;
- occurrence separation for earlier and later `Ψ`;
- Path Dependence without determinism or prediction;
- the complete Historical-Omission and source-bounded Alternative-History test;
- Failure, Claim Reduction, Minimal Record mapping, Stop, Non-Capture, and re-entry;
- integrated Chapter-12 audit and Provisional Lock.

```text
WP2 completion
≠ Chapter 12 provisional lock
≠ completed Path-Dependence test
≠ profile fusion
≠ universal dependence score
≠ all-profile requirement
≠ causal necessity
≠ prediction
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 12 — WP3:
Recontextualization, Modifiers,
Non-Determinism, Test, Failure,
Record, Stop, and Non-Capture
```

<a id="12-8-phi-under-path-dependence"></a>

### 12.8 `Φ` under Path Dependence

Recontextualization changes the frame in which an earlier Path, Trajectory, commitment, cost, role, expectation, or residue becomes legible. It may alter what the history means, which part of it remains relevant, how its carriers are interpreted, and which continuation is now available. It does not, by itself, remove the historical conditions through which the present configuration became possible.

```text
Recontextualization
≠ reset
```

This boundary is necessary because a new vocabulary, institution, role title, technical platform, contractual form, or interpretive frame can make an arrangement appear new while inherited friction, exposure, reliance, archive lineage, practical exit cost, or unresolved residue continues to structure the present. The reverse error is equally important: the existence of an earlier Path does not prove that every new frame continues it unchanged. `Φ` can weaken, translate, redistribute, contain, interrupt, or render irrelevant one or more historical carriers. Persistence and erasure both require testing.

#### Frame change and dependence continuity

A Path-Dependence claim across recontextualization must distinguish at least:

- the source frame under which the historical carrier formed;
- the recontextualizing occurrence or transition;
- the target frame at the present analytical cut;
- the carrier's retention, translation, attenuation, redistribution, replacement, or loss;
- the tested present dimension;
- the contribution of current target-frame conditions;
- the remaining source–result dependency;
- uncertainty and canonical Loss.

A historical carrier may continue under a new frame in several ways:

- a former routine becomes an informal default inside a new formal procedure;
- an earlier role hierarchy becomes uneven access to records or expertise;
- a terminated contract leaves reliance and migration costs;
- an unresolved Non-Event becomes a proof burden in a new review system;
- an old classification becomes a data field under a new technical label;
- an earlier Attractor is reproduced by new actors who inherited its infrastructure.

The new frame need not preserve the earlier semantics. What must remain traceable is the relation by which the earlier Path still changes the tested present reconstruction.

```text
semantic discontinuity
may coexist with
functional or cost continuity
```

This does not authorize `PROJECT_AS`. Chapter 12 is still testing a property of a historical object. It is not assigning the Path or Trajectory a contextual target function.

#### Recontextualization as attenuation or release

`Φ` may also materially reduce dependence. A new frame can:

- make an earlier route optional rather than default;
- redistribute knowledge and access;
- replace a historically accumulated technical carrier;
- release commitments under documented transition conditions;
- close a previously unresolved classification;
- provide new resources that make formerly costly alternatives accessible;
- alter the relevance of an earlier distinction.

The dependence claim must then be revised at the level of the affected dimension. It may remain strong in evidentiary burden while becoming weak in coordination friction, or disappear in access while persisting in exit cost.

```text
recontextualization of one dimension
≠ whole-object reset

continued dependence in one dimension
≠ unchanged dependence in every dimension
```

A declared restart therefore creates a new testable claim. It does not inherit either persistence or erasure.

#### Apparent restart

An apparent restart occurs where the target frame presents the present as a new beginning while material carriers remain active. Relevant indicators may include:

- the same archive or data lineage;
- inherited templates, routines, or default pathways;
- continuing role-specific knowledge concentration;
- transfer of commitments or reliance;
- retained provisional classifications;
- unchanged practical exit or reopening costs;
- continued dependence on earlier infrastructure.

These indicators support testing; they do not prove dependence by themselves. A new organization may inherit an archive while current rules fully explain the tested present outcome. Conversely, a formal clean break may leave historically indispensable costs outside the new institution's own record.

#### Recontextualization declaration

A bounded declaration may use:

```yaml
recontextualization_under_dependence:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  source_frame:
  recontextualizing_occurrence:
  target_frame:
  present_analytical_cut:
  tested_dimension:
  historical_carrier_before_recontextualization:
  carrier_status_after_recontextualization:
  current_target_frame_conditions:
  present_effect:
  source_result_dependency:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
```

Field completion does not establish persistence, attenuation, or reset.

#### C12-PHI-01 — new institutional frame, retained cost and role expectation

A public service is transferred from a departmental program into a formally independent agency. The new agency introduces:

- a new legal mandate;
- new role titles;
- a new public-facing application portal;
- a stated commitment to equal access;
- a new review vocabulary.

The present surface appears discontinuous from the earlier program. However:

- the agency inherits the complete legacy case archive;
- only former departmental specialists can interpret several legacy classifications;
- applicants whose cases began under the old program must supply additional bridging evidence;
- former internal escalation routes remain the fastest route for resolving disputed records;
- current staff continue to treat legacy classifications as presumptively valid unless challenged.

The new frame changes the public meaning of the service and opens additional formal routes. It does not fully explain why legacy applicants face higher evidentiary and coordination costs or why former specialists retain practical gatekeeping capacity.

```text
new mandate and vocabulary
+ retained archive and expertise carrier
+ continuing differential proof and access burden
→ recontextualization without full reset
```

The claim is bounded to evidentiary burden, specialist access, and dispute resolution. It does not establish that the new agency is merely the old department, that reform is ineffective, or that every current feature is Path Dependent. A suitable local result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim` where the archive, role, and burden lineage is supported.

---

<a id="12-9-chi-sigma-and-psi-as-later-trajectory-modifiers"></a>

### 12.9 `Χ`, `Σ`, and `Ψ` as Later Trajectory Modifiers

Later occurrences of Distance, Integration, and Self-Binding can change the practical force of a Path without making its earlier structure unreal. They are therefore treated as **modifiers of dependence-bearing relations**, not automatic reset functions.

```text
modifier
≠ erasure
```

A modifier is an occurrence that changes how one or more historical carriers operate at a later analytical cut. It may attenuate, interrupt, integrate, redistribute, release, stabilize, amplify, translate, or contain a carrier. The result must be tested dimension by dimension.

#### `Χ` as interruption and reflective distance

A later `Χ` occurrence can:

- interrupt an established route;
- suspend automatic continuation;
- expose a previously invisible historical carrier;
- create a review interval;
- permit alternatives to become legible;
- reduce immediate Attractor pressure;
- separate current evaluation from inherited expectation.

Distance does not itself remove accumulated cost, redistribute expertise, release commitments, repair missing records, or reopen lost windows. It changes the relation to the Path and may create conditions for later repair.

```text
successful interruption
≠ completed release

reflective legibility
≠ erased historical load
```

A pause can nevertheless be materially transformative. If it makes an alternative genuinely accessible, removes the present effect of a carrier, and survives the current-state and omission tests, the dependence claim must be reduced accordingly.

#### `Σ` as integration without historical neutralization

A later `Σ` occurrence can coordinate previously conflicting carriers or bring residual burdens into a workable structure. It may:

- combine old and new records;
- reconcile incompatible role expectations;
- distribute transition costs;
- establish a common review protocol;
- preserve disagreement while making continuation possible;
- convert an unresolved residue into an explicit, bounded obligation.

Integration can change the present reconstruction profoundly. Yet integration is not retroactive cancellation. An integrated settlement may still be costly because of the Path it repairs; a common record may preserve an earlier asymmetry in evidentiary burden; a workable compromise may retain dependence in one dimension while reducing it in another.

```text
integrated present
≠ history-free present
```

The analysis must distinguish the historical carrier, the integrating occurrence, and the post-integration present effect.

#### Later `Ψ` and occurrence separation

Chapter 11 and Section 12.6 examine `Ψ + Θ` where earlier commitments, reliance, or coordination investment accumulate into Binding Dependence. Section 12.9 also needs a separate concept: a **later `Ψ` occurrence** may bind a system to repair, release, transition, review, preservation, or a new continuation.

These are temporally distinct occurrences:

```text
earlier Ψ occurrences + Θ
→ possible Binding-Dependence carrier

later Ψ occurrence
→ possible modifier of that carrier
```

They may have different actors, frames, objects, validity periods, and effects. A later repair commitment does not prove that the earlier Binding was legitimate. An earlier commitment does not automatically authorize the later one. The Record must preserve both occurrence identities.

A later `Ψ` may:

- stabilize a repair process;
- bind resources to a transition;
- establish release conditions;
- preserve an archive while reducing gatekeeping;
- create a new commitment that conflicts with an older one;
- strengthen rather than reduce an inherited Path.

No semantic direction is built into the operator. Self-Binding can support repair, persistence, escalation, containment, or renewed dependence depending on the occurrence.

#### Modifier outcomes

A modifier review may find:

- **retention** — the carrier remains materially active;
- **attenuation** — its present effect is reduced but not removed;
- **redistribution** — burden or access changes location;
- **translation** — the carrier persists under a new representation;
- **containment** — its effect is bounded to a narrower scope;
- **release** — the tested present dimension no longer materially depends on the carrier;
- **replacement** — a current carrier now explains the feature better;
- **amplification** — the later occurrence increases dependence;
- **underdetermination** — sources do not establish the outcome.

These are local descriptive findings, not new Output Classes or a universal modifier enum. They must map to the canonical result architecture when the claim closes.

#### Modifier declaration

```yaml
later_modifier:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  earlier_dependence_claim_reference:
  modifier_occurrence:
    operator_typing:
    frame:
    temporal_position:
    source_basis:
  tested_dimension:
  historical_carrier_before_modifier:
  modifier_mechanism:
  carrier_status_after_modifier:
  current_conditions:
  present_effect:
  omission_or_comparison_result:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
```

A later modifier must not overwrite the earlier claim record. It creates a linked, later test.

#### C12-MOD-01 — interruption, integration, and later repair commitment with retained residue

A consortium has relied for years on one member's undocumented coordination routines. This history produces:

- concentrated procedural knowledge;
- unequal substitution capacity;
- repeated reliance on informal escalation;
- unresolved gaps in shared documentation.

After a service failure, the consortium introduces three later occurrences:

1. a six-week suspension of the informal escalation route (`Χ`);
2. a common documentation and review process (`Σ`);
3. a funded two-year commitment to knowledge transfer and transition support (later `Ψ`).

At the present cut:

- ordinary requests no longer require the informal route;
- procedural knowledge is more widely distributed;
- transition support reduces immediate substitution risk;
- several legacy cases still require the original member's interpretation;
- the historical documentation gap continues to affect evidentiary burden.

```text
later Χ + Σ + Ψ
→ material attenuation of access and coordination dependence

retained legacy interpretation and evidence burden
→ no complete historical erasure
```

The correct result is dimension-specific. Strong dependence in ordinary routing may require reduction; bounded Residual or Asymmetry Dependence may remain for legacy cases. The later `Ψ` is recorded separately from the earlier reliance-based `Ψ + Θ` carrier. A suitable integrated result is `partially_admissible` or a segmented set of canonical results, depending on record delimitation.

---

<a id="12-10-path-dependence-without-determinism"></a>

### 12.10 Path Dependence without Determinism

Path Dependence changes the reconstruction of present possibilities. It does not convert them into a necessary future.

```text
historical constraint
≠ historical destiny
```

A strongly Path-Dependent configuration may make one continuation cheaper, more credible, more visible, easier to authorize, or institutionally easier to coordinate. It may make other continuations costly, obscure, delayed, or dependent on external resources. None of these relations establishes impossibility, inevitability, optimality, legitimacy, or prediction.

#### Constraint, accessibility, and possibility

The chapter distinguishes:

- **current availability** — whether a continuation is structurally open at the present cut;
- **accessibility** — what friction, knowledge, authorization, time, coordination, or cost is required;
- **reachability** — whether the continuation can be attained through a source-supported sequence;
- **expectedness** — whether actors or institutions treat it as likely or normal;
- **determination** — the much stronger claim that another continuation cannot occur or that one future is necessary.

Path Dependence may support the first four. It does not establish the fifth.

```text
reduced accessibility
≠ impossibility

increased expectedness
≠ prediction

high exit cost
≠ no exit
```

#### Contingent intervention and new events

New events may alter a Path:

- resources may be added;
- a carrier may be replaced;
- a role may be redistributed;
- an archive may be opened;
- a commitment may be released;
- a repair window may be created;
- a new frame may make alternatives legible;
- external intervention may change practical cost.

These possibilities do not refute prior dependence. They show that dependence was conditional rather than metaphysically fixed. Conversely, the mere theoretical possibility of change does not make present dependence weak. The test concerns the current reconstruction under declared conditions, not an unlimited space of imaginable interventions.

#### Counterfactual and predictive boundary

A source-bounded alternative-history test asks whether a relevant change in the earlier Path would alter the present reconstruction. It does not forecast what will happen next. Nor does it establish that the alternative history would certainly have produced the modeled present.

```text
counterfactual sensitivity
≠ forecast
≠ causal isolation
≠ probability estimate
```

Qualitative claims such as “more costly,” “less accessible,” or “harder to reverse” require source support. Numerical probability, risk, or effect-size claims require independent empirical methods not supplied by STRATA.

#### No legitimacy from persistence

A continuation may be historically stabilized without being justified, desirable, mature, efficient, lawful, or normatively valid. A difficult exit may be structurally real without authorizing intervention. A successful repair may reduce dependence without retroactively validating the earlier Path.

```text
historical persistence
≠ legitimacy

structural dependence
≠ duty to continue

available alternative
≠ recommendation to choose it
```

#### C12-NDET-01 — strong historical load with a costly but real alternative continuation

A research network uses a specialized shared repository created over twelve years. Its current configuration depends on:

- accumulated metadata conventions;
- contributor-specific archive knowledge;
- long-standing citation and access commitments;
- unresolved migration exceptions.

A new open standard can replace the repository. The alternative is real and source-supported:

- conversion tools exist;
- funding has been approved;
- several comparable networks have migrated;
- the network can preserve the original archive in read-only form.

However, migration currently requires:

- manual correction of legacy metadata;
- renegotiation of contributor commitments;
- temporary duplication of infrastructure;
- resolution of unresolved exceptions;
- acceptance of a two-year transition burden.

The present configuration is strongly Path Dependent in migration cost, archive interpretation, and coordination burden. The alternative continuation remains reachable.

```text
strong present historical dependence
+ costly but real source-supported alternative
→ dependence without determinism
```

The claim does not predict whether migration will occur, recommend it, or infer that continued use is legitimate. A suitable result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

---

<a id="12-11-path-dependence-test"></a>

### 12.11 Path Dependence Test

The Path-Dependence test is a controlled property test applied to a warranted Path or Trajectory. It is not a fourth STRATA operation and does not replace `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS`.

```text
property test
≠ transformation operation
```

The test is conjunctive and non-compensatory. A detailed historical narrative cannot compensate for an invalid input. A strong profile cannot compensate for an unspecified present effect. A plausible alternative history cannot compensate for missing source support. A complete Record cannot compensate for failed Current-State Sufficiency pressure.

#### Stage 1 — Input integrity and lineage

Verify:

- a warranted Path, Trajectory, bounded Trajectory segment, or separately tested competing construction;
- declared frame, granularity, relative level, temporal scope, and present analytical cut;
- traceable source basis and uncertainty;
- explicit lineage to upstream Path and Trajectory results;
- no failed upstream claim being treated as settled;
- no operation or target function being inherited automatically.

If input integrity fails, the strong dependence test cannot proceed. A weaker chronology, sequence, Path segment, or source-uncertainty finding may remain.

#### Stage 2 — Tested dimension and claim segmentation

Declare exactly what is claimed to depend on history. Typical dimensions include:

- meaning or interpretation;
- credibility or expectation;
- cost or friction;
- role, burden, or exposure;
- access, authorization, or substitutability;
- practical exit or reopening conditions;
- reversibility;
- evidentiary burden;
- coordination feasibility;
- continuation accessibility or reachability.

A claim that shifts from one dimension to another during the argument must be segmented or reduced.

```text
dependence in one dimension
≠ whole-object dependence
```

#### Stage 3 — Current-state baseline

Construct the best source-supported explanation that uses current conditions only. It should include, where relevant:

- current rules and formal obligations;
- current resources and material constraints;
- current role allocation and access;
- current technical or institutional infrastructure;
- current frame and classification;
- current alternatives and available repair mechanisms;
- current external conditions.

The baseline must be a real counterpressure, not a deliberately weak rival. The question is whether current conditions adequately explain the tested present feature without the earlier Path.

#### Stage 4 — Historical carrier and profile support

Identify:

- the historical carrier;
- the relevant `Α + Θ`, `Ω + Θ`, `Ψ + Θ`, or `Λ + Θ` profile, if any;
- its temporal trace;
- retention, translation, attenuation, redistribution, replacement, or release;
- its present effect;
- its interaction with current conditions;
- uncertainty and Loss.

No profile is obligatory. The carrier may span more than one profile, but the relations must not be fused into a score or new operator.

#### Stage 5 — Historical-Omission test

Ask:

```text
What material part of the present reconstruction
becomes wrong, incomplete, or misleading
if the relevant prior Path is omitted?
```

A successful omission test identifies a specific changed reconstruction, such as:

- current cost appears lower than it is;
- role substitutability is overstated;
- credibility differences disappear;
- evidence burden is misallocated;
- a continuation appears equally accessible when it is not;
- practical exit is confused with formal exit;
- a current classification appears self-explanatory despite inherited lineage.

A more coherent or vivid narrative is insufficient. The omission must alter a warranted praxeological reconstruction.

#### Stage 6 — Source-bounded Alternative-History test

Ask:

```text
Would a relevant source-supported change in the prior Path
alter the tested present meaning, cost, role, credibility,
accessibility, reachability, or continuation claim?
```

The variation must be identified as one of:

- an actual comparator;
- a competing source-supported historical reconstruction;
- a historically available but unrealized bounded alternative;
- an analytically controlled omission;
- underdetermined with available sources.

The test must state:

- which earlier element or relation changes;
- why the variation is source-supported or historically available;
- which present features are held approximately constant;
- which present differences remain and why they matter;
- where comparability fails;
- what cannot be inferred.

Chapter 13 later owns the detailed branch taxonomy and availability windows. Chapter 46 later owns the general Counterfactual-Sensitivity architecture. Chapter 12 uses only the minimum source-bounded variation necessary for the property test.

#### Stage 7 — Current-condition challenge and contribution allocation

Compare the historical explanation with current conditions. Possible findings include:

- current conditions are sufficient;
- current conditions explain most of the feature while history adds a weak local difference;
- current and historical conditions jointly carry the feature;
- the historical carrier is materially indispensable in the tested dimension;
- sources do not permit responsible allocation.

Joint contribution is common. Strong Path Dependence does not require history to be the only cause or condition. It requires that omission of the relevant Path materially damages the present reconstruction.

```text
historical indispensability
≠ exclusive causation
```

#### Stage 8 — Dependence-strength finding

A local property finding may be recorded as:

- no material historical dependence at the tested level;
- weak order dependence;
- strong Path-Dependence candidate;
- warranted strong Path Dependence;
- underdetermined dependence;
- failed dependence claim.

These are local findings, not new canonical Output Classes. Closure requires mapping to one of the ten classes.

A typical mapping may be:

| Local finding | Possible canonical mapping | Boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| warranted strong Path Dependence | `admissible_with_bounded_claim` | dimension, period, source, and authority ceilings explicit |
| strong candidate with material unresolved support | `admissible_but_provisional` | re-entry condition specified |
| mixed carrier or segmented dimension | `partially_admissible` | passing and failing segments separated |
| detailed test with no changed reconstruction | `resolution_neutral` | no unsupported stronger claim retained |
| strong claim fails but weak order effect remains | `claim_reduction_required` | weaker finding and failed claim preserved |
| input or carrier invalid | `failed_transformation` where tied to a failed transformation claim, or bounded failed property claim in lineage | no laundering into later operation |
| unsafe continuation from known failure | `mandatory_stop` | downstream use prohibited |
| materially rival explanations remain undecidable | `non_capture` | re-entry condition explicit |

The mapping is contextual and human-governed. The formal model cannot select it automatically.

#### Stage 9 — Loss, alternatives, governance, and re-entry

Close the claim with:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Also declare:

- rival current-state explanations;
- competing historical constructions;
- source-bounded alternative histories;
- excluded generalizations;
- Source and Claim Ceiling;
- Stop condition;
- Non-Capture status;
- re-entry condition;
- `authority_inheritance: prohibited`.

#### Minimal Path-Dependence Claim View

Chapter 12 uses a compact view inside the Shared Transformation Record:

```yaml
path_dependence:
  reference_object:
  path_or_trajectory_reference:
  present_analytical_cut:
  tested_dimension:
  current_state_baseline:
  historical_carrier:
  operator_profile_support:
  recontextualization_or_modifier_status:
  historical_omission_result:
  alternative_history_variation:
  source_result_dependency:
  current_condition_challenge:
  dependence_strength_finding:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
  output_class_mapping:
```

The view must remain integrated with Chapter 7 source, operation, target, admissibility, Loss, alternatives, governance, Stop, and Non-Capture fields. The `operation.kind` field is populated only where a separate STRATA operation occurrence is actually being recorded.

```text
complete dependence view
≠ dependence proof
≠ operation completion
≠ causal proof
```

#### C12-TEST-01 — approximately held-present comparison with an actual comparator

Two regional emergency units now use the same dispatch software, staffing ratio, formal escalation policy, and service target.

**Unit A** reached the current arrangement through a decade of centralized dispatch:

- exception authority remained concentrated in one specialist role;
- local teams rarely practiced independent rerouting;
- failed reviews left several legacy exception categories unresolved;
- the specialist archive remains necessary for high-risk cases.

**Unit B** reached the same formal present through distributed dispatch:

- exception authority rotated among teams;
- independent rerouting was routinely practiced;
- review records were resolved before migration;
- no role holds unique archive access.

At the present cut, ordinary dispatch performance is similar. High-risk rerouting differs:

- Unit A requires specialist availability and additional evidence;
- Unit B can reassign high-risk cases across several teams;
- current software and policy do not explain this difference;
- omission of the earlier Paths makes the current substitutability difference invisible.

```text
approximately similar present rules and infrastructure
+ actual comparator with different prior Paths
+ different current high-risk substitutability
+ current conditions insufficient
→ bounded strong Path Dependence in substitutability and escalation cost
```

The case does not establish causal isolation, whole-unit dependence, or deterministic continuation. A suitable result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

#### C12-REC-01 — complete claim view with unsupported carrier

An analyst completes every field in the Minimal Path-Dependence Claim View for a university admission procedure. The Record names:

- a Trajectory reference;
- a present cut;
- an alleged `Λ + Θ` carrier;
- an omission result;
- a current-state rival;
- a strong dependence finding;
- a bounded claim scope.

The alleged Non-Event is a “missed reform opportunity,” but sources show no declared reform window, no responsible process, no documented expectation, and no evidence that the supposed absence altered later admission records.

```text
complete fields
+ unsupported expectation frame and carrier
→ no Residual-Dependence warrant
```

The Record is structurally complete and substantively insufficient. The strong claim fails or requires reduction. Schema validity and field presence do not protect it.

---

<a id="12-12-failure-to-establish-path-dependence"></a>

### 12.12 Failure to Establish Path Dependence

A Path-Dependence claim fails when the stronger historical-indispensability burden is not met. Failure is not a denial of history. It is a bounded result concerning the tested property, dimension, cut, and source basis.

```text
failed strong-dependence claim
≠ no history
≠ no Path
≠ no Trajectory
≠ no present constraint
```

#### Failure conditions

Strong Path Dependence is not sufficiently established where:

- only temporal duration is shown;
- repetition is shown without a retained present effect;
- a Trajectory exists but the current feature remains adequately current-state-reconstructible;
- historical narration adds coherence, detail, or plausibility without changed discrimination;
- the historical carrier is unspecified, unsupported, or no longer active in the tested dimension;
- current conditions fully explain the feature;
- the Historical-Omission test changes only narrative richness;
- the alternative history is invented, unavailable, or materially non-comparable;
- source changes leave the claimed result arbitrarily unchanged;
- missing information is treated as a Non-Event;
- a new frame is assumed either to erase or preserve history without testing;
- later `Χ`, `Σ`, or `Ψ` is treated as automatic reset;
- the dependence dimension shifts during the argument;
- one dramatic event is substituted for sedimented historical load;
- a complete claim view is treated as proof;
- source, temporal, contextual, or authority ceiling is exceeded.

#### Claim reduction ladder

Where the strong property fails, warranted upstream and weaker findings must be preserved:

```text
strong Path Dependence
→ weak order dependence
→ Trajectory without established dependence
→ Path without established Trajectory
→ sequence or chronology
```

The ladder is not automatic. Each retained level must independently satisfy its own burden. Reduction records:

- the failed stronger claim;
- the reason for failure;
- the retained weaker finding;
- the updated claim scope;
- canonical Loss;
- re-entry conditions.

#### Resolution neutrality

A detailed dependence test may produce no additional praxeological discrimination. If the current reconstruction remains unchanged after responsible historical testing and no unsupported stronger claim is retained, the result may map to `resolution_neutral`.

```text
no additional dependence finding
≠ analysis error
```

Resolution neutrality can be a successful Stop. It prevents historical detail from being rewarded merely for being detailed.

#### C12-FAIL-01 — rich history without changed present reconstruction

A professional association has operated for eighty years and possesses extensive archives documenting changing committees, repeated debates, delayed reforms, and multiple governance revisions. An analyst claims that the current membership-fee level is strongly Path Dependent.

The present fee is determined by:

- a newly enacted statutory formula;
- current membership count;
- current insurance cost;
- a mandatory external levy;
- an annually published calculation.

Historical omission does not change the fee reconstruction. Earlier debates explain why the association values fee stability, but they do not materially determine the tested current amount or calculation burden.

```text
historically rich Trajectory
+ current statutory formula sufficient
+ no changed fee reconstruction under omission
→ failure of strong Path Dependence in current fee level
```

The association's Trajectory remains warranted. The dependence test in the specified dimension may map to `resolution_neutral` or require reduction from the strong claim.

#### C12-REDUCE-01 — strong claim reduced to weak order dependence

Two project teams use the same current review checklist and have the same approval authority. Team A previously experienced a public review failure; Team B did not.

Today:

- both teams follow the same formal procedure;
- approval cost and access are materially similar;
- Team A interprets ambiguous checklist items more cautiously;
- the earlier failure changes local credibility expectations but does not materially alter current authorization or feasibility.

A whole-process strong Path-Dependence claim is unsupported. A bounded weak order-dependence claim concerning interpretation and credibility remains.

```text
failed strong whole-process claim
+ supported local order effect
→ claim_reduction_required
```

The failed strong claim remains in lineage and cannot later be restated as a target function or prediction without a new test.

#### Mandatory Stop

`mandatory_stop` applies where a known failed, unsupported, or non-traceable dependence claim is used to:

- infer inevitability, destiny, forecast, or impossibility;
- assign a RETYPE target function;
- authorize intervention, sanction, diagnosis, person typing, legitimacy judgment, or irreversible label;
- erase a failed Path or Trajectory claim;
- treat schema validation or formal-model output as historical proof;
- continue recursive historical testing after Source or Claim Ceiling is exceeded;
- convert Non-Capture into a stronger conclusion.

Stop preserves the failure, weaker findings, uncertainty, Loss, and re-entry condition.

#### C12-STOP-01 — failed dependence claim used as predictive authority

A workforce system has a documented history of centralized assignment. A strong Path-Dependence claim concerning future employee retention fails because current retention differences are fully explained by a newly introduced compensation structure and current labor-market conditions.

A later report nevertheless states:

- centralized history makes future exit “inevitable” for one group;
- the historical Path should be projected as an Attractor function;
- individuals may be classified by likely departure;
- management should intervene automatically.

```text
known failed dependence claim
→ prediction + target-function assignment + person classification + intervention authority
→ mandatory_stop
```

The earlier history may remain a warranted Trajectory. It cannot support the downstream claims without new, independent tests and application authority.

#### Non-Capture

`non_capture` remains available where:

- current-state and historical explanations remain materially rival and source-responsible;
- actual comparators are unavailable;
- bounded alternative histories remain equally plausible;
- the carrier's post-recontextualization status cannot be established;
- present effects can be allocated to multiple histories without responsible adjudication;
- sources support a history-sensitive difference but not its strength;
- every available construction destroys decisive heterogeneity.

Non-Capture does not protect a preferred claim. It records that the present sources, frame, granularity, or comparison architecture cannot responsibly decide.

#### C12-NC-01 — current-state and historical explanations remain equally responsible

A municipal licensing office has slower resolution times than comparable offices. Two source-supported explanations remain:

1. **historical explanation** — legacy classification practices created an inherited backlog and specialized evidence burden;
2. **current-state explanation** — present staffing shortage and a newly expanded legal review requirement fully account for delay.

Available sources show:

- the backlog exists, but its current share cannot be separated from new cases;
- legacy classifications and current legal reviews use overlapping status codes;
- staffing records are incomplete for the transition year;
- no suitable actual comparator preserves both current law and staffing while varying only the earlier Path;
- bounded alternative-history models support materially different allocations.

Neither explanation can responsibly displace the other.

```text
material historical candidate
+ material current-state rival
+ inseparable carriers and insufficient comparison
→ non_capture
```

Re-entry requires disaggregated backlog data, transition-year staffing records, or a source-responsible comparator. Non-Capture does not authorize a midpoint score or a blended certainty claim.

#### Stop and re-entry discipline

A stopped, reduced, failed, neutral, or non-captured claim may be reopened only through a new testable claim that identifies at least one of:

- new source material;
- a corrected Path or Trajectory input;
- a stable present analytical cut;
- a better current-state baseline;
- a traceable historical carrier;
- an actual or source-bounded comparator;
- a documented modifier or recontextualization occurrence;
- a narrower dependence dimension;
- a reduced claim ceiling.

```text
new frame, label, granularity, periodization,
operation, projection, or visualization
≠ automatic re-entry
```

#### Chapter and operation handoffs

Chapter 12 hands forward:

- graded property findings to Chapter 13's detailed alternative-space analysis;
- bounded dependence claims to Chapter 15's separate `COMPOSE` mechanics;
- carrier references to Chapter 24's later `DECOMPOSE` analysis;
- minimum source-bounded variation pressure to Chapter 46's general Counterfactual-Sensitivity architecture;
- no target function to RETYPE.

```text
warranted Path Dependence
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
```

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 12.8–12.12 now canonically establish:

- `Φ` as recontextualization that may preserve, translate, attenuate, redistribute, replace, release, or render irrelevant historical carriers without functioning as an automatic reset;
- explicit testing of apparent restart and target-frame current conditions;
- later `Χ`, `Σ`, and `Ψ` occurrences as modifiers without erasure, including strict temporal separation between earlier `Ψ + Θ` Binding carriers and later `Ψ` modifier occurrences;
- dimension-specific modifier outcomes without new Output Classes, scores, or automatic semantics;
- Path Dependence as constraint on present accessibility, reachability, credibility, cost, or expectedness without fate, inevitability, prediction, causal necessity, legitimacy, or recommendation;
- a complete, conjunctive, non-compensatory local Path-Dependence test covering input integrity, claim segmentation, current-state baseline, carrier/profile support, Historical Omission, source-bounded Alternative History, current-condition allocation, strength finding, canonical mapping, Loss, Stop, Non-Capture, and re-entry;
- a Minimal Path-Dependence Claim View inside the Shared Transformation Record rather than a second schema;
- explicit failure conditions, reduction ladder, resolution neutrality, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, and anti-laundering discipline;
- protected handoffs to Chapters 13, 15, 24, and 46 and continued separation from RETYPE;
- all nine assigned WP3 Pressure Cases.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP3 repair requirement. It preserves the remaining Chapter-12 WP4 duties:

- integrated redundancy and ownership audit across Sections 12.1–12.12;
- confirmation of all nineteen Pressure Cases;
- dependence-gradient, no-determinism, modifier, test, Failure, Stop, and Non-Capture audit;
- complete Reference and bounded Formal-Model synchronization review;
- Chapter-12 Completion Test and Provisional Lock.

```text
WP3 completion
≠ Chapter 12 provisional lock
≠ causal proof
≠ prediction
≠ unique counterfactual
≠ operation completion
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 12 — WP4:
Synchronization,
Integrated Audit,
and Provisional Lock
```

#### Chapter 12 completion boundary

Chapter 12 has now operationalized Path Dependence as a graded property of a warranted Path, Trajectory, or bounded segment without converting historical contribution into historical indispensability, a temporal object into a property automatically, a property test into a fourth operation, or historical constraint into fate, prediction, causal necessity, target-function warrant, or application authority.

```text
historical contribution
≠ historical indispensability

warranted Trajectory
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

The Chapter 12 completion test is satisfied:

1. Path Dependence remains a property of a declared reference object, present analytical cut, and tested dependence dimension rather than a new object, PMS primitive, STRATA operation, relative level, target function, score, or authority layer;
2. no material historical dependence, weak order dependence, and strong Path Dependence remain distinguishable local findings without becoming new canonical Output Classes;
3. weak order dependence requires a bounded historical difference while the present remains substantially reconstructible from current conditions;
4. strong Path Dependence requires historical indispensability under a real Current-State Sufficiency Challenge and cannot be established by Trajectory status, duration, repetition, institutional age, archive density, narrative coherence, or `Θ` alone;
5. approximately held-present comparison declares retained similarities and differences without claiming object identity, experimental control, causal isolation, or prediction;
6. `Α + Θ`, `Ω + Θ`, `Ψ + Θ`, and `Λ + Θ` remain separate occurrence-level dependence-bearing profiles whose Chapter-11 sedimentation support does not automatically establish Chapter-12 dependence warrant;
7. each profile retains its own historical carrier, present effect, current-state rival, omission or source-variation pressure, repair or replacement status, uncertainty, Loss, and Claim Ceiling;
8. profile interaction does not fuse operators, create a universal causal chain, require all profiles, generate an additive dependence score, or compensate for an invalid substrate, absent present effect, failed current-state challenge, missing Source–Result Dependency, or exceeded Source/Claim Ceiling;
9. `Φ` may preserve, translate, attenuate, redistribute, replace, release, or render irrelevant historical carriers but does not function as an automatic reset;
10. later `Χ`, `Σ`, and `Ψ` occurrences remain modifiers rather than erasure operators, and an earlier `Ψ + Θ` Binding carrier remains distinct from a later `Ψ` modifier occurrence;
11. Path Dependence may alter accessibility, reachability, credibility, cost, expectedness, role, exposure, evidentiary burden, or continuation possibilities without making alternatives impossible, prescribing conduct, proving legitimacy, or predicting the future;
12. the complete local Path-Dependence test remains conjunctive and non-compensatory across input integrity, claim segmentation, current-state baseline, carrier/profile support, Historical Omission, source-bounded Alternative History, current-condition allocation, dependence-strength finding, canonical mapping, Loss, Alternatives, governance, Stop, Non-Capture, and re-entry;
13. the Minimal Path-Dependence Claim View remains a compact view inside the Shared Transformation Record and cannot turn field presence, schema validity, visualization, or machine-readable completeness into historical-indispensability proof or Output-Class selection;
14. Failure and Claim Reduction preserve warranted weaker findings through the ladder from strong dependence to weak order dependence, Trajectory without established dependence, Path without established Trajectory, and sequence or chronology, each under its own burden;
15. Mandatory Stop applies where a known failed dependence claim is reused for prediction, target-function assignment, person classification, intervention, sanction, legitimacy, or authority; Non-Capture remains available where materially rival historical and current explanations remain source-responsible but undecidable;
16. Chapter 13 receives bounded alternative-space and source-bounded comparison pressure without inherited branch classifications, Chapter 15 retains complete `COMPOSE` mechanics, Chapter 24 retains later `DECOMPOSE` analysis of carriers, Chapter 46 retains general Counterfactual-Sensitivity architecture, and RETYPE receives no target function;
17. all nineteen prepared Chapter-12 Pressure Cases are represented in the canonical chapter without being misreported as produced `03_cases/*` artifacts, empirical evidence, calibration data, training data, graph specifications, or application findings;
18. a graph, Reader view, revised frame, new label, granularity, periodization, operation, projection, or visualization cannot erase a failed result or inherit missing warrant;
19. no part of Chapter 12 changes `PMS.yaml`, the Δ–Ψ inventory, operator order, operator dependencies, the three-operation grammar, the ten Output Classes, the sixteen Admissibility Rules, or the twelve audit stages.

The integrated audit confirms that Chapter 12's dependence gradient is not a narrative-strength scale. A richer history is not automatically more indispensable. A visually dense profile map is not stronger evidence. A long Trajectory may be only weakly order-dependent, while one narrowly bounded present dimension may be strongly path-dependent under a short but source-traceable carrier history.

```text
historical richness
≠ dependence strength

visual complexity
≠ substantive warrant

formal completeness
≠ historical indispensability proof
```

The anti-laundering boundary remains active. A failed strong Path-Dependence claim may be reopened only as a new testable claim with a corrected input, present cut, dependence dimension, current-state baseline, historical carrier, comparator, source basis, modifier status, or reduced Claim Ceiling. A new graph, frame, label, operation, target function, or application context cannot inherit or conceal the failed burden.

```text
new dependence claim
= new testable claim

later operation success
≠ retroactive dependence repair

later PROJECT_AS success
≠ retroactive property warrant
```

The chapter-level result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known local Chapter-12 repair requirement. It preserves later pressure from Chapter 13 alternative-space reconstruction, Chapter 14 Non-Events, Chapter 15 `COMPOSE`, Chapter 16 PATH Boundary Conditions, Chapter 17 integrated PATH cases and audit, Chapter 24 dependence decomposition, Chapter 46 Counterfactual Sensitivity, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, Appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, and Model Finalization.

```text
Chapter 12 provisional lock
≠ causal proof
≠ prediction
≠ unique counterfactual
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

Reopen Chapter 12 only if later work demonstrates a concrete conflict involving the property/object distinction, dependence gradient, tested dimension, present analytical cut, Current-State Sufficiency Challenge, Historical Omission, source-bounded Alternative History, profile carrier, modifier status, non-determinism boundary, Failure/Reduction route, Minimal Claim View, Stop/Non-Capture route, Chapter-13/15/24/46 handoff, or unchanged PMS Base. Mere preference for a richer history, broader claim, more dramatic graph, stronger label, finer detail, or more decisive conclusion is not a reopening ground.

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 13 — Preparation:
Branches, Aborts, Delays,
and Unavailable Alternatives
```

<a id="chapter-13-branches-aborts-delays-and-unavailable-alternatives"></a>

## 13. Branches, Aborts, Delays, and Unavailable Alternatives
