- authorize intervention, sanction, or irreversible classification;
- assign a RETYPE target function;
- treat the branch finding as a completed `COMPOSE` operation;
- visually render an imagined branch as historically established;
- erase a prior failed claim by changing frame, label, graph, or granularity.

##### C13-STOP-01 — free counterfactual reused for authority

An analyst constructs an unsupported “reform path” from a later policy proposal and claims that earlier officials deliberately blocked the only successful alternative. The branch is then used to recommend sanctions and to label the institution structurally incapable of repair.

The historical availability, blocking relation, and unrealized success are all unsupported. Continuing the claim for sanction, person judgment, or institutional target-function assignment requires `mandatory_stop`.

```text
free story
+ unsupported success claim
+ authority use
→ mandatory_stop
```

#### Non-Capture

`non_capture` remains a positive outcome where the sources preserve a material alternative problem but do not responsibly distinguish the necessary status or availability relation.

It may apply where:

- records support both resource blockage and explicit rejection without allowing priority to be established;
- a continuation appears prepared, but its practical availability remains undecidable;
- a later same-labelled path may be resumed or newly constituted and the sources cannot decide;
- Non-Selection and informal rejection remain indistinguishable;
- the counterfactual horizon occurs immediately after the Branch Point;
- compression cannot preserve the material alternative field without inventing unsupported structure.

##### C13-NC-01 — rejection or blockage remains undecidable

A hospital network documented a proposal for a shared emergency laboratory. Meeting summaries show repeated objections and repeated statements that no building was available. The surviving records do not show whether the governing body rejected the proposal while it remained feasible or whether the lack of space made it practically unavailable before the decision.

The realized Path and the proposal's material relevance are supported. The rejected/blocked classification is not.

```text
material alternative problem supported
+ decisive status relation unresolved
→ non_capture
```

The result must not be forced into a more definite branch status merely to complete a graph or record.

#### Operation and function handoff

Chapter 13 classifies source-bounded historical alternatives and supplies them to later operations. It does not itself complete `COMPOSE`, because Chapter 15 still owns source selection, ordering, formation rule, target object, preserved structure, compression, exclusion, irrecoverable loss, and operation result.

It also does not assign a RETYPE target function. A blocked or lost alternative may later be relevant to a frame-, attractor-, macro-event-, or other contextual-function claim, but that claim requires a separate `PROJECT_AS` record and test.

##### C13-HANDOFF-01 — branch finding misused as operation and target function

A valid record establishes that a community-owned service route was materially available and later blocked by an authorization rule. A later summary states:

```text
blocked branch
→ completed COMPOSE of institutional exclusion
→ PROJECT_AS proof that the institution is an attractor
```

Both arrows are invalid. The branch finding is source material for a possible later composition and projection. It is neither a completed operation nor a target function. The claims must be separated; known continued reuse without the required records triggers `mandatory_stop`.

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 13.9–13.12 now canonically establish:

- Counterfactual Path as a bounded hypothetical continuation beginning only from a source-supported historical alternative;
- a declared variation rule, approximate stability conditions, source ceiling, and counterfactual horizon;
- historical availability as insufficient to establish an unrealized outcome;
- Counterfactual Path as non-predictive and distinct from Chapter-46 general Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- Non-Selection as source-supported absence of selection inside an active decision context and bounded window with praxeological consequence;
- Non-Selection as distinct from missing information, rejection, blockage, deferral, and automatic `Λ` classification;
- possible implicit realization through a bounded default rule plus actual traversal;
- Alternative-Space Compression as necessary but Loss-governed reduction of the visible branch field;
- preservation, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverability burdens for alternatives;
- graph and Reader rendering as Traceability support without status inference or claim upgrade;
- Alternative Status Record as a controlled Shared-Record `extensions` view;
- strict separation of historical alternatives from the top-level rival-transformation `alternatives` field;
- sufficiency of the existing schema carrier, with no new schema field, parallel schema, Rule, operation, Output Class, audit stage, enum, score, probability, or truth engine;
- formal completeness as distinct from historical availability or branch-status proof;
- Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, and Non-Capture routes;
- operation and RETYPE-function handoff without inheritance;
- all eight assigned WP3 Pressure Cases.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP3 repair requirement. It preserves the remaining Chapter-13 duties:

- WP4 integrated Contract, case-duty, source-window, Loss, anti-laundering, Stop, Non-Capture, Reference, and Formal-Model audit;
- later Chapter-14 Non-Event detail;
- later Chapter-15 complete `COMPOSE` procedure;
- later Chapter-46 general Counterfactual-Sensitivity architecture;
- later Reader graph implementation under the declared status and source boundaries.

```text
WP3 completion
≠ Chapter 13 provisional lock
≠ counterfactual truth
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ branch-status truth engine
≠ RETYPE target-function assignment
≠ causal, normative, predictive, or application authority
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 13 — WP4:
Synchronization,
Integrated Audit,
and Provisional Lock
```

#### Chapter 13 completion boundary

Chapter 13 has now operationalized the historical alternative space that makes a realized Path selective without converting retrospective plausibility into historical availability, an unrealized continuation into a known outcome, a branch status into an Output Class, a bounded counterfactual into prediction, or a source-supported alternative into completed `COMPOSE`, `PROJECT_AS`, causal proof, normative judgment, or application authority.

```text
retrospectively imaginable
≠ historically available

historically available
≠ known unrealized outcome
```

The Chapter 13 completion test is satisfied:

1. every warranted alternative claim declares a historical cut or window, source basis, frame, relevant roles, resources, permissions, costs, constraints, uncertainty, and bounded Claim Ceiling;
2. the alternative field remains selective and source-supported rather than an inventory of all logically imaginable or later technically possible continuations;
3. Branch Point requires at least two materially distinct, contemporaneously reachable or conditionally reachable continuations with differentiating conditions and source support;
4. Realized Branch requires actual entry and traversal rather than announcement, plan, preference, formal choice, or post-hoc success attribution;
5. Rejected Branch requires an available continuation and source-supported refusal or non-selection while the relevant window remained open;
6. Blocked Branch requires earlier availability or preparation plus a positive prevention relation and remains distinct from rejection, impossibility, present unavailability, and mere non-realization;
7. Aborted Branch requires an initiation trace and partial traversal and remains distinct from a never-begun or merely planned continuation;
8. Deferred Branch records a materially consequential temporal displacement and remains distinct from uninterrupted continuation or automatic later identity;
9. Lost Alternative requires earlier source-supported availability, later material unavailability or unreachability, and a traceable loss transition; it remains distinct from current unattractiveness, temporary blockage, increased cost alone, or retrospective imagination;
10. status and mechanism remain separable, so one continuation may be initiated, later blocked, and consequently aborted without forcing a single exclusive status label;
11. a later same-labelled continuation does not automatically inherit identity, continuity, availability, or the earlier branch's evidentiary status;
12. Counterfactual Path begins only from a source-supported historical alternative, declares the divergence rule and approximately held-stable conditions, and stops at the counterfactual horizon where source support ceases;
13. historical availability is insufficient to establish unrealized success, failure, preference, legitimacy, inevitability, causality, prediction, or optimality;
14. Non-Selection requires an active decision context, a bounded window, available alternatives, source-supported absence of selection, and a material Path effect; missing records alone do not establish it, and Chapter 14 retains full `Λ` determination;
15. Alternative-Space Compression remains governed by the canonical five-part Loss declaration and may not hide a constitutive alternative, status ambiguity, historical window, source gap, or irrecoverable branch information;
16. the Alternative Status Record remains an owner-bound view in the Shared Transformation Record's `extensions` carrier and does not overload the top-level rival-transformation `alternatives` field, replace required fields, create a parallel schema, or establish branch status from syntax;
17. local branch findings remain distinct from the ten canonical Output Classes and are mapped rather than promoted into a new status-class or output vocabulary;
18. Failure and Claim Reduction preserve warranted weaker findings from strong branch-status claim to bounded candidate continuation, uncertain historical alternative, realized Path without established alternative field, or no alternative claim;
19. Mandatory Stop applies where an unsupported alternative, free counterfactual, graph, label, or branch status is knowingly reused for prediction, sanction, legitimacy, person judgment, target-function assignment, irreversible classification, completed-operation claims, or authority;
20. Non-Capture remains available where the material alternative problem is source-supported but availability, rejection/blockage, resumption/reconstitution, selection/non-selection, or the counterfactual horizon cannot be responsibly resolved;
21. Chapter 14 receives bounded decision-window and non-selection candidates without inherited `Λ` typing, Chapter 15 receives source-supported alternatives and Loss declarations without completed `COMPOSE`, Chapter 16 retains PATH boundary tests, Chapter 17 retains integrated cases/audit, Chapter 46 retains general Counterfactual Sensitivity, and RETYPE receives no target function;
22. all nineteen prepared Chapter-13 Pressure Cases are represented in the canonical chapter without being misreported as produced `03_cases/*` artifacts, empirical evidence, calibration data, training data, finished graph specifications, historical truth, or application findings;
23. no graph, Reader rendering, revised frame, new label, granularity, periodization, operation, projection, record completion, schema validity, or visual complexity may erase a failed result or inherit missing historical warrant;
24. no part of Chapter 13 changes `PMS.yaml`, the Δ–Ψ inventory, operator order, operator dependencies, the three-operation grammar, the ten Output Classes, the sixteen Admissibility Rules, or the twelve audit stages.

The integrated audit confirms that Chapter 13's branch taxonomy is not a story-generation engine. More visible alternatives do not imply a richer historical field. A polished fork diagram does not establish availability. A complete extension record does not determine status. A source-supported Branch Point does not reveal what an unrealized continuation would have produced.

```text
more rendered branches
≠ more historical availability

record completeness
≠ branch-status proof

source-supported alternative
≠ known counterfactual outcome
```

The anti-laundering boundary remains active. A failed alternative or status claim may be reopened only as a new testable claim with a corrected historical cut, window, source basis, availability burden, status/mechanism distinction, initiation trace, loss transition, later-reachability analysis, uncertainty declaration, or reduced Claim Ceiling. A new graph, label, frame, granularity, operation, target function, application context, or same-named later path cannot inherit or conceal the failed burden.

```text
new alternative claim
= new testable claim

later COMPOSE success
≠ retroactive branch-status repair

later PROJECT_AS success
≠ retroactive availability warrant
```

The chapter-level result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known local Chapter-13 repair requirement. It preserves later pressure from Chapter 14 Non-Event discipline, Chapter 15 complete `COMPOSE`, Chapter 16 PATH Boundary Conditions, Chapter 17 integrated PATH cases and audit, Chapter 46 Counterfactual Sensitivity, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, Appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and later Reader graph implementation.

```text
Chapter 13 provisional lock
≠ historical possibility proof
≠ counterfactual truth
≠ prediction
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

Reopen Chapter 13 only if later work demonstrates a concrete conflict involving historical availability, temporal windows, Branch Point threshold, realized/rejected/blocked/aborted/deferred/lost distinction, status/mechanism separation, continuation identity, counterfactual horizon, Non-Selection burden, Alternative-Space Compression, five-part Loss, Alternative Status Record ownership, Failure/Reduction route, Stop/Non-Capture route, Chapter-14/15/16/17/46 handoff, RETYPE separation, or unchanged PMS Base. Mere preference for more branches, a fuller graph, a stronger counterfactual, a cleaner status, a more decisive conclusion, or a larger alternative field is not a reopening ground.

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 14 — Preparation:
Non-Events within Paths and Trajectories
```

<a id="chapter-14-non-events-within-paths-and-trajectories"></a>

## 14. Non-Events within Paths and Trajectories

Chapter 13 established that a realized Path becomes selective only relative to historically available continuations. It also established that a bounded Non-Selection finding may matter to a Path without thereby receiving automatic `Λ` typing. Chapter 14 now asks the stronger and more specific question:

> When does a non-realization become a path-forming Non-Event, and how must its frame, temporal bound, source basis, positive sub-events, and practical load be reconstructed?

A Path does not consist only of realized events. A transition may remain unrealized, a review may fail to occur, a release may not be completed, a decision window may close without disposition, or an expected repair may remain outstanding. Such non-realizations can preserve an earlier configuration, redistribute cost and exposure, alter later alternatives, accumulate residue, or change the meaning of subsequent events.

But STRATA cannot infer a Non-Event from blank space. The governing threshold remains conjunctive:

```text
identifiable expected occurrence
+ warranted expectation relation
+ declared expectation frame
+ bounded expected window or condition
+ source-supported non-realization
+ praxeological load
+ relevance to the temporal claim
→ PATH-specific Non-Event candidate
```

Each burden is necessary. More duration, stronger narrative emphasis, greater visual salience, formal record completeness, or a later adverse outcome cannot compensate for a missing expectation relation, an undefined window, inadequate source support, or absent praxeological load.

```text
nothing happened
≠ path-forming Non-Event
```

Chapter 3 retains the foundational definition of the Non-Event object. Chapter 14 does not create a second definition or a new primitive. It operationalizes the stronger PATH burden: how a warranted `Λ` occurrence enters a Path or Trajectory, how its expected structure remains visible, how realized sub-events may coexist with the governing non-realization, and how delay becomes structurally consequential without importing intention, refusal, blame, duty, motive, or prediction.

Sections 14.1–14.3 establish:

- why `Λ` is indispensable to PATH while ordinary absence remains inadmissible;
- the expectation relation, expectation frame, expected occurrence, and expected window;
- source-supported non-realization and the separation from missing information;
- frame-sensitive comparison of identical surface absences;
- Delay as a Non-Event without erasing positive postponement or coordination events;
- praxeological load, changed alternatives, and bounded later relevance;
- four WP1 Pressure Cases.

Sections 14.4–14.8 later address repeated Non-Decision, Blocked Responsibility, Missing Repair, Missing Exit, and Non-Event Sedimentation. Sections 14.9–14.11 later establish preservation through composition, False Non-Event, the Minimal Non-Event Record, Failure, Claim Reduction, Stop, Non-Capture, and the schema/extension decision. Chapter 15 retains the complete `COMPOSE` procedure. Chapter 23 retains internal decomposition mechanics. Chapter 49 retains the general source-gap architecture.

<a id="14-1-why-λ-is-central-to-path"></a>

### 14.1 Why `Λ` Is Central to PATH

A Path is a source-supported, selective temporal formation composed of configurations, events, Non-Events, transitions, and their material ordering. If only realized events are retained, the reconstruction can mistake continuation for active reproduction, silence for neutrality, an unresolved commitment for completed coordination, or the persistence of an earlier configuration for evidence that no transition was expected.

`Λ` is central to PATH because a warranted non-realization can perform temporal work. It can:

- leave an expected transition incomplete;
- preserve an earlier configuration beyond its expected review or replacement point;
- shift coordination, evidentiary, financial, or exposure costs;
- hold one alternative open while weakening another;
- close an availability window without a positive selection event;
- preserve an asymmetry that an expected review was meant to reconsider;
- prolong or deepen a binding relation;
- accumulate residue that later events must inherit;
- alter the interpretation of subsequent action;
- contribute to a Trajectory where the non-realization remains historically active.

None of these effects follows merely from absence. The analysis must show the relation between the expected occurrence, the bounded non-realization, and the claimed Path difference.

```text
absence named
≠ temporal work established
```

#### The object-level and Path-level burdens

Chapter 3 established the minimum object-level burden for a Non-Event. Chapter 14 adds a Path-level burden. A warranted local `Λ` occurrence may exist without being central to the Path under review. To become path-forming, the non-realization must matter to at least one declared relation such as:

- transition completion or failure;
- configuration persistence;
- alternative availability or closure;
- role distribution or authorization;
- cost, exposure, or evidentiary burden;
- binding, reliance, or release;
- residue and later repair burden;
- later event meaning;
- Path segmentation or periodization;
- Trajectory sedimentation.

The distinction is:

```text
warranted local Non-Event
≠ path-forming Non-Event automatically
```

A promised review may fail within its window and therefore qualify locally as `Λ`. If the review was peripheral to the declared Path and changed no relevant transition, alternative, cost, role, or later interpretation, it may remain below the Praxeological Relevance Floor for that Path claim. Conversely, a single bounded non-realization can be central where it preserves the governing configuration or closes a material alternative.

#### Positive events do not automatically eliminate the governing Non-Event

A Non-Event interval is not necessarily empty. It may contain:

- reminders;
- acknowledgements;
- scheduling messages;
- interim reports;
- partial approvals;
- rescheduling decisions;
- resource transfers;
- temporary workarounds;
- renewed commitments;
- other realized events.

Those positive events must be recorded where material. They may explain how the expectation persisted, how the window changed, or how costs accumulated. They do not realize the expected occurrence merely because activity took place.

```text
activity within the interval
≠ realization of the governing expected occurrence
```

This is a granularity-sensitive distinction. At a fine granularity, the interval may contain several events. At the governing Path granularity, the expected review, release, repair, or disposition may remain unrealized. Finer detail neither erases the higher-level Non-Event nor receives automatic truth priority. The analyst must preserve both where both are warranted.

#### Non-Event relevance without intention or blame

A path-forming Non-Event may arise through:

- distributed procedural failure;
- unresolved authority;
- resource shortage;
- competing commitments;
- an unfilled role;
- deadline interaction;
- repeated deferral;
- structural blockage;
- a deliberate refusal independently supported by evidence;
- or an unresolved combination.

The `Λ` finding itself does not determine which of these mechanisms occurred. It does not establish intention, negligence, guilt, legitimacy, duty, motive, or legal responsibility.

```text
warranted non-realization
≠ hidden intention
≠ person verdict
```

Where agency or responsibility is independently supported, it may be stated under its own source and claim ceiling. It cannot be inherited from Non-Event status.

#### `Λ`, `Θ`, and later profile interaction

`Λ` becomes temporally extended through relation with `Θ`, but the operators remain distinct occurrence types.

```text
Λ + Θ
≠ fused operator
```

Temporal extension may preserve the duration, repetition, or later consequences of a Non-Event. Possible interactions with `Α`, `Ω`, and `Ψ` may later support claims about stabilization, unequal load, or binding persistence. Those interactions require their own occurrence-level warrants. A Non-Event does not automatically establish an Attractor, Asymmetry, Binding, Trajectory, or Path Dependence.

```text
local Λ
≠ sedimentation automatically
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

#### Relevance Floor, Traceability Ceiling, and Loss

A Non-Event falls below the Praxeological Relevance Floor where it can be named but changes no warranted Path relation. It rises above the Traceability Ceiling where the analyst attributes broad historical effects without preserving the expected occurrence, frame, window, source basis, and intermediate load.

A controlled reconstruction must disclose:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

For a Non-Event, relevant Loss questions include:

- Was the expected occurrence preserved or replaced by vague language such as “nothing happened”?
- Was the expectation relation retained?
- Was the original window preserved, compressed, or retrospectively redrawn?
- Were positive sub-events omitted?
- Was source uncertainty hidden?
- Were affected alternatives or residues compressed?
- Did the reconstruction convert non-realization into a positive-event summary?

Chapter 14 prepares these preservation duties. Chapter 15 owns their application inside a completed `COMPOSE` occurrence.

#### C14-CENT-01 — expected review remains unrealized while scheduling activity occurs

A public research consortium adopts a data-access rule on 3 February. The adoption minutes include a written commitment to conduct a formal review within sixty days and to issue either a confirmed rule, a modified rule, or a written extension decision.

During the sixty-day interval:

- the secretariat sends two scheduling polls;
- three members submit review memoranda;
- the chair acknowledges that review remains due;
- one proposed meeting is cancelled because quorum is unavailable;
- a new tentative date is circulated after the original deadline.

The archive contains complete meeting calendars, formal dispositions, and rule registers for the interval. No review meeting, confirmed rule, modified rule, or written extension decision occurs within sixty days. The original access rule remains operative. External researchers continue to bear the additional approval burden that the review was expected to reconsider.

The reconstruction is:

```text
expected occurrence:
formal review and disposition within sixty days

expectation relation:
written commitment in the adoption minutes

expectation frame:
consortium rule-review procedure

expected window:
3 February through 4 April

positive sub-events:
scheduling polls, memoranda, acknowledgement, cancellation, tentative rescheduling

source-supported non-realization:
complete calendar, disposition, and rule records show no governing review or disposition within the window

praxeological load:
original access rule remains operative;
approval costs persist;
the reconsideration commitment becomes residue;
later review begins from a changed evidentiary and coordination position
```

The positive scheduling events are preserved. They do not realize the governing expected review. The Non-Event is central to the Path because the rule persists beyond the committed review point and the alternative of timely reconsideration closes for that window.

A bounded local result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. It does not establish why quorum failed, whether any participant intended delay, whether the rule was illegitimate, or whether the later review will change it.

### 14.2 Frame-Dependence of Non-Events

A Non-Event exists only relative to a warranted expectation structure. The same surface absence may qualify as `Λ` under one frame and remain ordinary absence under another. Chapter 14 therefore requires the expectation frame to be declared rather than inferred from the analyst's sense that something should have happened.

```text
same observed absence
+ different expectation frames
→ potentially different Non-Event findings
```

This does not make `Λ` arbitrary. The frame must be source-supported, relevant to the reference object, and bounded by the claim. A frame cannot be selected merely because it produces the desired Non-Event.

#### Expected occurrence

The record must state what occurrence was expected. Suitable descriptions identify a transition, decision, review, repair, response, release, exit, delivery, authorization, or other bounded occurrence.

Insufficient descriptions include:

- “nothing happened”;
- “there was silence”;
- “the institution did not improve”;
- “the person failed to act appropriately”;
- “the expected outcome did not occur” without specifying the occurrence;
- “an alternative was not chosen” without an active decision context.

The expected occurrence must be specific enough to distinguish realization, partial realization, substitution, extension, and non-realization.

#### Warranted expectation relation

An expectation relation may arise from:

- an explicit promise or commitment;
- a rule or procedure;
- a calendar, deadline, or schedule;
- a declared role responsibility;
- a recurrent institutional practice;
- an adopted plan;
- a condition-triggered review, repair, release, or exit process;
- another source-supported relation that makes occurrence due or conditionally due.

The relation must be distinguished from:

- analyst preference;
- moral intuition;
- retrospective surprise;
- statistical frequency alone;
- general desirability;
- a merely conceivable continuation;
- a later rule projected backward;
- a role stereotype not grounded in the relevant frame.

```text
analyst expectation
≠ warranted expectation relation
```

Frequency can support an expectation only where the recurrent practice itself is source-supported and relevant. Even then, recurrence does not establish a normatively binding duty or hidden intention.

#### Expectation frame

The **expectation frame** is the declared contextual structure within which the expected occurrence becomes due, meaningful, or conditionally required. It may be:

- institutional;
- procedural;
- contractual;
- relational;
- technical;
- operational;
- role-specific;
- or another source-supported frame.

A single reference object may support more than one frame. The analysis must therefore identify which frame carries the expectation and which frames do not.

For example, a weekly coordination call may be expected under a project-governance frame but not under an informal social frame. The absence of a call may be `Λ` in the former and ordinary absence in the latter. These are not contradictory claims if the frames and reference relations differ. If both claims concern the same bounded procedural expectation, however, frame plurality cannot be used to neutralize a real contradiction.

#### Expected window or realization condition

A warranted expectation requires a bounded realization condition. The window may be:

- a fixed date or interval;
- a recurring decision window;
- a deadline;
- a condition-triggered interval;
- a bounded sequence position;
- a phase-completion condition;
- another source-supported temporal or procedural bound.

An open-ended possibility is insufficient.

```text
not yet realized
≠ failed to realize within the expected window
```

The window must also be historically appropriate. A deadline introduced later cannot be projected backward. A frame change may terminate, replace, extend, translate, or leave uncertain the earlier expectation. It does not silently preserve the old window.

Where the window remains open at the analytical cut, the correct finding is ordinarily pending, unresolved, or no completed Non-Event claim—not a completed non-realization.

#### Window extension, replacement, and contested bounds

An expected window may change. The analyst must distinguish:

- **extension** — the same expectation remains operative under a source-supported later deadline;
- **replacement** — a new expectation supersedes the earlier one;
- **conditional suspension** — the expectation is paused until a declared condition occurs;
- **contested window** — sources disagree about when realization was due;
- **expired window with renewed expectation** — the first Non-Event may be complete while a new expectation begins.

```text
new deadline
≠ retroactive erasure of missed earlier window automatically
```

A valid extension issued before the original deadline may prevent a Non-Event at that cut. An extension issued after the deadline may coexist with an already warranted missed-window Non-Event. The substantive classification depends on the source-supported relation and timing, not the label “extended.”

#### Source-supported non-realization

Non-realization must be supported within the declared bound. Relevant source structures may include:

- complete decision logs;
- calendars and meeting registers;
- disposition or completion records;
- signed delivery or release records;
- verified absence from an otherwise complete event register;
- procedural audit trails;
- system logs with known coverage;
- another source bundle capable of establishing bounded non-occurrence.

Insufficient by themselves are:

- no document found;
- an incomplete archive;
- silence in one channel;
- endpoint difference;
- later recollection without adequate chronology;
- the analyst's inability to identify the event;
- a gap in a graph or timeline.

```text
no record found
≠ record of non-occurrence
```

The source claim must state why the bundle is capable of supporting non-realization. Completeness is itself a bounded claim. A complete formal-decision register may establish that no formal disposition occurred while remaining unable to establish whether informal discussions occurred. The Non-Event claim must stay at the supported granularity.

#### Missing information and unknown occurrence

Three states must remain separate:

```text
source-supported non-realization
missing information
unknown occurrence status
```

Missing information identifies a source limitation. Unknown occurrence status states that the available sources cannot determine realization or non-realization. Neither state is `Λ` by itself.

Where the distinction is material and cannot be resolved, the analysis may require:

- Claim Reduction;
- `resolution_neutral` for a genuinely resolution-neutral transformation issue;
- `mandatory_stop` where a stronger unsupported claim is knowingly reused;
- `non_capture` where the structured expectation problem is material but cannot be responsibly captured by a weaker affirmative claim.

No route may protect an unsupported Non-Event claim merely because the story appears coherent.

#### C14-FRAME-01 — same surface silence, different expectation frames

A regional planning office maintains two communication channels with an independent research group:

1. a contractual data-delivery channel requiring written confirmation within five working days after each transfer;
2. an optional informal discussion channel used irregularly for non-binding commentary.

On 8 June the research group sends a data package through the contractual channel and separately posts an informal question in the discussion channel. No reply appears in either channel during the next five working days.

Under the contractual delivery frame:

```text
expected occurrence:
written receipt confirmation

expectation relation:
explicit five-working-day clause

expected window:
8–15 June

source support:
complete contractual correspondence log contains no confirmation

praxeological load:
downstream validation cannot formally begin;
responsibility for data integrity remains unresolved
```

A bounded Non-Event candidate is supported.

Under the informal discussion frame:

- no response deadline exists;
- replies are irregular;
- no adopted practice makes response due;
- no source-supported Path consequence follows from the silence alone.

The same surface absence therefore does not support a second `Λ` occurrence.

```text
silence in contractual frame
→ bounded Non-Event candidate

silence in optional informal frame
→ ordinary absence on current evidence
```

The result does not rank the frames or infer discourtesy, motive, breach, or blame. It demonstrates that Non-Event status depends on a warranted expectation relation rather than visual or linguistic similarity.

A suitable local mapping is `partially_admissible` if the initial claim treated both silences as equivalent; after segmentation, the contractual claim may be `admissible_with_bounded_claim` while the informal claim is reduced.

#### C14-SOURCE-01 — incomplete archive cannot establish non-realization

A later report states that an oversight committee “never reviewed” a procurement exception during a six-month period. The surviving archive contains:

- minutes for two of six scheduled meetings;
- an incomplete email export;
- no complete decision register;
- a later participant recollection that the matter was “probably deferred.”

The governing procedure did require review within the six-month period, so the expected occurrence, frame, and window are identifiable. But the source bundle cannot establish that no formal or written review occurred. The absence of a surviving review record may reflect non-realization, lost files, an unarchived written disposition, or incomplete collection.

The warranted reconstruction is:

```text
expectation supported
+ expected window supported
+ occurrence status unresolved
≠ established Non-Event
```

The strong claim must be reduced to a documented expected review with unresolved realization status. If the later report uses the alleged non-review to attribute blame, invalidate decisions, impose sanctions, or establish a Path-Dependence claim, continuation requires `mandatory_stop` until the source burden is repaired. Where the expectation problem is materially central and the archive cannot distinguish non-realization from source loss, `non_capture` may remain available.

### 14.3 Delay as Non-Event

Delay may qualify as `Λ` where an expected transition does not occur within a warranted window and the missed window changes a material Path relation. Delay is not established merely because an occurrence happens later than the analyst prefers, takes longer than comparable cases, or is described retrospectively as slow.

```text
expected transition
+ warranted time bound
+ source-supported non-realization within that bound
+ material temporal load
→ Delay-as-Non-Event candidate
```

#### Delay as `Λ` versus delay as observed event chain

The same interval may support two distinct analytical descriptions:

1. **positive delay events** — postponement notices, rescheduling decisions, partial approvals, interim work, or deadline extensions that actually occurred;
2. **governing Non-Event** — the expected transition remained unrealized within the relevant bound.

```text
Delay as Non-Event
≠ observed sequence of postponement events
```

These descriptions may coexist. The positive events explain how the delay was organized, acknowledged, or extended. The `Λ` claim identifies what failed to occur within the warranted window and what that non-realization changed.

The analyst must not convert the interval into an empty gap. Nor may the positive events be used to imply that the governing transition occurred.

#### Minimum delay declaration

A controlled Delay-as-Non-Event claim should declare:

```yaml
delay_non_event:
  reference_path:
  expected_transition:
  expectation_relation:
  expectation_frame:
  original_expected_window:
  window_status:
  positive_sub_events:
  non_realization_support:
  accumulated_load:
  affected_roles:
  affected_alternatives:
  later_reachability:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
  output_class_mapping:
```

This is a chapter-level declaration view, not a new Shared-Record schema and not an Output Class. Field completeness does not prove delay, expectation, or `Λ` status.

#### Window status

The delay claim must state whether the original window:

- remained open;
- expired;
- was validly extended before expiry;
- was extended after expiry;
- was replaced by a new window;
- became contested;
- closed an alternative;
- or cannot be reconstructed.

This matters because different temporal relations support different claims. A timely, authorized extension may mean the transition was not yet due. A post-deadline rescheduling may preserve the original missed-window Non-Event while creating a new expected window. An indefinite postponement may terminate the original expectation or leave it unresolved depending on the frame.

#### Material temporal load

Delay becomes path-forming where the missed window changes one or more of:

- coordination cost;
- resource commitment;
- role exposure;
- evidentiary burden;
- reliance or binding;
- availability of an alternative;
- sequence order;
- later transition conditions;
- residue;
- action-corridor accessibility.

A later successful occurrence does not automatically erase those effects.

```text
later realization
≠ no earlier Delay Non-Event
```

The later occurrence may:

- repair some consequences;
- leave accumulated costs intact;
- realize a modified rather than identical transition;
- occur under a new frame;
- reopen only part of the earlier alternative space;
- or establish that the original delay was temporary rather than permanent.

These are separate testable claims.

#### Delay without intention

Delay may be intentional, accidental, distributed, emergent, structurally blocked, or unresolved. `Λ` status does not select among these explanations.

```text
delayed expected occurrence
≠ intentional delay
```

Where the sources independently establish an intentional postponement decision, that positive event may be recorded alongside the governing Non-Event. The analysis still may not infer blame, legitimacy, or sanction authority from intention alone.

#### Delay, alternatives, and Chapter 13

Chapter 13 distinguishes a Deferred Branch from uninterrupted continuation. Chapter 14 addresses a different claim: whether an expected occurrence failed within its warranted window and thereby carried `Λ` load.

```text
Deferred Branch
≠ Delay Non-Event automatically
```

A branch may be formally deferred through a realized postponement decision without an independently warranted missed-window Non-Event. Conversely, a transition may become a Delay Non-Event even where no formal branch-status record exists. Where both apply, they must be recorded separately and linked through the same reference Path, historical window, and source structure.

#### Delay, open windows, and premature claims

If the warranted window remains open, non-realization is ordinarily incomplete. The analyst may record:

- pending expectation;
- elapsed time;
- interim events;
- emerging costs;
- uncertainty;
- risk that alternatives may close.

But a completed `Λ` claim requires the bounded realization condition to have failed or another source-supported condition to establish non-realization.

```text
increasing concern before deadline
≠ completed Delay Non-Event
```

This protects analysis from converting anticipation into historical fact.

#### C14-DELAY-01 — missed authorization window changes costs and alternatives

A cooperative energy project receives conditional engineering approval on 10 January. Under the funding agreement, the regional authority is expected to issue the final grid-connection authorization within forty-five days after receipt of the certified design. The complete submission is acknowledged on 12 January, making 26 February the governing deadline.

During the window:

- the authority requests one clarification that the cooperative answers within two days;
- an internal technical note confirms that the file is complete;
- two coordination calls occur;
- no final authorization, refusal, or valid extension is issued by 26 February.

The authorization arrives on 20 April. By then:

- the original equipment reservation has expired;
- the cooperative must accept a higher-priced replacement order;
- the planned spring installation window is unavailable;
- a temporary financing commitment must be renewed;
- one participating site withdraws from the first implementation phase.

The reconstruction is:

```text
expected transition:
final grid-connection authorization

expectation relation:
forty-five-day funding-agreement procedure

expected window:
12 January through 26 February

positive sub-events:
clarification request, response, completeness note, coordination calls

source-supported non-realization:
complete authorization register and file history show no disposition or valid extension by deadline

material load:
equipment cost increase;
installation-window loss;
financing renewal;
changed first-phase participation;
modified later continuation
```

The April authorization does not erase the February missed-window Non-Event. It establishes later realization under changed costs, timing, and participation. The claim does not establish intentional obstruction, legal breach, person blame, or that the earlier implementation would certainly have succeeded.

A bounded local result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 14.1–14.3 now canonically establish:

- `Λ` as indispensable to PATH where a source-supported non-realization changes a transition, configuration, alternative, cost, role, binding, residue, later meaning, or temporal continuation;
- strict separation of path-forming Non-Event from ordinary absence, missing information, unknown occurrence, analyst surprise, and merely conceivable alternatives;
- the stronger PATH burden beyond the foundational Chapter-3 object definition without redefining the object or creating a new primitive;
- the conjunctive and non-compensatory chain of identifiable expected occurrence, warranted expectation relation, declared frame, bounded window, source-supported non-realization, praxeological load, and temporal-chain relevance;
- positive events and positive sub-events as compatible with a governing higher-level Non-Event;
- granularity-sensitive coexistence without finer-resolution truth priority;
- expectation relations grounded in commitment, rule, procedure, schedule, role, recurrent practice, plan, or triggered condition rather than analyst preference, hindsight, desirability, or frequency alone;
- frame-dependent Non-Event status without analytical arbitrariness or contradiction laundering;
- fixed, recurring, deadline-based, condition-triggered, sequence-position, and phase-completion windows;
- extension, replacement, suspension, contestation, expiry, and renewed expectation as distinct temporal relations;
- source-supported non-realization and the strict rule that missing records do not prove non-occurrence;
- source-coverage claims bounded to the supported granularity;
- Delay as a Non-Event distinguished from observed postponement-event chains;
- original-window status, positive sub-events, accumulated load, changed alternatives, later reachability, and uncertainty as required delay burdens;
- later realization without automatic erasure of the earlier missed-window Non-Event;
- delay without automatic intention, blame, legitimacy, sanction, or authority inference;
- Deferred Branch and Delay Non-Event as distinct claims that may coexist but do not entail each other;
- all four assigned WP1 Pressure Cases without presenting them as produced Case files or empirical evidence.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP1 repair requirement. It preserves the remaining Chapter-14 duties:

- WP2 Repeated Non-Decision, Blocked Responsibility, Missing Repair, Missing Exit, Non-Event Sedimentation, and seven assigned Pressure Cases;
- WP3 preservation through composition, False Non-Event, Minimal Non-Event Record, schema/extension decision, Failure, Claim Reduction, Stop, Non-Capture, and eight assigned Pressure Cases;
- WP4 integrated audit and Provisional Lock;
- later Chapter-15 `COMPOSE` execution;
- later Chapter-23 internal Non-Event decomposition;
- later Chapter-49 source-limit architecture;
- later case, appendix, model-finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP1 completion
≠ Chapter 14 provisional lock
≠ Non-Event sedimentation automatically
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ completed DECOMPOSE occurrence
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ person attribution
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 14 — WP2:
Repeated Non-Decision,
Blocked Responsibility,
Missing Repair,
Missing Exit,
and Non-Event Sedimentation
```

<a id="14-4-repeated-non-decision"></a>

### 14.4 Repeated Non-Decision

A single unrealized decision window and a repeated pattern of Non-Decision are not the same analytical object. Repeated Non-Decision requires more than elapsed time, unresolved controversy, or a long archive in which no final decision is visible. It requires a sequence of renewed or recurring decision contexts in which a source-supported expected decision repeatedly fails to occur.

```text
one prolonged unresolved window
≠ repeated Non-Decision automatically

repeated missing documentation
≠ repeated Non-Decision
```

The distinction matters because repeated Non-Decision may acquire temporal load that a single missed window does not carry. Each renewed context can preserve or increase coordination burdens, defer reallocations, stabilize expectations of continued non-resolution, reinforce a default continuation, distribute costs asymmetrically, or narrow later alternatives. These effects must be reconstructed rather than inferred from repetition alone.

#### Recurring or renewed decision contexts

A repeated Non-Decision claim must identify the decision contexts that recur or are renewed. Such contexts may be generated by:

- recurring agenda slots;
- periodic review cycles;
- repeated submission or renewal windows;
- successive escalation stages;
- recurring authorization rounds;
- reopened negotiation or remediation procedures;
- repeated triggering conditions;
- separate phase gates that require equivalent dispositions.

Each context must be bounded enough to answer:

- what decision was expected;
- under which frame and relation;
- during which window or condition;
- what positive events occurred;
- whether the decision remained unrealized;
- whether the next context renewed the same expectation or created a materially new one.

```text
similar agenda label
≠ renewed identical expectation automatically
```

The same nominal agenda item may carry a different frame, decision scope, authorized actors, evidentiary record, or consequence in each recurrence. Conversely, differently named agenda items may instantiate the same governing expectation. The reconstruction therefore follows the expectation relation and reference object, not labels alone.

#### One prolonged window versus repeated windows

A prolonged window remains one Non-Event occurrence where the same expectation, frame, realization condition, and temporal bound remain continuously open or unresolved. Repeated Non-Decision requires distinct renewed or recurring decision opportunities.

A controlled record should distinguish:

```yaml
repeated_non_decision:
  reference_path:
  expected_decision:
  expectation_relation:
  expectation_frame:
  decision_contexts:
    - context_id:
      window_or_condition:
      positive_events:
      non_realization_support:
      closure_or_renewal_status:
  continuity_or_change_between_contexts:
  accumulated_load:
  affected_roles:
  affected_alternatives:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
  output_class_mapping:
```

This is a chapter-level declaration view. It does not create a new schema, status enumeration, or Output Class. A complete list of decision contexts does not prove that each context contained a warranted decision expectation.

#### Non-Decision versus refusal

Refusal is a positive disposition where a source-supported actor or authorized configuration rejects, declines, or denies a specified continuation. Non-Decision is the failure to produce the expected decision within the governing context.

```text
no affirmative decision
≠ refusal

no final disposition
≠ rejection
```

A meeting may contain disagreement, requests for further information, procedural motions, deferrals, or referrals without producing a refusal. If the sources establish an explicit rejection, the local structure is no longer merely Non-Decision for that window, even if later contexts reopen the issue.

The analyst must not convert silence, abstention, agenda removal, procedural referral, or missing minutes into refusal unless the sources independently support that disposition.

#### Repetition without automatic sedimentation

Repeated Non-Decision may create:

- an attractor toward continued provisional arrangements;
- asymmetrical exposure for roles that bear the costs of uncertainty;
- binding through repeated reliance on temporary solutions;
- residual evidentiary, technical, or procedural burdens;
- reduced credibility of future decision windows;
- closure of previously available alternatives.

But these effects are separate testable claims.

```text
repeated Non-Decision
≠ sedimentation automatically

repeated windows
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

The chapter must identify what later configuration carries the accumulated load. Where each window closes without material carryover, the pattern may remain a sequence of local `Λ` occurrences rather than a sedimented trajectory feature.

#### C14-NDEC-01 — recurring agenda windows close without decision

A regional data-governance board adopts an annual review procedure requiring a disposition on whether a temporary data-sharing protocol should be renewed, replaced, or terminated. The board calendar and charter establish one decision window each March. The temporary protocol remains operational only because an emergency continuation clause automatically extends it until a disposition is entered.

Across three annual cycles:

- the review is placed on the March agenda;
- the secretariat circulates the required briefing package;
- affected departments submit comments;
- members discuss scope, costs, and replacement options;
- the meeting closes without a renewal, replacement, termination, or valid transfer of the decision;
- the emergency continuation clause preserves the temporary protocol.

The minutes document discussion and procedural activity but no refusal. In the second and third cycles, departments postpone investments in a replacement architecture because the protocol's status remains unresolved. Two smaller departments continue to bear manual reconciliation costs that the larger departments can absorb through existing staff.

The reconstruction is:

```text
expected decision:
renew, replace, or terminate the temporary protocol

renewed contexts:
three independently bounded annual March review windows

positive events:
briefing circulation, comments, discussion, agenda handling

non-realization:
no disposition entered in any of the three windows

material carryover:
automatic continuation;
postponed replacement investment;
asymmetrical reconciliation cost;
reduced credibility of later review windows
```

This supports repeated Non-Decision. It does not establish refusal, bad faith, illegality, or that any individual member caused the pattern. Whether the pattern has become sedimented requires the separate Section 14.8 burden.

A bounded local result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

---

### 14.5 Blocked Responsibility

Blocked Responsibility describes a Non-Event configuration in which an expected action or decision remains unrealized because the relevant responsibility architecture cannot produce a terminating disposition. The block may be procedural, distributed, cyclic, mutually conditional, jurisdictionally divided, authorization-dependent, or structurally under-specified.

The object of analysis is the configuration, not a person.

```text
blocked responsibility
≠ individual blame
≠ diagnosis
≠ legal-duty determination
```

A role may be formally named while lacking the authority, information, resources, or downstream acceptance required to realize the expected occurrence. Conversely, several roles may each possess partial authority while no admissible sequence allows any one of them to complete the transition.

#### Responsibility architecture

A controlled reconstruction should distinguish:

- the expected occurrence;
- the roles or units involved;
- each role's declared authority and dependency;
- the referral or conditionality relations;
- the blocking configuration;
- the expected window;
- positive attempts or referrals;
- source-supported non-realization;
- affected alternatives and accumulated load;
- unresolved agency or causal questions.

Typical blocking structures include:

```text
A may act only after B certifies
B may certify only after C allocates
C may allocate only after A submits final scope
```

or:

```text
local unit has operational knowledge but no authorization
central unit has authorization but no evidentiary basis
review unit can validate but cannot initiate
```

Such structures may generate a Non-Event even where every participant performs locally permitted actions. The absence of a terminating occurrence may be configuration-level rather than attributable to one role.

#### Structural block versus refusal or incapacity

Blocked Responsibility must remain distinct from:

- an explicit refusal by an authorized actor;
- individual incapacity;
- simple workload delay;
- missing documentation;
- absence of an expectation relation;
- an open window that has not yet failed;
- a branch that was never practically available.

```text
responsibility distributed
≠ responsibility blocked automatically
```

Distribution becomes analytically relevant only where the relations among roles prevent or materially obstruct realization of the expected occurrence.

If one authorized role could complete the action but does not, the record may support a local Non-Decision or other `Λ` claim. It does not automatically support a structural blockage claim. If the sources cannot distinguish refusal, incapacity, workload, or configuration-level blockage, the appropriate result may be `non_capture` or `claim_reduction_required`.

#### Blocking without authority inheritance

STRATA may reconstruct that a configuration failed to generate a due disposition. It may not use that finding to infer:

- who ought legally to have acted;
- who is morally responsible;
- whether a sanction is justified;
- whether a person lacked competence or motivation;
- whether the arrangement was legitimate;
- what intervention must follow.

These claims require independent authority and method.

```text
praxeological trace of blockage
≠ sanction authority
```

#### C14-BRESP-01 — distributed responsibility blocks a due disposition

A municipal housing-repair program requires a final disposition on emergency structural work within ten business days after an engineer marks a building as eligible. The engineer marks one building eligible and uploads the complete report.

The governing procedure distributes responsibility as follows:

- the district office may issue a work order only after the central procurement unit confirms the contracting route;
- procurement may confirm the route only after the finance unit reserves funds;
- finance may reserve funds only after the district office enters a final scope code;
- the district office cannot enter the final scope code until procurement identifies the contracting route.

During the ten-day window:

- the engineer answers all technical questions;
- the district office requests a route determination;
- procurement requests a funding reservation;
- finance requests the final scope code;
- each request is documented;
- no work order, route confirmation, funding reservation, or valid extension is produced.

Temporary protective measures continue, increasing relocation costs and extending resident displacement.

The reconstruction is:

```text
expected occurrence:
final emergency-work disposition

blocking structure:
cyclic dependency among district scope, procurement route, and funding reservation

positive events:
technical clarification and documented inter-unit referrals

source-supported non-realization:
no terminating disposition within the ten-day window

praxeological load:
extended temporary measures;
additional relocation cost;
continued resident displacement;
reduced repair alternatives
```

The finding supports a structurally blocked responsibility configuration. It does not establish that any named person refused, acted in bad faith, violated law, or deserves sanction.

A bounded local result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

---

### 14.6 Missing Repair

Missing Repair is a PATH-specific Non-Event candidate where a warranted integration, correction, remediation, reconciliation, restoration, review, or other repair occurrence fails within its expected frame and window, and the non-realization leaves a material residue or changes later praxis.

A defect, conflict, incompatibility, or harm does not establish a repair expectation by itself.

```text
defect observed
≠ repair due

repair desirable
≠ repair expected
```

The expectation must be grounded in a commitment, adopted plan, assigned role, procedure, schedule, triggered condition, recurrent practice, settlement, or other source-supported relation.

#### Repair object and completion condition

The analyst must declare what would count as the repair occurrence. A vague expectation that matters would be "resolved" is insufficient. The expected occurrence may be:

- a reconciliation entry;
- a corrective release;
- an integration review;
- a safety remediation;
- a data correction;
- a formal restoration step;
- a compensating procedure;
- a documented closure decision;
- a release from a temporary workaround.

The completion condition must distinguish:

```text
repair initiated
≠ repair completed

partial mitigation
≠ full reconciliation automatically

new workaround
≠ repair of the original residue
```

Positive repair attempts, partial fixes, interim safeguards, or diagnostic work may coexist with a governing Missing Repair Non-Event.

#### Residue and later readability

Missing Repair becomes path-forming where the unrepaired structure remains active in later:

- evidentiary burden;
- technical incompatibility;
- role allocation;
- cost distribution;
- reliance or binding;
- interpretation of later events;
- availability of exit or replacement;
- action-corridor accessibility.

A later event may become legible only relative to the unrepaired residue. For example, a later dispute may not be an isolated conflict if it reactivates an unresolved reconciliation burden from an earlier missed repair window.

```text
later disturbance
≠ proof of earlier Missing Repair
```

The earlier expectation, non-realization, and carryover must still be independently supported.

#### Repair, blame, and normativity

A Missing Repair finding identifies an unrealized expected occurrence and its praxis load. It does not establish:

- that a person intentionally withheld repair;
- that repair was legally required;
- that the original condition was wrongful;
- that the repair would have succeeded;
- that later costs were caused exclusively by the missing repair;
- that a specific remedy is now authorized.

Where the source record supports only a defect and later residue, but not a warranted repair expectation, the claim must be reduced.

#### C14-REPAIR-01 — committed reconciliation fails and leaves residue

Two hospital systems merge their patient-index services under a signed transition plan. The plan requires a reconciliation review within thirty days after the first combined data load. The review must identify duplicate records, assign resolution owners, and approve a closure report before the next migration phase.

The first combined load occurs on 3 April. During the following thirty days:

- automated duplicate reports are generated;
- three technical meetings identify unresolved patient identifiers;
- temporary manual flags are added;
- no reconciliation review is convened;
- no resolution owners are assigned;
- no closure report is approved;
- the second migration phase begins under a temporary exception.

Over the next six months:

- staff maintain parallel lookup procedures;
- several later corrections require reconstructing which source index controlled at the time;
- smaller clinics bear higher manual verification costs;
- the temporary exception becomes the default continuation.

The reconstruction is:

```text
expected repair:
reconciliation review, owner assignment, and closure report

expectation relation:
signed transition plan triggered by first combined load

window:
thirty days from 3 April

positive sub-events:
duplicate reports, technical meetings, manual flags

non-realization:
no review, owner assignment, or closure report within the window

residual load:
parallel lookup procedures;
manual verification cost;
evidentiary uncertainty;
temporary-exception continuation
```

This supports Missing Repair with bounded sedimentation pressure. It does not establish negligence, legal liability, or that a completed review would have eliminated every later error.

A bounded local result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

---

### 14.7 Missing Exit

Missing Exit is a Non-Event candidate where an expected, triggered, announced, contractually due, procedurally required, or otherwise warranted exit or release occurrence fails within a bounded realization condition and the non-realization materially changes the Path.

The mere existence of an exit option is insufficient.

```text
formal exit available
≠ exit expected

remaining in place
≠ Missing Exit automatically
```

An exit may concern:

- release from a temporary arrangement;
- termination of an emergency status;
- migration from a legacy system;
- handoff from an interim role;
- closure of a provisional program;
- withdrawal from a triggered commitment;
- decommissioning after replacement;
- transfer out of a restricted procedure;
- expiration of a temporary exception.

#### Expected exit occurrence

The analysis must identify:

- what occurrence would count as exit or release;
- what triggered or warranted it;
- the responsible configuration or procedure;
- the relevant window or condition;
- whether the exit path remained practically available;
- positive steps toward or away from exit;
- source-supported non-realization;
- the load carried by continuation.

A statement that exit was permitted does not show that exit was due. A stated desire to leave may support an expectation only where it is connected to a warranted process or commitment. A later analyst's view that exit would have been preferable is not an expectation relation.

#### Missing Exit without motive inference

The same surface continuation can be consistent with different underlying structures:

- the release procedure failed;
- authorization was blocked;
- switching costs increased;
- the exit window expired;
- the role remained necessary under a new frame;
- the actor withdrew the request;
- records are incomplete;
- the exit was never expected.

```text
exit did not occur
≠ motive known
≠ coercion established
≠ voluntary continuation established
```

PATH reconstructs the supported structure of the unrealized exit. It does not infer internal desire, consent quality, fear, incapacity, coercion, loyalty, or rationality without independent evidence and authority.

#### Exit, attractors, asymmetries, and bindings

A Missing Exit may interact with:

- `Α + Θ` where continued participation becomes the default corridor;
- `Ω + Θ` where exit costs are distributed unevenly;
- `Ψ + Θ` where reliance, commitments, or dependencies make release costly;
- `Λ + Θ` where repeated failures to complete exit preserve the continuation.

These are interactions, not fused operators or automatic explanations.

```text
Missing Exit
≠ attractor proof
≠ asymmetry proof
≠ binding proof
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

Each interaction requires its own occurrence support, present effect, counterpressure, uncertainty, and Claim Ceiling.

#### C14-EXIT-01 — triggered release process fails without motive inference

A regional emergency logistics consortium assigns one nonprofit warehouse as the temporary coordination hub during a six-month disruption. The signed emergency protocol states that once the replacement hub passes an operational-readiness audit, the temporary hub must be released within twenty business days and its reserved capacity returned to ordinary programs.

The replacement hub passes the audit on 1 September. During the next twenty business days:

- the consortium confirms the audit result;
- a transition checklist is opened;
- inventory owners submit transfer lists;
- two transfer meetings occur;
- no release certificate is issued;
- reserved capacity remains unavailable to the nonprofit's ordinary programs;
- no valid extension or cancellation of the release condition is recorded.

The temporary hub continues for four additional months. During that period, the nonprofit declines two ordinary-program deliveries because reserved space remains committed.

The reconstruction supports:

```text
expected exit:
release of the temporary coordination hub

trigger:
replacement-hub readiness audit

window:
twenty business days from 1 September

positive sub-events:
checklist, transfer lists, transition meetings

non-realization:
no release certificate or valid extension

material load:
continued reserved capacity;
lost ordinary-program deliveries;
extended temporary-role exposure
```

The claim does not establish why the nonprofit remained, whether it wanted to remain, whether the consortium coerced continuation, or whether any person intentionally blocked release.

A bounded local result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`.

---

### 14.8 Non-Event Sedimentation

Non-Event Sedimentation occurs where one or more warranted `Λ` occurrences remain structurally active in later meaning, cost, role distribution, alternatives, bindings, residue, or action corridors. It is the point at which a Non-Event is not merely an unrealized occurrence within one window but becomes load-bearing for later temporal organization.

```text
repetition
≠ sedimentation automatically

long duration
≠ trajectory-forming load automatically
```

The sedimentation claim must answer what later structure carries the earlier non-realization and how that carryover is traceable.

#### Occurrence architecture

The analyst must distinguish at least three possible formations:

1. **one prolonged Non-Event** — one expectation and realization condition remain unresolved across an extended interval;
2. **repeated Non-Events** — distinct renewed windows each produce a separate warranted non-realization;
3. **mixed formation** — an initial failed window is followed by renewed contexts, partial repairs, new expectations, or modified non-realizations.

```text
one long unresolved interval
≠ many occurrences automatically

many recorded dates
≠ many Non-Events automatically
```

Occurrence boundaries must follow the expectation relation, frame, window, and realization condition rather than calendar segmentation alone.

#### Sedimentation carrier

A Non-Event becomes sedimented only where its load remains present in a later configuration or transition. Possible carriers include:

- preserved temporary procedures;
- accumulated coordination routines;
- reliance on workarounds;
- unresolved evidentiary residue;
- redistributed role burden;
- increased switching or reopening costs;
- reduced availability of alternatives;
- changed credibility of future commitments;
- normalized provisional arrangements;
- narrowed action corridors.

A sedimentation record should declare:

```yaml
non_event_sedimentation:
  reference_path_or_trajectory:
  source_non_event_occurrences:
  occurrence_architecture:
  preserved_or_accumulated_load:
  later_carrier:
  changed_meaning_or_cost:
  affected_roles:
  affected_alternatives:
  action_corridor_effect:
  possible_operator_profile_interactions:
  current_state_rival:
  competing_constructions:
  loss:
    preserved:
    compressed:
    excluded:
    uncertain:
    irrecoverable:
  uncertainty:
  claim_scope:
  output_class_mapping:
```

This is a declaration view, not a new schema or proof engine.

#### Profile interaction without fusion

Non-Event Sedimentation may interact with the Chapter-11 and Chapter-12 profiles:

```text
Λ + Θ
→ repeated or persistent non-realization structure

Α + Θ
→ continued provisional state becomes easier or more expected

Ω + Θ
→ cost and exposure accumulate unevenly

Ψ + Θ
→ reliance and reopening burdens bind later continuation
```

These interactions may coexist, conflict, or remain only partially supported.

```text
profile interaction
≠ operator fusion
≠ universal causal chain
≠ additive sedimentation score
```

A rich profile does not compensate for an invalid `Λ` substrate. If the original expectation or non-realization is unwarranted, later cost, attraction, asymmetry, or binding cannot retroactively create the missing Non-Event.

#### Changed action corridors

Sedimentation is especially visible where later action corridors change. The earlier Non-Event may make some continuations:

- more costly;
- less credible;
- procedurally harder to reopen;
- dependent on additional authorization;
- unavailable within the original window;
- reachable only through a workaround;
- easier to continue by default;
- burdened by unresolved residue.

The claim remains non-teleological:

```text
narrowed corridor
≠ impossible alternative

stabilized provisional arrangement
≠ inevitable continuation
```

The analyst must preserve competing constructions, including current-condition explanations. If present policy, technology, funding, or authorization fully explains the current corridor, the historical Non-Event may remain narratively relevant without carrying a warranted sedimentation claim.

#### Sedimentation without strong Path Dependence

A sedimented Non-Event may contribute to a Trajectory without establishing strong Path Dependence. Chapter 12 requires the stronger demonstration that the relevant present dimension cannot be reconstructed adequately without the historical path.

```text
Non-Event Sedimentation
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

The sedimentation claim establishes carryover. The Path-Dependence claim establishes historical indispensability for a tested present dimension. These burdens must remain separate.

#### C14-SED-01 — repeated warranted Non-Events reshape later action corridors

A public laboratory adopts a quarterly governance procedure requiring a decision on whether a temporary manual sample-tracking process should be replaced by the validated digital workflow. Each quarter, the review package is complete and the decision is placed before the governance group. Across four quarters:

- the review occurs;
- known data-quality limitations are documented;
- implementation options are costed;
- no adoption, rejection, or valid deferral decision is entered;
- the manual process continues under the temporary exception.

During the year:

- staff create additional manual reconciliation steps;
- new hires are trained primarily on the temporary process;
- one interface project is cancelled because the workflow decision remains unresolved;
- the digital vendor's original implementation slot expires;
- the later replacement option requires a more expensive revalidation.

In the fifth quarter, the governance group finally approves the digital workflow. The decision does not restore the original implementation slot, cancelled interface work, or the year of accumulated manual procedures.

The reconstruction supports:

```text
source Non-Events:
four renewed quarterly decision windows without disposition

sedimentation carriers:
manual routines;
training dependence;
cancelled interface work;
expired vendor slot;
revalidation burden

changed action corridor:
continued manual operation became easier in the short term;
digital transition became more expensive and procedurally distant

later realization:
approval in quarter five under changed conditions
```

This supports Non-Event Sedimentation. It does not prove that manual continuation was inevitable, that the governance group intended delay, or that the historical pattern is indispensable to every present dimension.

A bounded local result is `admissible_but_provisional`.

#### C14-CONF-01 — positive postponement events coexist with a higher-level Delay Non-Event

A safety-certification procedure requires a final test within sixty days after equipment installation. The record contains three positive postponement events: a formally scheduled test date, a rescheduling notice after a facility closure, and a second rescheduling notice after replacement equipment arrives. The original sixty-day window expires without a final test or valid pre-expiry extension.

The positive event chain is real and must be preserved. The governing expected test nevertheless remains unrealized within its original warranted window.

```text
scheduling and rescheduling events
+ missed governing test window
→ positive event chain and higher-level Delay Non-Event coexist
```

A composition that reports only "the test was repeatedly rescheduled" would compress the missed-window `Λ`; a composition that reports only "nothing happened" would erase the positive event chain. The bounded finding is therefore partially layered rather than contradictory.

A local result may be `partially_admissible` until the relation between the original window and later authorized windows is fully resolved.

#### C14-CONF-02 — repeated Non-Decision remains distinct from refusal

A university committee considers whether to terminate a provisional laboratory-access restriction at three monthly meetings. At each meeting:

- the required evidence packet is complete;
- discussion occurs;
- members request additional comparison material;
- no vote is taken;
- the restriction continues under the provisional rule.

One draft email from a member states opposition to termination, but the committee record contains no authorized refusal, denial, or vote. The three windows support repeated Non-Decision if the procedure required a disposition at each meeting. They do not support three refusals, nor even one committee-level refusal, solely from the draft email.

```text
individual opposition evidence
≠ authorized collective refusal

repeated absence of disposition
≠ repeated rejection
```

Where the sources cannot determine whether the agenda rule required a vote or merely permitted one, the stronger repeated Non-Decision claim must be reduced or receive `non_capture`.

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 14.4–14.8 now canonically establish:

- Repeated Non-Decision as recurring or renewed decision contexts with separately bounded expectations and source-supported non-realization;
- strict separation of one prolonged unresolved window, repeated windows, explicit refusal, deferral, blockage, missing documentation, and renewed expectation;
- label continuity as insufficient to establish expectation continuity;
- Non-Decision and refusal as distinct positive/non-realization structures;
- repeated Non-Decision without automatic repetition count, sedimentation, Path Dependence, intention, or blame;
- Blocked Responsibility as a configuration-level prevention structure rather than person attribution;
- cyclic, distributed, conditional, jurisdictional, and authorization-dependent blocking relations;
- responsibility distribution without automatic blockage;
- structural blockage without automatic legal duty, fault, incapacity, legitimacy, sanction, intervention, or authority inference;
- Missing Repair as a warranted, bounded unrealized repair occurrence rather than analyst preference or defect alone;
- repair initiation, partial mitigation, workaround, and completed repair as distinct relations;
- unrepaired residue as potentially active in later evidentiary burden, technical incompatibility, role allocation, costs, binding, alternatives, and interpretation;
- Missing Exit as a triggered or otherwise warranted unrealized release occurrence rather than formal exit availability or observed continuation alone;
- exit non-realization without motive, coercion, voluntariness, consent-quality, incapacity, rationality, or blame inference;
