- possible `Α + Θ`, `Ω + Θ`, `Ψ + Θ`, and `Λ + Θ` interactions without fusion, universal causal chain, additive score, or automatic Path Dependence;
- Non-Event Sedimentation as traceable carryover into later meaning, cost, roles, alternatives, bindings, residue, or action corridors;
- one prolonged Non-Event, repeated occurrences, and mixed formations as distinct occurrence architectures;
- repetition and duration as insufficient without a later sedimentation carrier;
- changed action corridors without determinism or impossibility inference;
- sedimentation without automatic strong Path Dependence;
- all seven assigned WP2 Pressure Cases without presenting them as produced Case files, empirical evidence, calibration data, training data, or Reader graph specifications.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP2 repair requirement. It preserves the remaining Chapter-14 duties:

- WP3 preservation of `Λ` through composition, False Non-Event, Minimal Non-Event Record, schema/extension decision, Failure, Claim Reduction, Mandatory Stop, Non-Capture, and eight assigned Pressure Cases;
- WP4 integrated audit and Provisional Lock;
- later Chapter-15 `COMPOSE` execution;
- later Chapter-23 internal Non-Event decomposition;
- later Chapter-49 source-limit architecture;
- later case, appendix, model-finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP2 completion
≠ Chapter 14 provisional lock
≠ every repeated absence is Λ
≠ Non-Event sedimentation automatically
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ completed DECOMPOSE occurrence
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ person attribution
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 14 — WP3:
Preserving Λ in Composition,
False Non-Event,
and Minimal Non-Event Record
```

<a id="14-9-preserving-λ-in-composition"></a>

### 14.9 Preserving `Λ` in Composition

Chapter 14 does not execute `COMPOSE`. It specifies the minimum preservation burden that Chapter 15 must satisfy when a later composition contains one or more warranted `Λ` occurrences.

A Non-Event may be compressed, grouped, or represented at a coarser granularity. Its constitutive non-realization structure may not be silently replaced by positive-event language.

```text
expected occurrence did not occur within its warranted window
≠ merely: the existing state continued
```

The distinction matters because continued positive activity may be compatible with the higher-level Non-Event. A composition that records only the visible activity can erase the expectation, the missed realization condition, and the practical load that made the interval path-forming.

#### Preservation burden

Where a `Λ` occurrence is constitutive to the later composite, the composition must preserve or explicitly place under canonical Loss:

- the expected occurrence;
- the expectation relation;
- the expectation frame;
- the expected window or realization condition;
- source support for non-realization;
- positive sub-events inside the interval;
- persistence, repetition, or occurrence boundaries;
- affected roles and alternatives;
- accumulated costs, bindings, asymmetries, workarounds, or residue;
- the later carrier through which the Non-Event remains structurally active;
- uncertainty and competing constructions;
- the distinction between non-realization and missing information.

The five-part Loss declaration remains controlling:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

A later composition may state that several recurring review failures are represented as one repeated-Non-Decision pattern. It must then disclose what occurrence boundaries, local frames, positive sub-events, and variations are compressed. It may not claim that nothing happened throughout the interval.

#### `Λ` character versus positive-result description

A positive-result description may be accurate and still be insufficient. For example:

```text
temporary authorization remained in force
```

may truthfully describe the observed state, while failing to preserve:

```text
the required review did not occur in three separately warranted windows
```

Where the omitted expectation and non-realization explain later costs, alternatives, roles, or corridor changes, the positive description alone breaches Functional and Reference Continuity.

```text
positive description of continuation
≠ preservation of the governing Λ structure
```

Preservation does not require rhetorical emphasis. It requires traceable retention of the relations that carry the claim.

#### Positive sub-events and higher-level non-realization

Positive sub-events may remain fully represented without decomposing the governing Non-Event away. Scheduling, reminders, partial repairs, interim releases, evidence collection, or postponement decisions can be positive occurrences. They do not realize the expected occurrence unless they satisfy its declared realization condition.

```text
positive sub-event present
≠ governing expected occurrence realized
```

This is not a privilege of coarse granularity. The higher-level `Λ` remains warranted only if its own expectation, window, source, and load burdens remain satisfied. Finer resolution may reveal that the purported Non-Event was actually realized, that its window remained open, or that the expected occurrence was misidentified. Resolution gain is therefore evidentially relevant but resolution-neutral in authority.

#### Compression without causal overreach

A later composition may preserve that a review, repair, decision, or exit did not occur and that costs or alternatives changed afterward. It may not infer from the absence alone:

- hidden intention;
- deliberate obstruction;
- personal blame;
- legal duty or breach;
- moral legitimacy;
- a unique causal chain;
- inevitable later outcomes;
- a target function.

```text
preserved Λ
≠ causal, intentional, normative, or person-level explanation
```

Where multiple mechanisms could connect the Non-Event to later conditions, the composition must preserve those alternatives or reduce the claim.

#### `Λ` preservation and operation ownership

Chapter 14 supplies a source object that may later enter `COMPOSE`. It does not authorize the operation. Chapter 15 must still declare:

- selected source occurrences;
- order and constitutive relations;
- reference continuity;
- composite object formation;
- excluded alternatives;
- Loss;
- uncertainty;
- output mapping and Stop conditions.

```text
preservable Λ structure
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
```

Likewise, representing positive sub-events does not execute `DECOMPOSE`, and assigning contextual meaning to a Non-Event does not execute `PROJECT_AS`.

#### C14-PRES-01 — composition preserves expectation, window, non-realization, and residue

A regional procurement board was required by its published procedure to review an emergency purchasing authorization every thirty days. Three review windows closed without a vote. During those months, purchase orders, supplier meetings, and monthly expenditure reports continued. The temporary authorization remained active, and two alternative procurement routes became unavailable because their bid windows expired.

A later PATH summary proposes the composite statement:

> Emergency procurement continued for ninety days.

That statement preserves the positive continuation but erases the constitutive `Λ` structure. A minimally admissible later composition must preserve at least:

- the thirty-day review expectation;
- the three bounded review windows;
- the source-supported absence of votes;
- the positive purchasing and reporting events;
- expiry of the alternative bid windows;
- the resulting procurement and evidentiary residue;
- uncertainty about why no vote occurred.

The Chapter-14 handoff is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. Chapter 15 must still perform the actual `COMPOSE` test.

#### C14-SUBEV-01 — positive sub-events do not realize the governing expected event

A data-reconciliation agreement required a signed reconciliation report by 30 June. Before the deadline, the teams exchanged datasets, held four workshops, corrected several records, and circulated two unsigned drafts. No signed report was issued by the deadline, and the downstream audit remained unable to close.

The workshops, exchanges, corrections, and drafts are positive events. None satisfies the declared realization condition: issuance of the signed report.

```text
substantial activity
+ partial correction
+ draft production
≠ signed reconciliation report
```

The higher-level Missing Repair claim remains locally admissible if the expectation and non-realization sources are sound. A decomposition that lists only the positive sub-events would be incomplete if it concluded that the governing repair occurred with no remainder.

### 14.10 False Non-Event

A **False Non-Event** is a claimed `Λ` occurrence that fails one or more constitutive burdens required for a structured Non-Event. It is a local failure description, not an eleventh Output Class and not proof that the alleged expected event occurred.

```text
failed Non-Event claim
≠ positive-event proof
```

The claim fails where the record cannot responsibly establish:

- an identifiable expected occurrence;
- a warranted expectation relation;
- a declared expectation frame;
- a bounded expected window or realization condition;
- source-supported non-realization;
- a praxis-relevant difference;
- relevance to the temporal chain under analysis.

The failure is non-compensatory. Long duration, serious consequences, dense records, repeated silence, later residue, or visual prominence cannot replace a missing expectation or non-realization basis.

```text
large consequence
≠ missing expectation supplied

dense graph
≠ non-realization established
```

#### Retrospectively invented expectation

An analyst may not infer that an event was expected merely because it would have prevented a later problem, appears standard from a later perspective, or would now be regarded as prudent.

A valid expectation may be institutional, relational, procedural, contractual, or otherwise source-supported. It need not be normatively binding. But it must exist at the relevant historical cut rather than being imported from later knowledge.

```text
later desirability
≠ earlier expectation
```

#### Open window and premature failure

An occurrence is not a Non-Event merely because it has not yet occurred. Where the expected window remains open, the record may describe:

- an expected occurrence pending;
- an unresolved realization status;
- emerging delay pressure;
- incomplete sources;
- a future checkpoint.

It may not mark the occurrence as failed unless the realization condition, deadline, or structurally relevant interval has been reached or otherwise warrantably breached.

```text
not yet realized
≠ failed within the expected window
```

#### Missing source and unknown occurrence

A source gap can leave the occurrence status unknown. It does not establish non-occurrence. Conversely, failure to establish `Λ` does not establish that the expected event occurred.

The responsible outputs may include:

- `claim_reduction_required`;
- `admissible_but_provisional`;
- `resolution_neutral`;
- `mandatory_stop`;
- `failed_transformation`;
- `non_capture`.

The mapping depends on whether a weaker claim remains supportable, whether further analysis would exceed the source ceiling, and whether the unsupported claim is being reused for authority.

#### Ordinary absence and mere possibility

A continuation that was merely possible but never expected does not create a Non-Event when it fails to occur. Chapter 13 may establish that a branch was historically available; Chapter 14 still requires an expectation that the relevant occurrence should take place within a bounded frame and window.

```text
available alternative
≠ expected occurrence
```

Similarly, a blank interval in a timeline, a missing node in a graph, or an unobserved transition cannot carry `Λ` without the full expectation structure.

#### C14-FALSE-01 — retrospectively invented expectation fails

A city introduced a digital permit system in 2024. Two years later, an analyst argues that the city failed to conduct a public usability review during the first six months and labels the absence a central Non-Event. The project charter, procurement documents, launch plan, meeting records, and applicable procedure contain no review commitment, trigger, scheduled window, or role assigned to conduct such a review. The review would plausibly have been useful, and later complaints make its absence salient, but the historical record supports only later desirability.

```text
later complaints
+ plausible benefit of early review
≠ historically warranted review expectation
```

The claimed `Λ` fails. A weaker statement—no early usability review is documented—may remain admissible with a source limitation. Treating the invented Non-Event as evidence of neglect, blame, or causal responsibility requires `mandatory_stop`.

### 14.11 Minimal Non-Event Record

The **Minimal Non-Event Record** is a bounded analytical view inside the Shared Transformation Record. It preserves the fields needed to evaluate one PATH-specific `Λ` claim without creating a second record system or replacing the top-level source, reference, operation, admissibility, Loss, alternatives, uncertainty, and governance fields.

The controlled carrier decision is:

```text
existing owner-bound extensions carrier
= sufficient
```

No amendment to `Transformation_Record.schema.json` is required at this stage. No Smoke Record migration is required. A second independent schema remains prohibited.

#### Canonical extension form

```yaml
extensions:
  - extension_id: pms-strata:chapter14:non-event:v0.1
    owner_or_source: PMS-STRATA Chapter 14
    control_source_pointer: 01_blocks/02_part_i_path.md#14-11-minimal-non-event-record
    purpose: Preserve one bounded PATH-specific Non-Event claim without replacing required Shared-Record fields.
    payload:
      non_event:
        reference_object:
        expected_occurrence:
        expectation_relation:
        expectation_frame:
        expected_window_or_condition:
        non_realization_support:
        positive_sub_events:
        persistence_or_repetition:
        affected_roles:
        affected_alternatives:
        accumulated_costs_or_residue:
        sedimentation_status:
        source_support:
        uncertainty:
        claim_scope:
        output_class_mapping:
    does_not_replace_required_field: true
```

The extension is optional and owner-bound. It does not convert its payload keys into new universal PMS or STRATA primitives.

```text
complete Non-Event view
≠ Non-Event proof
≠ operation completion
```

#### Field burdens

`reference_object` identifies the occurrence, Path, Trajectory, or bounded composite to which the claim belongs.

`expected_occurrence` identifies what was expected to occur. It must be more specific than a general preference for improvement, action, repair, exit, or decision.

`expectation_relation` records why the occurrence was expected: for example a procedure, commitment, trigger, schedule, role relation, renewal rule, or independently supported relational expectation.

`expectation_frame` declares the frame under which the expectation applies.

`expected_window_or_condition` declares the deadline, interval, trigger condition, recurring window, or completion condition against which non-realization is tested.

`non_realization_support` records evidence that the expected occurrence did not satisfy the realization condition. A mere absence of records must be identified as a source gap rather than entered as proof.

`positive_sub_events` preserves observed events inside the interval so that `Λ` is not represented as an empty span.

`persistence_or_repetition` distinguishes one prolonged Non-Event, repeated occurrences, and mixed formations.

`affected_roles`, `affected_alternatives`, and `accumulated_costs_or_residue` record the praxeological load without inferring person motive, fault, or legitimacy.

`sedimentation_status` states whether later carryover is established, provisional, absent, or unresolved in prose. It is not a closed machine enum and does not automatically establish Trajectory or Path Dependence.

`source_support`, `uncertainty`, and `claim_scope` preserve the source and claim ceilings.

`output_class_mapping` maps the local result to one canonical Output Class. It cannot create an additional class.

#### Record completion and semantic warrant

A syntactically complete extension may contain weak, disputed, or unsupported assertions. Formal validation can check field presence, types, owner pointer, and non-replacement of required top-level fields. It cannot determine:

- whether the expectation was historically warranted;
- whether the occurrence failed;
- whether the sources prove non-realization;
- whether positive sub-events satisfy the governing occurrence;
- whether repetition or sedimentation is established;
- whether blame, duty, coercion, motive, or intention exists;
- whether `COMPOSE`, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS` is admissible;
- which Output Class is substantively correct.

```text
machine-readable completeness
≠ semantic validity
```

#### C14-REC-01 — complete record retains source uncertainty and bounded mapping

A hospital policy required quarterly review of a temporary specialist-access restriction. The agenda and policy establish the expectation and window. Meeting minutes document discussion and requests for additional evidence, but the archive is missing the final two pages on which a vote would normally appear. The restriction remained active into the next quarter.

A complete Non-Event extension records:

- the quarterly review expectation;
- the meeting window;
- discussion as positive sub-events;
- the missing pages;
- continued restriction;
- affected access and evidentiary burdens;
- uncertainty between non-realization and undocumented disposition.

The extension is complete, but the substantive mapping is `non_capture`, not established `Λ`. Record completeness correctly preserves the unresolved source/non-realization relation rather than laundering it into certainty.

#### Failure and Claim Reduction

A failed or overextended Non-Event claim should be reduced to the strongest supportable statement. The reduction ladder is:

```text
sedimented PATH-forming Λ
→ bounded local Λ occurrence
→ documented expectation with unresolved realization status
→ observed positive event chain or ordinary absence
→ source-gap statement
→ mandatory_stop or non_capture
```

Reduction does not erase the earlier failed claim. A new frame, granularity, graph, composition, extension payload, or target label creates a new testable claim; it does not retroactively validate the old one.

```text
new representation
≠ old claim repaired
```

##### C14-REDUCE-01 — sedimented `Λ` reduces to expectation plus unresolved occurrence status

A licensing office was expected under a published annual schedule to review a temporary fee waiver. The schedule and meeting agenda establish the expected review. Later budgets show the waiver continuing for three years, and staff reports describe growing reconciliation costs. The minutes for the first review year are missing, while the second and third years document no vote.

The evidence supports two later Non-Decision occurrences and subsequent residue. It does not establish the first year's non-realization. A claim of one uninterrupted three-year sedimented `Λ` must be reduced to:

- a documented first-year expectation with unresolved occurrence status;
- two later source-supported Non-Decision occurrences;
- bounded sedimentation findings tied only to the supported occurrences.

The correct local output is `claim_reduction_required`.

#### Mandatory Stop

`mandatory_stop` applies where continued use of the Non-Event claim would exceed source, type, reference, or governance boundaries, especially where:

- missing records are treated as proof of non-occurrence;
- a retrospectively invented expectation is preserved as fact;
- a graph renders an empty interval as established `Λ`;
- non-realization is used to infer individual blame, motive, incapacity, coercion, consent, or diagnosis;
- a local `Λ` claim is used to authorize sanction, intervention, prediction, or irreversible classification;
- an unresolved source gap is hidden by composition or record completeness;
- the claim is relabelled as an operation result or target function without a new test.

##### C14-STOP-01 — missing records used for blame and sanction

A project archive contains no approval memo for a scheduled safety review. The schedule proves that a review was expected, but the archive is known to be incomplete and contains no reliable evidence that the review failed to occur. A manager nevertheless labels the missing memo a deliberate refusal by one employee and proposes disciplinary action.

The move makes four unsupported transitions:

```text
missing memo
→ non-occurrence
→ deliberate refusal
→ individual blame
→ sanction
```

The required result is `mandatory_stop`. STRATA supplies no basis for the person attribution or sanction.

#### Non-Capture

`non_capture` is required where the structure matters materially but the available sources cannot responsibly separate the relevant claims. Typical unresolved pairs include:

```text
non-realization
or
undocumented realization

one prolonged Non-Event
or
multiple renewed occurrences

structural blockage
or
refusal outside the surviving record

Missing Exit
or
continued participation under a changed frame
```

Non-Capture is not a weak substitute for proof. It preserves the boundary where neither a positive-event claim nor a Non-Event claim can carry the available record without distortion.

##### C14-NC-01 — non-realization and source gap remain materially undecidable

A benefits board was required to reconsider a temporary eligibility restriction at its September meeting. The agenda confirms the expected review, and later records show the restriction remained active. The meeting recording is corrupted, the minutes omit the relevant item, and no disposition notice survives. The continuing restriction has substantial effects on access and later appeals.

The record cannot determine whether:

- the review did not occur;
- a review occurred without disposition;
- a disposition occurred but was not preserved;
- a procedural change moved the decision to another forum.

The expectation problem is materially important, but neither `Λ` nor positive realization can be established. The canonical result is `non_capture`.

#### Operation and owner handoff

A warranted `Λ` occurrence is a source object or occurrence-level finding. It is not itself a completed transformation.

```text
local Λ finding
≠ COMPOSE
≠ DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS
```

Chapter 15 owns composite formation and compression. Chapter 23 owns internal decomposition of events and Non-Events. Chapter 49 owns the general source-gap architecture. RETYPE owns contextual target functions. None receives authority by inheritance from Chapter 14.

##### C14-HANDOFF-01 — local `Λ` finding misused as operation and target function

An audit establishes that a required renewal decision did not occur within two supported windows and that the resulting continuation altered costs and alternatives. An analyst then records:

- the local `Λ` finding as a completed `COMPOSE` result;
- the list of reminders as a completed `DECOMPOSE` result;
- the Non-Event as a projected function called “organizational negligence.”

All three moves fail:

- no composite object, selection, constitutive relation, or Loss test was performed;
- listing sub-events does not by itself reconstruct the same reference object at finer granularity;
- “organizational negligence” imports a normative target function and authority not established by PATH analysis.

The local Non-Event may remain admissible. The operation and function claims require reduction; continued authoritative use requires `mandatory_stop`.

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 14.9–14.11 now canonically establish:

- the preservation burden for `Λ` structures entering later composition without executing `COMPOSE`;
- mandatory retention or Loss disclosure for expectation, frame, window, non-realization, positive sub-events, persistence/repetition, affected roles and alternatives, accumulated residue, later carrier, uncertainty, and missing-information distinction;
- positive-event descriptions as insufficient where they erase constitutive non-realization;
- positive sub-events and a higher-level governing Non-Event as compatible;
- preservation without intention, blame, causal uniqueness, normativity, prediction, or target-function inference;
- False Non-Event as a local failure description rather than a new Output Class;
- retrospectively invented expectation, open windows, missing sources, mere possibility, and ordinary absence as insufficient for `Λ`;
- failure of a Non-Event claim without positive-event proof;
- the Minimal Non-Event Record as an optional owner-bound `extensions` view inside the Shared Transformation Record;
- sufficiency of the existing extension carrier, with no schema amendment, Smoke Record migration, parallel schema, new Rule, operation, Output Class, audit stage, enum, score, probability, or truth engine;
- record completeness without semantic proof;
- a bounded claim-reduction ladder;
- Mandatory Stop where unsupported `Λ` claims are reused for person attribution, sanction, authority, prediction, operation, or function;
- Non-Capture where material source and non-realization structures remain responsibly undecidable;
- Chapter 15, Chapter 23, Chapter 49, and RETYPE ownership without authority inheritance;
- all eight assigned WP3 Pressure Cases without presenting them as produced Case files, empirical evidence, calibration data, training data, or Reader graph specifications.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP3 repair requirement. It preserves:

- WP4 integrated audit and Provisional Lock;
- Chapter 15 execution of `COMPOSE`;
- Chapter 17 integrated PATH cases and audit;
- Chapter 23 internal Non-Event decomposition;
- Chapter 49 source-limit architecture;
- later appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP3 completion
≠ Chapter 14 provisional lock
≠ every absence or source gap is Λ
≠ semantic proof from record completeness
≠ completed COMPOSE or DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ person attribution
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 14 — WP4:
Synchronization,
Integrated Audit,
and Provisional Lock
```

#### Chapter 14 completion boundary

Chapter 14 has now operationalized `Λ` within Paths and Trajectories without converting ordinary absence, missing information, incomplete archives, silence, delay, unchosen alternatives, positive sub-event chains, graph gaps, or later residue into Non-Events by default. It preserves the foundational Chapter-3 Non-Event object while adding the stronger PATH burden required for temporal composition, sedimentation, and later `COMPOSE`.

```text
absence
≠ missing source
≠ unknown occurrence status
≠ warranted Non-Event
```

The Chapter 14 completion test is satisfied:

1. a PATH-specific `Λ` claim requires an identifiable expected occurrence, a warranted expectation relation, a declared expectation frame, a bounded expected window or realization condition, source-supported non-realization, praxeological load, and relevance to the temporal chain;
2. those burdens are conjunctive and non-compensatory: duration, repetition, graph density, later residue, narrative coherence, record completeness, or operator-profile richness cannot replace a missing expectation, window, or non-realization basis;
3. ordinary absence, missing information, incomplete source coverage, unknown occurrence status, and supported non-realization remain distinct;
4. no-record-found remains insufficient to establish non-occurrence, and Chapter 49 retains the general source-gap architecture;
5. expectation is frame-bound and source-supported rather than retrospectively selected to manufacture `Λ`;
6. positive scheduling, reminder, coordination, mitigation, drafting, partial-release, or postponement events may coexist with a governing higher-level Non-Event and must remain visible at their own granularity;
7. Delay as `Λ` remains distinct from an observed postponement-event chain, a Chapter-13 Deferred Branch, intention, refusal, and blame; later realization does not automatically erase the earlier missed-window load;
8. Repeated Non-Decision requires renewed or recurring active decision contexts and remains distinct from one prolonged unresolved window, repeated missing documentation, refusal, blockage, and deferral;
9. Blocked Responsibility reconstructs a role-and-authorization configuration, not person fault, diagnosis, legal duty, moral ranking, or sanction;
10. Missing Repair requires an independently warranted repair, integration, correction, reconciliation, or closure occurrence with a bounded completion condition; defect, desirability, initiation, workaround, mitigation, and completed repair remain distinct;
11. Missing Exit requires an independently warranted, announced, triggered, contractually due, or procedurally expected exit or release occurrence; formal exit availability and observed continuation alone do not establish it, and motive, coercion, consent, voluntariness, incapacity, or rationality remain unclaimed;
12. Non-Event Sedimentation requires traceable carryover into later meaning, costs, roles, alternatives, bindings, residue, evidentiary burden, workarounds, or Action Corridors; repetition and duration alone do not establish sedimentation;
13. one prolonged Non-Event, repeated Non-Event occurrences, and mixed formations remain distinguishable, and calendar segmentation does not determine occurrence identity automatically;
14. possible `Λ + Θ`, `Α + Θ`, `Ω + Θ`, and `Ψ + Θ` interactions remain non-fused, non-scored, non-compensatory, and insufficient by themselves to establish strong Path Dependence;
15. later `COMPOSE` may compress `Λ` but must preserve or declare through canonical Loss the expected occurrence, expectation relation and frame, window or condition, non-realization support, positive sub-events, persistence or repetition, affected roles and alternatives, accumulated costs or residue, later carrier, uncertainty, and missing-information boundary;
16. a positive description of continuity, activity, adaptation, or workaround does not preserve a governing Non-Event when its expectation-grounded non-realization is constitutive of the Path claim;
17. False Non-Event remains a local failure description rather than an Output Class, and a failed `Λ` claim does not prove that the expected positive event occurred;
18. the Minimal Non-Event Record remains an optional owner-bound view inside the Shared Transformation Record's `extensions` carrier; it does not replace required fields, create a parallel schema, overload another field, or establish semantic validity from syntactic completeness;
19. Failure and Claim Reduction preserve weaker supported findings from sedimented PATH-forming `Λ`, to bounded local `Λ`, to documented expectation with unresolved realization status, to observed event chain or ordinary absence, to source-gap statement;
20. Mandatory Stop applies where a known unsupported `Λ`, inferred intention, blame, duty, motive, coercion, sanction, graph gap, record completion, operator profile, operation label, or target function is reused to manufacture authority or irreversible conclusions;
21. Non-Capture remains available where a materially relevant expectation and temporal burden are source-supported but realization status, forum change, occurrence boundaries, responsibility configuration, repair completion, exit status, or sedimentation cannot be responsibly resolved;
22. Chapter 15 receives preservable source structures and Loss burdens without inherited `COMPOSE` authorization, Chapter 23 retains internal Non-Event `DECOMPOSE`, Chapter 49 retains general source limits, Chapter 16 retains PATH boundary tests, Chapter 17 retains integrated cases and audit, and RETYPE receives no target function;
23. all nineteen prepared Chapter-14 Pressure Cases are represented in canonical prose without being misreported as produced `03_cases/*` artifacts, empirical evidence, calibration data, training data, finished graph specifications, operation results, person findings, or application authority;
24. no graph, Reader rendering, revised frame, new label, finer granularity, longer duration, operation, projection, extension record, schema validity, or visual complexity may erase a failed claim or inherit missing expectation and source warrant;
25. no part of Chapter 14 changes `PMS.yaml`, the Δ–Ψ inventory, operator order, operator dependencies, the three-operation grammar, the ten Output Classes, the sixteen Admissibility Rules, or the twelve audit stages.

The integrated audit confirms that Chapter 14 is not an absence-classification engine. A silent interval is not `Λ`. A missing node or edge in a graph is not non-realization. A complete record is not semantic proof. A later residue does not retroactively establish that an expected occurrence existed or failed.

```text
empty interval
≠ Non-Event

later residue
≠ retroactive expectation warrant

record completeness
≠ semantic validity
```

The anti-laundering boundary remains active. A failed Non-Event claim may be reopened only as a new testable claim with a corrected expected occurrence, expectation relation, frame, window, source basis, granularity, non-realization burden, positive-sub-event account, affected role or alternative structure, sedimentation carrier, uncertainty declaration, or reduced Claim Ceiling. A new graph, label, frame, operation, target function, application context, or completed extension record cannot inherit or conceal the failed burden.

```text
new Non-Event claim
= new testable claim

later COMPOSE success
≠ retroactive Λ repair

later PROJECT_AS success
≠ retroactive expectation warrant
```

The chapter-level result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known local Chapter-14 repair requirement. It preserves later pressure from Chapter 15 complete `COMPOSE`, Chapter 16 PATH Boundary Conditions, Chapter 17 integrated PATH cases and audit, Chapter 23 SUB decomposition, Chapter 49 source limits, RETYPE, LIMITS, Appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and later Reader graph implementation.

```text
Chapter 14 provisional lock
≠ Non-Event truth engine
≠ hidden-intention inference
≠ blame or duty finding
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

Reopen Chapter 14 only if later work demonstrates a concrete conflict involving expectation relation, frame, expected window, non-realization support, positive sub-events, Delay, repeated Non-Decision, Blocked Responsibility, Missing Repair, Missing Exit, occurrence architecture, sedimentation carrier, `Λ` preservation through composition, canonical Loss, False Non-Event, Minimal Non-Event Record ownership, Failure/Reduction route, Stop/Non-Capture route, Chapter-15/16/17/23/49 handoff, RETYPE separation, or unchanged PMS Base. Mere preference for more inferred absences, a cleaner graph, stronger intentionality, a more decisive status, more detail, or a larger Non-Event inventory is not a reopening ground.

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 15 — Preparation:
COMPOSE: Selection, Formation, and Compression
```

<a id="chapter-15-compose-selection-formation-and-compression"></a>

## 15. COMPOSE: Selection, Formation, and Compression

Chapters 9–14 established the temporal structures that may later enter a composition: warranted transitions, Sequences, Paths, Trajectories, Path-Dependence findings, branch structures, alternatives, and expectation-grounded Non-Events. Chapter 15 now performs a different task. It operationalizes the existing `COMPOSE` operation for PATH.

```text
many or sequential source structures
→ new composite analytical object
```

The arrow is not automatic. Multiple sources, chronological order, a connected graph, or a stable macro-label do not by themselves establish an operation occurrence. A Chapter-15 `COMPOSE` occurrence must disclose why particular source structures were selected, how they are ordered, which frame controls the operation, what relation forms the target, what source load remains traceable, and what is compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable.

```text
source plurality
≠ composition

connected representation
≠ formed composite
```

Chapter 15 does not redefine the operation type established in Chapter 4. It distinguishes:

```text
COMPOSE operation type
≠ particular COMPOSE occurrence
≠ resulting composite analytical object
```

The source structures retain their origin types. A `Λ` occurrence does not cease to be `Λ` because it enters a Path composite. An `Ω`-bearing occurrence does not become a frame or attractor. A prior subpath remains a derived composite source rather than becoming a new PMS primitive. The resulting target is a new analytical object only in the bounded sense licensed by the operation record.

Sections 15.1–15.6 establish the operation-entry architecture:

- the purpose of `COMPOSE`;
- conjunctive preconditions;
- source-object identity and typing;
- explicit selection;
- temporal or structural ordering;
- the composition frame.

Sections 15.7–15.11 later establish formation, preserved structure, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable Loss. Sections 15.12–15.16 later establish composition claims, no-retyping, counterfactual sensitivity, failure, the complete record, Stop, and Non-Capture. Chapter 16 retains PATH boundary adjudication. Chapter 17 retains integrated PATH cases. Chapter 24 retains `DECOMPOSE`. RETYPE retains every contextual target function through `PROJECT_AS`.

<a id="15-1-purpose-of-compose"></a>

### 15.1 Purpose of `COMPOSE`

The purpose of `COMPOSE` is to form a new analytical object where several source structures jointly support a praxeological discrimination that no source, source list, or unformed chronology supports by itself.

```text
jointly traceable source load
+ warranted formation
→ new bounded analytical discrimination
```

The operation is needed because praxis structures are often distributed across occurrences and scales. A transition may carry a change in configuration. A later `Λ` may preserve a missed review window. A branch record may preserve an alternative that remained available. An asymmetry-bearing occurrence may explain why the same formal transition imposed different practical costs. A subpath may retain the order by which these relations became linked. The composite may make visible how those elements jointly constitute a Sequence, Path, Trajectory, branch structure, phase, or another declared temporal object.

The operation is not justified merely because combining sources is convenient. It must add controlled analytical purchase. A valid target must permit at least one warranted discrimination concerning, for example:

- transition identity or completion;
- continuity and discontinuity;
- selection among available continuations;
- persistence of a configuration;
- accumulation or release of binding load;
- preservation of a Non-Event within a positive event chain;
- changed reachability of later actions;
- distinction between Sequence, Path, and Trajectory;
- comparison with a rival composition or no-composition route.

Where the target adds only a shorter label, a smoother narrative, or an attractive visualization, the operation has not yet crossed the Praxeological Relevance Floor.

```text
summary convenience
≠ praxeological purchase
```

#### Object formation without authority formation

A composite analytical object may be more structured than any individual source. It does not thereby inherit more factual, causal, normative, diagnostic, political, legal, or application authority.

```text
more structure
≠ more authority
```

A valid composition may support a bounded claim such as:

> These configurations and transitions form one source-traceable Path under the declared frame.

It may not infer, merely from successful formation:

- that the Path was necessary or optimal;
- that a Trajectory or strong Path Dependence is established;
- that an actor intended the result;
- that the composite has a macro-event, frame, attractor, or other target function;
- that an intervention, sanction, diagnosis, or irreversible classification is warranted.

Those are separate claims with separate owners and ceilings.

#### New object, preserved lineage

The target must be new in analytical organization while remaining dependent on reconstructible source lineage.

```text
new composite object
≠ source erasure
```

The composite is not a container that makes its components interchangeable. Its identity depends on selected source relations. If the record cannot show which source elements and relations support the target, the target is a label rather than a formed object.

#### `C15-PURP-01` — a composite that adds temporal discrimination

A public service replacement involved four source structures:

1. a configuration in which the legacy access channel remained active;
2. a warranted transition that moved one user group to the replacement channel;
3. a source-supported `Λ` occurrence in which the scheduled access review did not occur within its declared window;
4. a later transition that closed the legacy channel while a documented alternative support route remained only partially available.

Considered separately, the sources establish a configuration, two transitions, one Non-Event, and an alternative-access condition. A simple chronology lists them. A Chapter-15 candidate instead proposes a bounded Path composite: **staged access replacement with an unresolved review gap that altered the conditions of final closure**.

The target adds a praxeological discrimination unavailable from the source list alone. It preserves that the review was not a positive transition, that final closure followed rather than contained the earlier migration, and that the alternative support route affected the practical meaning of closure. It does not claim that the missed review caused the closure, that the resulting Path was inevitable, or that the composite has a macro-event function.

```text
typed sources
+ warranted order
+ review-gap preservation
+ closure relation
→ bounded Path composite candidate
```

The candidate is locally `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, subject to the later formation and Loss tests in Sections 15.7–15.16.

### 15.2 Preconditions

A Chapter-15 operation occurrence may begin only when its entry burdens are sufficiently declared and supportable. The preconditions are conjunctive and non-compensatory.

A candidate must provide:

1. identifiable source objects;
2. explicit source typings;
3. a declared source frame;
4. source granularity and relative level;
5. temporal or structural scope;
6. warranted ordering where order is claimed;
7. an explicit selection rule;
8. an explicit formation rule or formation hypothesis;
9. candidate constitutive relations;
10. source support and source-limit disclosure;
11. an expected praxeological difference between the source set and the proposed target;
12. a possible five-part Loss account;
13. a bounded target object and composition claim;
14. at least one rival composition or no-composition option where materially relevant.

```text
COMPOSE entry
= conjunctive gate
```

The gate does not require every burden to be finally adjudicated before analysis begins. It requires that the candidate expose what must be tested and that missing burdens are not concealed by detail, volume, graph density, or formal completeness.

```text
more sources
≠ repair for missing relation

more detail
≠ repair for missing formation rule
```

#### Source sufficiency and operation sufficiency

A source may be individually well supported while the proposed composition remains unsupported. Conversely, a plausible formation hypothesis may fail because one load-bearing source is unavailable or materially uncertain.

```text
valid source objects
≠ valid composition automatically

plausible target
≠ sufficient source support
```

The precondition gate therefore separates:

- **source-object warrant** — whether a source occurrence or composite is sufficiently identified and typed;
- **relation warrant** — whether the claimed order or structural relation is supported;
- **formation warrant** — whether those relations form the proposed target;
- **claim warrant** — which bounded statement the resulting object can support.

#### No-composition as a positive route

The correct result may be that the sources should remain:

- a collection;
- a chronology;
- an unresolved transition field;
- a partial order;
- several separate subpaths;
- rival bounded compositions;
- or an explicitly uncomposed source set.

No-composition is not analytical failure when formation would destroy distinctions or exceed the source basis. It may be the traceably superior result.

#### `C15-PRE-01` — abundant material without a common operation basis

An archive contains hundreds of records concerning five years of organizational change: meeting minutes, personnel notices, procurement files, incident reports, training logs, and public statements. The materials are individually dateable and many are internally reliable. An analyst proposes the target label **institutional transformation trajectory** and places every record into one timeline.

The candidate fails the Chapter-15 entry gate. The record does not declare:

- one reference object shared across the source families;
- a common frame or level;
- which events are relevant to the proposed target;
- how procurement, staffing, incidents, and public communication are related;
- a selection rule;
- a formation hypothesis;
- a bounded target claim;
- or a viable Loss account.

The abundance of sources does not compensate for the missing relation and formation burdens.

```text
large archive
+ chronological sorting
≠ COMPOSE occurrence
```

The appropriate local result is `failed_transformation` for the proposed composite. Weaker source-level findings remain available. The archive may support several later candidates, but each requires a new testable operation occurrence.

<a id="15-3-source-objects"></a>

### 15.3 Source Objects

The sources of a PATH-specific `COMPOSE` occurrence may include:

- configurations;
- transitions;
- events;
- Non-Events;
- Sequences;
- subpaths;
- branch structures;
- partial trajectories;
- operator-typed occurrences;
- previously formed composite analytical objects.

Eligibility does not mean interchangeability. Each source enters the occurrence with its own identity, type, frame, granularity, level, temporal scope, support status, uncertainty, and relation to the proposed target.

#### Source-object declaration

For every load-bearing source, the operation record must make reconstructible:

- source identifier;
- object or occurrence type;
- origin frame;
- origin granularity and relative level;
- temporal location or scope;
- source pointer or lineage;
- current support and uncertainty;
- role in the proposed composition;
- whether the source is constitutive, supporting, illustrative, contested, or excluded.

```text
source identifier
≠ source typing

source typing
≠ constitutive role
```

A source may be valid but merely illustrative. Another may be uncertain yet potentially constitutive. Those statuses must not be collapsed.

#### Type Integrity

Composition does not retroactively rewrite source identity.

```text
source occurrence type before composition
=
source occurrence type after composition
```

Accordingly:

- a configuration remains a configuration;
- a transition remains a transition;
- a `Λ` occurrence remains a Non-Event where preserved;
- a rejected or blocked branch retains its local status;
- an `Ω`-bearing occurrence remains asymmetry-bearing rather than becoming the target itself;
- a previously formed Path remains a derived composite source rather than becoming a primitive.

The resulting target may organize these sources into a new object. It may not absorb them into one undifferentiated type.

#### Source-lineage inheritance is bounded

A source composite may itself depend on earlier records. Chapter 15 may reference that lineage rather than re-derive it, but it cannot inherit more support than the source actually possesses.

```text
source composite admissible_with_bounded_claim
→ bounded input only

bounded input
≠ unrestricted target warrant
```

Known uncertainty, excluded material, and irrecoverable Loss in a source composite travel forward as burdens. A later composition cannot reset them by relabeling the object or moving to a coarser frame.

#### Source-set boundary

Every occurrence must declare the source domain from which selection was made. At minimum:

- temporal range searched;
- source families considered;
- inclusion horizon;
- unavailable or inaccessible source domains;
- contested source identities;
- later materials used for periodization or comparison;
- prohibition of back-projecting later availability into earlier configurations.

Source-set closure is claim-bound, not absolute. The operation need not possess every possible source. It must show why the searched and selected domain is adequate for the bounded target claim and where it is not.

#### Heterogeneous sources

Heterogeneity is not itself a defect. A Path may require configurations, transitions, Non-Events, branch records, and asymmetry-bearing occurrences. The defect arises when heterogeneity is hidden, type boundaries are erased, or no formation relation explains why the sources belong in one target.

```text
heterogeneous sources
≠ invalid composition automatically

untyped heterogeneity
≠ formed composite
```

#### `C15-SRC-01` — heterogeneous sources with preserved typing

A local access-reform candidate uses four sources:

1. a configuration describing the original authorization structure;
2. a `Λ` occurrence documenting that a scheduled accessibility review did not occur;
3. a blocked branch in which an alternative authorization route was prepared but could not be entered during the relevant window;
4. a previously warranted subpath describing the later introduction of a temporary workaround.

The proposed composite treats the four sources as jointly relevant to one bounded Path claim. It does not retype them. The missed review remains `Λ`; the blocked route remains a Chapter-13 branch-status finding; the workaround remains a prior composite source with its own Loss and uncertainty; the original configuration remains the baseline.

The operation record carries each source's lineage and bounded status forward. It does not claim that the branch was rejected, that the `Λ` caused the blockage, or that the workaround became an attractor.

```text
heterogeneous typed inputs
+ preserved lineage
≠ operator fusion
```

At the source-object stage, the candidate is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. Formation and constitutive load remain to be established in WP2 and WP3.

<a id="15-4-selection-rule"></a>

### 15.4 Selection Rule

Selection is an explicit analytical decision constrained by source support, target relevance, and claim scope. It is not passive discovery.

```text
selection
≠ discovery
```

An operation record must disclose:

- why each included source is included;
- which target relation it is expected to support;
- whether it is constitutive, supporting, illustrative, redundant, or contested;
- why each materially relevant omitted source is omitted;
- what alternative selections were considered;
- which selection disputes change the target boundary or claim;
- what Loss follows from inclusion and omission.

#### Selection cannot be endpoint-driven

A selection rule is inadmissible when it begins with a desired endpoint and retains only sources that make the endpoint appear coherent, inevitable, successful, harmful, legitimate, or functionally stable.

```text
desired target label
→ source filtering
= endpoint-driven selection pressure
```

Selection must remain answerable to contrary and boundary-changing material. A source that complicates the target is not removable merely because it weakens narrative clarity.

#### Load-bearing, supporting, illustrative, and redundant sources

The operation should distinguish at least four source roles where relevant:

- **load-bearing** — removal or alteration is expected to change target identity, class, boundary, or claim;
- **supporting** — strengthens or clarifies the same relation without independently constituting it;
- **illustrative** — makes the relation easier to inspect but does not materially alter it;
- **redundant** — duplicates already sufficient support within the declared claim.

These local roles are provisional until counterfactual-sensitivity testing in Section 15.14. They are not new canonical classes.

#### Omission and exclusion

An omitted source is not automatically irrelevant, false, or disproven. The selection record must distinguish:

- outside the declared temporal or contextual scope;
- duplicate support;
- insufficiently linked to the target;
- materially contested;
- unavailable;
- excluded for a declared bounded reason;
- improperly omitted.

The later Loss account records the consequence. Selection cannot hide exclusion inside silence.

#### Competing selections

Where two source selections remain materially supportable and produce different target boundaries, the correct result may be:

- two competing compositions;
- `admissible_but_provisional` for each bounded candidate;
- `claim_reduction_required`;
- or `non_capture` where adjudication would require destructive simplification.

```text
one selected source set
≠ uniquely best composition automatically
```

#### `C15-SEL-01` — one contested transition changes the target boundary

A candidate Path reconstructs the recovery of a distributed service after a failure. The agreed sources establish:

1. service interruption;
2. temporary manual processing;
3. restoration of the central system;
4. later retirement of the workaround.

One transition is contested: a source set indicates that regional units regained independent processing authority before central restoration, while another source family treats that authority as informal and non-operative.

Including the transition yields a composite with two recovery phases: **regional operational recovery** followed by **central technical restoration**. Excluding it yields one later recovery boundary at central restoration. The difference changes the target boundary and the roles attributed to the workaround.

The analyst may not silently choose the cleaner single-boundary account. The record must preserve the contested transition, state the source conflict, and either carry two bounded compositions or retain a provisional target with explicit uncertainty.

```text
contested inclusion
→ changed target boundary
→ competing composition pressure
```

A suitable local result is `admissible_but_provisional`, not because both accounts are equally true, but because the available sources do not yet warrant a unique selection.

<a id="15-5-temporal-ordering-rule"></a>

### 15.5 Temporal Ordering Rule

A source set becomes temporally composable only when the claimed ordering is warranted at the granularity required by the target. Ordering is not identical to the sequence in which records are narrated, discovered, displayed, or entered into a graph.

```text
narrative sequence
≠ warranted temporal or structural order
```

#### Permissible ordering forms

A Chapter-15 occurrence may use:

- linear order;
- partial order;
- overlapping intervals;
- parallel subpaths;
- synchronized transitions;
- bounded recurrent order;
- uncertain order;
- retrospectively declared periodization;
- structural priority where temporal priority is not claimed.

The ordering rule must state which form is used and why it is adequate for the target claim.

#### Linear order is a claim

A line should not be imposed merely because it is easy to read. To represent sources as:

```text
A → B → C
```

is to claim more than that A, B, and C occurred. It may claim that B followed A at the relevant granularity, that C followed B, and that the sequence is material to the target. Where only some relations are supported, the record should retain a partial order.

```text
A before C
B before C
A/B relation unresolved
```

is not analytically inferior to a false line. It is the more traceable structure.

#### Overlap and parallelism

Parallel activity does not eliminate composability. It changes the formation burden. The record must declare:

- which subpaths overlap;
- whether they interact or merely coexist;
- what synchronization point, dependency, or shared configuration relates them;
- whether one subpath is necessary for the target;
- which cross-order relations remain uncertain.

A graph may render parallel paths. Its layout must not silently convert visual left-to-right placement into temporal precedence.

#### Retrospective periodization

Later knowledge may support a bounded periodization of earlier material, but the record must distinguish:

- historical order supported at the time;
- order reconstructed from later sources;
- analyst-imposed period boundaries;
- later events used only to mark an endpoint;
- later information that would improperly alter earlier availability or meaning.

```text
retrospective ordering aid
≠ permission to rewrite the historical field
```

#### Cyclic constraints and causal restraint

Some structures may contain recurrent or mutually constraining relations. Chapter 15 may represent recurrence or feedback-sensitive order where sources support it. It may not infer a closed causal loop merely because the diagram is cyclic.

```text
cyclic graph
≠ causal-cycle proof
```

#### `C15-ORDER-01` — parallel subpaths without a false line

A program transition required two subpaths:

- **technical subpath:** platform configuration, interface testing, and activation;
- **practice subpath:** role reassignment, training, and local procedure adoption.

The sources establish that both began after authorization and that full operation began only after both reached declared readiness conditions. They do not establish whether interface testing preceded role reassignment or whether training preceded final configuration. Several activities overlapped.

A false linear representation would choose one of many unsupported orders:

```text
configuration → training → testing → reassignment → activation
```

The warranted ordering is instead partial:

```text
authorization
→ technical subpath
→ readiness_T

and

authorization
→ practice subpath
→ readiness_P

readiness_T + readiness_P
→ full operation
```

The composite may preserve the synchronization condition without deciding every internal cross-order relation. A suitable local mapping is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. Forcing a total line would require `claim_reduction_required` or `failed_transformation` for that ordering claim.

<a id="15-6-composition-frame"></a>

### 15.6 Composition Frame

Every `COMPOSE` occurrence operates within a declared analytical frame. The frame identifies the reference object and the contextual boundaries under which the selected sources are treated as one candidate composite.

A minimum frame declaration includes:

- reference object;
- source frame;
- target-object frame;
- temporal scope;
- included roles, institutions, systems, and contexts;
- excluded roles, institutions, systems, and contexts;
- source granularity and relative level;
- target granularity and relative level;
- Claim Boundary;
- Claim Ceiling.

```text
frame declaration
≠ formation rule
```

A coarser frame may make several sources visible together. It does not by itself form them into a target.

```text
coarser frame
≠ composite object automatically
```

#### Source frame and target-object frame

The source frame states how each input was originally identified and warranted. The target-object frame states the bounded context in which those sources are proposed as one composite. A frame change is permitted only when it is explicit and does not rewrite source identity.

For example, local authorization transitions may be composed within a regional access frame. They do not become regional merely by relabeling. The operation must show how local occurrences jointly bear on the regional reference object and what local distinctions are preserved or lost.

#### Frame, granularity, and relative level remain distinct

```text
frame
≠ granularity
≠ relative level
```

A composition may keep the same frame while moving to coarser granularity. It may change frame while retaining similar temporal granularity. It may project sources to a different relative level only as part of an explicitly controlled operation and without assigning a contextual target function.

The record must disclose each change separately. Otherwise frame shift can hide aggregation, retyping, or authority inflation.

#### Same sources, different bounded composites

The same source set may support more than one composition under different declared frames. This does not make the targets interchangeable. Each is a new testable claim with its own:

- reference object;
- selection relevance;
- formation burden;
- target boundary;
- Loss;
- uncertainty;
- claim ceiling.

```text
same sources
+ different frame
→ different testable composition claims
```

A later function claim still requires `PROJECT_AS`. A frame-bound target object is not a frame function.

#### Frame conflict and Non-Capture

Where the sources support materially different frames and no frame can be selected without importing authority or erasing relevant load, the correct result may be competing compositions or `non_capture`. The analyst may not choose a frame solely because it yields a more unified target.

#### `C15-FRAME-01` — the same sources under two bounded frames

A source set documents a service transition:

- local offices adopted temporary procedures;
- a central review window elapsed without decision;
- one alternative branch was blocked by authorization limits;
- the central platform was later activated;
- local workarounds remained active for several months.

Under a **service-continuity frame**, the sources support a Path composite focused on how access was maintained, degraded, restored, and redistributed across channels. The local workarounds and blocked branch are load-bearing.

Under a **governance-response frame**, the same sources support a different bounded composite focused on review, authorization, non-decision, and the relation between central and local roles. The central review `Λ` and authorization structure are load-bearing; some service-level variation is compressed.

Neither target automatically replaces the other. Neither is a contextual frame function. Each requires its own selection, formation, Loss, and claim record.

```text
same source field
+ service-continuity frame
→ service-continuity composite candidate

same source field
+ governance-response frame
→ governance-response composite candidate
```

The local result is `admissible_but_provisional` for both candidates, pending formation and Loss testing. Treating the two composites as one universal transformation would exceed the traceable frame load.

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 15.1–15.6 have established the operation-entry side of PATH-specific `COMPOSE`:

- operation purpose requires new praxeological discrimination rather than convenience;
- preconditions are conjunctive and non-compensatory;
- source objects require identity, typing, lineage, support, and source-set boundaries;
- selection is explicit, contestable, and separated from discovery;
- ordering may be linear, partial, overlapping, parallel, recurrent, uncertain, or retrospectively periodized, but must not be imposed by narrative or graph layout;
- the composition frame is explicit and remains distinct from granularity, level, formation, target function, and authority;
- rival composition and no-composition routes remain available.

All six assigned WP1 Pressure Cases are represented once in the canonical chapter prose.

These cases are methodological chapter tests and later `03_cases/*` seeds. They are not empirical findings, produced case artifacts, calibration data, training data, completed Reader graphs, or application outputs.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP1 repair requirement. It preserves:

- WP2 formation and five-part Loss;
- WP3 composition claims, no-retyping, sensitivity, failure, record, Stop, and Non-Capture;
- WP4 integrated audit and Provisional Lock;
- Chapter 16 PATH boundaries;
- Chapter 17 integrated cases;
- Chapter 24 `DECOMPOSE`;
- RETYPE and `PROJECT_AS`;
- Chapter 48 general Loss ontology;
- later case production, appendices, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP1 completion
≠ Chapter 15 provisional lock
≠ completed formation test
≠ lossless composition
≠ established Path, Trajectory, or Path Dependence automatically
≠ completed DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 15 — WP2:
Formation Rule,
Preserved Structure,
Compressed Structure,
Excluded Structure,
and Uncertain and Irrecoverable Loss
```

<a id="15-7-formation-rule"></a>

### 15.7 Formation Rule

A formation rule states why the selected and ordered source structures constitute a new analytical object rather than a collection, chronology, archive, or display.

```text
selected sources
+ warranted order
+ declared frame
≠ formed composite automatically
```

Formation requires a relation that is constitutive for the target object and a new praxeological discrimination that the sources do not provide when treated only as separate items.

```text
collection
+ label
≠ formation
```

The formation rule must declare:

- the proposed target object and target-object class;
- the relation or relations that unify the sources;
- the source elements treated as constitutive;
- the source elements treated as supporting, illustrative, redundant, contested, or exchangeable;
- the target boundary and the conditions under which it changes;
- the new praxeological distinction produced by the composite;
- the source trace through which the claimed target remains reconstructible;
- the stronger target classes that remain unwarranted;
- the rival composition or no-composition option where materially relevant.

#### Formation is claim-relative

The same source field may support a Sequence under one claim and fail to support a Path under another. Formation is therefore not an all-or-nothing property of the archive. It is tested relative to a declared target class and bounded composition claim.

```text
formed Sequence
≠ formed Path automatically

formed Path
≠ formed Trajectory automatically

formed Trajectory
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

The target-object class carries its own threshold:

- a **Sequence** requires warranted ordering beyond a list;
- a **Path** requires actual traversal, selective reconstruction, constitutive relations, alternatives where relevant, and Loss;
- a **Trajectory** additionally requires sedimentation and historical load;
- a **branch structure** requires historically available alternatives and their bounded statuses;
- a **phase** requires a declared boundary and a formation rule that makes the grouped interval analytically discriminating;
- another composite family must state its identity without creating a new PMS primitive.

A lower target class may be admissible when a stronger target class fails.

#### Constitutive relations

Constitutive relations may be temporal, structural, configurational, branching, asymmetrical, binding, residual, or mixed. Their presence must be source-supported and claim-relevant. Mere adjacency, co-location, common vocabulary, or shared ownership does not make a relation constitutive.

```text
co-occurrence
≠ constitutive relation

shared label
≠ composite identity
```

Examples of potentially constitutive relations include:

- a transition that changes the configuration in which later events occur;
- a `Λ` occurrence whose non-realization preserves or redirects the path;
- an `Ω` distribution that changes who can traverse an alternative;
- a `Ψ` binding that makes a later transition costly or condition-dependent;
- a branch point that explains why one continuation became realized and another blocked or lost;
- a synchronization condition that joins parallel subpaths into one bounded operation.

The rule must not fuse source types. An operator-typed occurrence remains typed as before; the new object arises from their relation, not from retroactive reclassification.

#### Formation hypothesis and formation finding

Before Loss and counterpressure are complete, the analyst may state a **formation hypothesis**. It becomes a formation finding only after the source trace, target threshold, preservation, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, irrecoverability, and later WP3 sensitivity tests are satisfied.

```text
formation hypothesis
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence
```

A graph may assist by rendering candidate relations. It cannot establish them. A connected component, dense cluster, or visually stable target node is not a formation finding.

```text
connected graph
≠ constituted composite
```

#### Target boundary and internal heterogeneity

A composite must be bounded enough that the analyst can state what belongs to it, what remains outside it, and why. The boundary may be temporal, configurational, role-based, institutional, spatial, or mixed. It may remain provisional where sources are incomplete.

Internal heterogeneity need not disappear. A valid composite may contain conflicting subpaths, uneven exposure, unresolved order, competing frames, or mixed event and Non-Event structures. The formation rule must explain why those differences belong to the target rather than averaging them away.

```text
internal heterogeneity
≠ formation failure automatically

unity
≠ homogeneity
```

#### `C15-FORM-01` — formation beyond an archive bundle

A regional access transition is documented through:

1. local offices adopting temporary intake procedures;
2. a central review window closing without the expected authorization decision;
3. unequal local capacity to maintain the temporary procedures;
4. a later central activation;
5. continued workarounds in offices whose local systems could not immediately connect.

A source bundle can list all five elements. A formed Path candidate requires more. The formation rule states that the target is the **access-transition Path from temporary local substitution to uneven central integration**. Its constitutive relations are:

- the temporary procedures preserve access while changing local workload;
- the review `Λ` extends the temporary configuration beyond the expected window;
- unequal capacity changes which offices can remain functional;
- central activation changes the formal configuration but does not immediately remove local workarounds;
- the later mixed state is therefore related to the earlier temporary structure rather than a wholly separate episode.

Removing the review `Λ` changes the warranted duration and the accumulated workload claim. Removing unequal local capacity changes the target boundary from uneven integration to generic rollout. The target is more than a chronology because it discriminates how access was preserved, delayed, redistributed, and only partially integrated.

The local formation finding is `admissible_but_provisional`. It supports a bounded Path candidate. It does not yet establish a Trajectory, strong Path Dependence, a macro-event function, or completed operation passage.

### 15.8 Preserved Structure

Preservation is the duty to retain the source load necessary for reconstructing the target object and evaluating the composition claim.

```text
preserved
≠ copied without change
≠ complete retention
≠ lossless
```

A structure may be preserved through direct representation, an explicit relation, a lineage pointer, a bounded summary with recoverable detail, or another traceable carrier. The decisive question is whether the target remains reconstructibly dependent on the source feature claimed to constitute it.

#### Minimum preservation burden

Where relevant to the target, a Chapter-15 composition preserves:

- source identity, origin type, and lineage;
- source frame, granularity, relative level, and uncertainty;
- warranted temporal or structural order;
- load-bearing configurations and transitions;
- central events and expectation-grounded Non-Events;
- asymmetry and exposure distributions;
- binding and release conditions;
- branch points, rejected, blocked, aborted, deferred, and lost alternatives;
- positive sub-events inside higher-level `Λ` occurrences;
- internal heterogeneity necessary to the claim;
- the relation between source structures and the target boundary;
- inherited Loss and known source gaps.

Not every source detail must appear in the composite. Preservation is claim-relative. A service-continuity Path may preserve local workload asymmetry that a narrower authorization Sequence need not. The latter must not claim to preserve what it deliberately leaves outside its target.

#### Preservation by lineage

A compressed composite may preserve a source feature through an external source trace rather than by reproducing all detail internally. This is permissible where:

- the lineage pointer is stable;
- the compression rule is declared;
- the omitted detail remains reconstructible from available sources;
- the target claim does not depend on distinctions removed by compression;
- uncertainty and inaccessible material remain visible.

```text
external recoverability
≠ internal retention
```

The Loss record must still distinguish what the composite itself preserves from what remains recoverable only through external lineage.

#### Preservation of Non-Events

A preserved `Λ` occurrence retains, as relevant:

- expected occurrence;
- expectation relation and frame;
- expected window or condition;
- support for non-realization;
- positive sub-events;
- affected roles and alternatives;
- later residue or carrier;
- uncertainty.

Translating the structure only as “continued operation” or “status quo” is insufficient where the expected but unrealized transition is constitutive.

```text
positive continuity label
≠ preserved Λ structure
```

#### Preservation of asymmetry and bindings

A chronology may remain correct while destroying the praxeological object if it removes who bore costs, who could access an alternative, what commitments constrained later action, or what release conditions remained unmet.

```text
chronology preserved
≠ praxeological load preserved
```

The operation must therefore state whether distributions and bindings are retained at the target granularity or compressed into a form that changes the claim.

#### `C15-PRES-01` — preserving load rather than dates alone

A proposed composition summarizes a transition as:

```text
pilot → review → rollout → stabilization
```

The dates are correct. The source materials also show:

- the review decision did not occur within its warranted window;
- two local units carried the temporary workload during the delay;
- one unit could exit the workaround after rollout while the other remained bound by a legacy interface;
- a previously available phased alternative became unavailable before central rollout.

A date-only composite preserves chronology but destroys the review `Λ`, the `Ω` workload distribution, the `Ψ`-like interface binding, and the lost branch. It therefore cannot carry the proposed Path claim.

A revised formation preserves those structures explicitly while compressing routine weekly updates. The local result is `claim_reduction_required` for the original date-only target and `admissible_but_provisional` for the revised bounded Path candidate.

### 15.9 Compressed Structure

Compression reduces resolution while retaining a declared representation of the compressed material and its relation to the target.

```text
compression
≠ erasure
≠ falsification
≠ irrecoverability automatically
```

A compression rule must state:

- what material is compressed;
- why finer resolution is unnecessary for the bounded claim;
- which distinctions remain represented;
- which distinctions are lost at the target level;
- whether the underlying detail remains recoverable through lineage;
- how compression affects uncertainty and counterfactual sensitivity;
- when re-expansion through a later `DECOMPOSE` occurrence might be attempted without presuming restoration.

#### Common compression forms

Permissible forms may include:

- grouping repeated similar occurrences;
- reducing temporal resolution;
- summarizing parallel minor subpaths;
- representing a distribution by bounded categories rather than every local value;
- collapsing non-constitutive administrative steps;
- periodizing a long sequence into source-supported phases;
- carrying a branch family through a bounded status summary.

Each form remains claim-relative. A detail that is non-constitutive for one target may be constitutive for another.

#### Compression and reversibility

The existence of a source trace may make detail externally recoverable, but it does not make the composite internally reversible.

```text
DECOMPOSE(COMPOSE(X))
≠ X
```

Compression may alter grouping, boundaries, salience, and relation structure. A later `DECOMPOSE` occurrence must work from the composite, lineage, new sources, and a new reconstruction question. It cannot be treated as automatic inversion.

#### Resolution neutrality

Finer resolution has no automatic truth priority. A compressed representation may be fully adequate for a bounded claim where it preserves the constitutive relation. Conversely, added detail cannot repair a missing formation rule.

```text
finer resolution
≠ stronger warrant automatically

more detail
≠ less Loss automatically
```

Where two resolutions preserve the same claim-relevant structure and neither gains praxeological purchase, the local result may be `resolution_neutral`.

#### Compression ceiling

Compression becomes inadmissible where it:

- removes a constitutive transition or Non-Event;
- averages away materially different exposure or cost;
- collapses contested alternatives into one line;
- conceals uncertainty as settled order;
- converts a mixed or partial state into a punctual event;
- makes the source path non-reconstructible;
- preserves the target label after the target's constitutive structure has disappeared.

Such compression may exceed the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling even when the resulting diagram is elegant.

#### `C15-COMP-01` — bounded compression of repeated local transitions
