Thirty local offices move through the same declared technical activation sequence over four months. The sources show three materially different groups:

- offices that activate directly;
- offices that require a temporary workaround;
- offices whose activation is delayed by authorization or interface constraints.

The proposed regional composite compresses individual daily logs into three source-linked transition classes and four bounded time windows. It preserves:

- the three materially different paths;
- the timing range of each group;
- authorization and interface constraints;
- local workload differences;
- the source identifiers needed to recover each office record.

It compresses routine status messages, repeated successful checks, and exact intra-day timing. These details remain externally recoverable and do not alter the regional Path claim.

The result is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. It would become `claim_reduction_required` if the three classes were collapsed into a single “regional activation event,” because that punctualization would destroy the differing paths and loads.

### 15.10 Excluded Structure

Excluded structure is material deliberately kept outside the target composition. Exclusion must be explicit, reasoned, and separated from absence, uncertainty, compression, and disproof.

```text
excluded
≠ false
≠ irrelevant in every frame
≠ absent from the source domain
```

The record must state:

- the excluded source or source family;
- whether it was searched, found, unavailable, or outside the declared source domain;
- why it does not belong to the target formation;
- whether it may matter under another frame or target class;
- whether exclusion is contested;
- how exclusion changes the target boundary and Claim Ceiling;
- whether the material remains accessible for a rival composition.

#### Exclusion versus omission

An omission may be accidental, undeclared, or discovered only through audit. Exclusion is an explicit analytical act. Undeclared omission cannot be converted into legitimate exclusion after the target has been formed without opening a new testable composition claim.

```text
undeclared omission
≠ controlled exclusion
```

#### Exclusion versus compression

Compressed material remains represented at reduced resolution. Excluded material is outside the target representation.

```text
compressed
→ represented with reduced resolution

excluded
→ outside the target construction
```

If excluded material is necessary to test the target's formation, exclusion may be inadmissible even when the material is not included in the final composite. Counterpressure and rival composition evidence cannot be removed merely because they do not belong inside the target.

#### Frame-bound exclusion

A source may be legitimately excluded from one composition and load-bearing in another. This follows from the Chapter-15 frame rule and does not imply inconsistency.

For example, promotional communications may be excluded from a narrowly technical activation Path while remaining material to a public-expectation or governance-response composition. The record must state the frame-bound reason rather than call the material globally irrelevant.

#### `C15-EXCL-01` — exclusion without disproof

A technical migration composition uses configuration changes, interface tests, authorization decisions, local workarounds, and activation logs. The archive also contains:

- a marketing campaign describing the migration as instantaneous;
- unrelated office-renovation records;
- a contested employee survey concerning perceived preparedness.

The renovation records are excluded because they have no supported constitutive relation to the migration target. The marketing campaign is excluded from the technical target object but retained as counterpressure against the public “instantaneous migration” label. The survey is not excluded as irrelevant; it remains `uncertain` for this claim because its sampling and relation to actual operational readiness are unresolved.

The resulting exclusion account is `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. Treating all three source families as false or irrelevant would exceed the claim. Including all of them without a formation relation would collapse the composition into aggregation.

### 15.11 Uncertain and Irrecoverable Loss

The final two Loss fields protect different epistemic conditions. Every Chapter-15 occurrence continues to use exactly the canonical structure:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

No sixth Loss category is permitted.

```text
uncertain
≠ irrecoverable
```

**Uncertain** material remains known as a material question but cannot be responsibly assigned, ordered, typed, or interpreted at the claimed level.

**Irrecoverable** material cannot be reconstructed from the composite and the available source lineage under the declared scope.

#### Uncertain structure

Uncertainty may concern:

- whether an occurrence happened;
- occurrence timing or order;
- source identity or completeness;
- whether a relation is constitutive;
- whether a branch was rejected, blocked, deferred, or merely unavailable;
- whether an expected occurrence was realized;
- target boundary or periodization;
- whether compressed detail changes the claim.

Uncertain material must remain visible. It may lead to:

- `admissible_but_provisional`;
- `admissible_with_bounded_claim`;
- `claim_reduction_required`;
- `resolution_neutral`;
- competing compositions;
- `mandatory_stop`;
- `non_capture`.

Its route depends on materiality and claim burden, not on a score.

```text
uncertainty declared
≠ uncertainty resolved
```

#### Irrecoverable loss

Irrecoverable loss may arise from the source condition, the composition procedure, or both.

**Source-inherited irrecoverability** may include:

- destroyed records;
- vanished intermediate states;
- unavailable local testimony or logs;
- alternatives whose historical content is no longer reconstructible;
- non-separable costs or exposures already lost before composition.

**Composition-induced irrecoverability** may include:

- aggregation that destroys internal variation;
- boundary choices that sever source relations;
- compression without retained lineage;
- punctualization of an extended process;
- transformation of mixed branch statuses into one settled label.

The record must distinguish these origins. `COMPOSE` may disclose source-inherited loss; it may not claim to have caused or repaired it.

```text
source-inherited irrecoverability
≠ composition-induced irrecoverability
```

#### Irrecoverability and admissibility

Irrecoverable loss does not automatically invalidate every composition. The operation may remain admissible where:

- the loss is declared;
- the target claim does not depend on the lost material;
- the remaining source trace supports the bounded target;
- no stronger claim is made;
- alternatives and Stop conditions remain visible.

It becomes disqualifying where the lost material is constitutive to the target or necessary to distinguish the target from a rival composition.

```text
irrecoverable loss present
≠ failed transformation automatically

constitutive irrecoverable loss
→ target claim under severe pressure
```

#### No residual bucket

`uncertain` and `irrecoverable` must not become catch-all fields for material the analyst has not classified. Each item requires a reason and relation to the target claim. Likewise, `excluded` cannot hide uncertainty, and `compressed` cannot hide irrecoverability.

#### `C15-UNC-01` — uncertainty retained rather than settled by composition

Two source families document a transition. One places the operational handoff before the formal authorization; the other places authorization first and treats the earlier activity as a pilot. Both support that full operation began only after both conditions were satisfied, but the internal relation of the earlier steps cannot be resolved.

The composite preserves a partial order and records the cross-order relation as uncertain. It does not use the target label to settle the dispute. The bounded Path claim remains `admissible_but_provisional`. A causal or compliance claim based on one order would require `mandatory_stop` at the available Source Ceiling.

#### `C15-IRREC-01` — constitutive local variation destroyed by aggregation

A legacy dataset retains only monthly regional totals for service interruption. The original office-level logs were destroyed before the Chapter-15 analysis. The totals establish that disruption occurred and later declined. They cannot reconstruct:

- which offices carried the disruption;
- whether the same offices remained exposed across months;
- which local workarounds were used;
- whether one branch failed everywhere or only in particular configurations.

The office-level heterogeneity is source-inherited and irrecoverable. A regional Sequence of total disruption may remain `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. A Path claim about distribution, branch traversal, or persistent local asymmetry is `failed_transformation` or `non_capture`, depending on whether rival bounded interpretations remain materially possible.

The analyst may not label the totals “complete regional recovery” merely because the aggregate returns to baseline.

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 15.7–15.11 have established the formation-and-Loss side of PATH-specific `COMPOSE`:

- the Formation Rule must produce a declared target object through source-supported constitutive relations and new praxeological discrimination;
- target-class thresholds remain graded and do not inherit upward automatically;
- preservation means reconstructible source dependence rather than total copying or losslessness;
- chronology alone does not preserve `Λ`, `Ω`, `Ψ`, branch, alternative, or internal-heterogeneity load;
- compression requires an explicit rule, bounded resolution, lineage, recoverability statement, and Traceability-Ceiling test;
- exclusion is explicit, frame-bound, reasoned, and distinct from disproof, omission, compression, uncertainty, and source absence;
- uncertainty remains visible rather than being converted into settled order or exclusion;
- irrecoverable loss is distinguished as source-inherited, composition-induced, or mixed;
- exactly the five canonical Loss fields remain in force;
- the presence of Loss does not automatically fail the operation, but concealed or constitutive Loss can require reduction, failure, Stop, or Non-Capture.

All six assigned WP2 Pressure Cases are represented once in the canonical chapter prose.

These cases are methodological chapter tests and later `03_cases/*` seeds. They are not empirical findings, produced case artifacts, calibration data, training data, finished Reader graphs, or application outputs.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP2 repair requirement. It preserves:

- WP3 composition claims, no-retyping, sensitivity, overelasticity, failure, record, Stop, and Non-Capture;
- WP4 integrated audit and Provisional Lock;
- Chapter 16 PATH boundaries;
- Chapter 17 integrated cases;
- Chapter 24 `DECOMPOSE` and non-invertibility;
- RETYPE and `PROJECT_AS`;
- Chapter 48 general Loss ontology;
- later case production, appendices, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP2 completion
≠ Chapter 15 provisional lock
≠ completed counterfactual-sensitivity test
≠ completed COMPOSE occurrence automatically
≠ lossless composition
≠ established Trajectory or Path Dependence automatically
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ authority increase
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 15 — WP3:
Composition Claim,
Composition without Retyping,
Counterfactual Sensitivity,
Composition Failure,
and COMPOSE Record
```

<a id="15-12-composition-claim"></a>

### 15.12 Composition Claim

A completed Chapter-15 analysis must state not only that sources were selected and related, but **what bounded claim the resulting composite licenses**. The target object and the claim about that object are connected but not identical.

```text
formed composite object
≠ every claim made about that composite
```

A composition claim must declare:

- the target reference object;
- the target object type;
- the operation occurrence and its source lineage;
- the Formation Rule and constitutive relations;
- the praxeological discrimination added by the composite;
- the temporal, structural, and contextual scope;
- the source and claim ceilings;
- the five-part Loss account;
- the canonical Output Class;
- any stronger claim that remains withheld.

The claim must be segmented. A single admissible `COMPOSE` occurrence may support one bounded statement and fail another. For example, selected configurations and transitions may form a source-traceable Sequence while failing to support a Path because actual traversal, branch load, or changed continuation conditions remain unresolved. A bounded Path may be warranted while Trajectory or strong Path Dependence remains unestablished.

```text
operation occurrence admissible
≠ all target-strength claims admissible
```

#### Object claim, relation claim, and strength claim

Three burdens should be distinguished:

1. **object claim** — the selected sources form one declared composite object;
2. **relation claim** — specified source relations are constitutive of that object;
3. **strength claim** — the object reaches the declared level, such as Sequence, Path, Trajectory, or another bounded composite class.

A failure at one burden does not automatically erase the others. The source field may remain valid even when formation fails. A Sequence may remain after a Path claim is reduced. A Path may remain after Trajectory or dependence claims fail.

```text
failed stronger composition claim
≠ failed source field
≠ no temporal structure automatically
```

#### Claim ceiling and non-inheritance

The claim ceiling is limited by the weakest load-bearing source, relation, ordering, boundary, or Loss condition required by the target. Supporting or illustrative sources cannot compensate for a missing constitutive relation. Formal completeness cannot raise the ceiling.

The composite inherits neither stronger truth status nor authority from its scale, coherence, duration, graph density, number of sources, or institutional importance.

```text
more sources
+ more relations
+ cleaner target
≠ higher authority
```

#### `C15-CLAIM-01` — one operation, graded composition claims

A source field documents:

- a legacy configuration;
- two partially ordered transitions;
- an expectation-grounded review `Λ`;
- a later activation transition;
- one blocked alternative whose exact blocking mechanism remains uncertain.

The Formation Rule and source trace support a composite in which the activation followed the migration work and the missed review remained relevant to final closure. The operation supports a bounded Path claim. It does not support:

- a unique total order between the two migration transitions;
- a claim that the blocked alternative was impossible;
- a Trajectory claim based on historical sedimentation;
- strong Path Dependence;
- a causal claim that the review `Λ` produced the closure.

The operation occurrence is locally `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. The Path claim is retained, while the stronger claims remain withheld or require separate testing.

### 15.13 Composition without Retyping

`COMPOSE` creates a new composite analytical object. It does not rewrite the origin types of the sources and does not assign a contextual target function.

```text
source type before COMPOSE
=
source type after COMPOSE
```

A Configuration remains a Configuration source. A Transition remains a Transition. A `Λ` occurrence remains a Non-Event occurrence. An `Ω`-bearing occurrence retains its asymmetry typing. A prior Path used as a source remains a derived Path object. Their roles in formation may change; their types do not.

The resulting composite receives its own declared object type. This new object typing does not propagate backward into the components.

```text
new composite type
≠ retroactive source retyping
```

#### Analytical role is not target function

A source may be constitutive, supporting, illustrative, exchangeable, contested, compressed, or excluded relative to one composition. These are local analytical roles, not new PMS operator types and not RETYPE target functions.

Likewise, a resulting Path may later be projected as a frame, macro-event, attractor, constraint, or other contextual function only through a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence with preserved origin type, bounded context, continuity, Loss, and Claim Ceiling.

```text
COMPOSE target object
≠ PROJECT_AS target function
```

Chapter 15 therefore prohibits outputs such as:

- “the composed Path is now a frame”;
- “the composite is a macro-event by scale”;
- “the Trajectory functions as an attractor automatically”;
- “the aggregate asymmetry is a higher-level operator”;
- “the composite inherits the authority of all sources.”

#### Composite of composites

A prior composite may be used as a source in a later `COMPOSE` occurrence. Its prior Formation Rule, Loss, uncertainty, and lineage remain inherited burdens. Nesting does not reset them.

```text
composite used as source
≠ primitive
≠ loss reset
≠ authority reset upward
```

The later record must show whether the prior composite is load-bearing as a whole or whether particular internal relations must remain independently traceable. A convenient nested label cannot hide constitutive detail required by the new target.

#### `C15-RETYPE-01` — formed Path mislabelled as a frame

A valid `COMPOSE` occurrence forms a bounded service-transition Path from typed configurations, transitions, one review `Λ`, and a lost alternative. A later report describes the Path as “the governing frame” for all subsequent decisions without a separate operation record, context boundary, or continuity test.

The composition itself remains admissible. The automatic frame assignment does not.

```text
admissible Path composite
+ unsupported frame-function assignment
→ composition retained
→ retyping claim rejected
```

The correct local output for the frame claim is `failed_transformation` or `claim_reduction_required`, depending on whether a bounded descriptive use remains available. The original Path sources and object types are unchanged.

### 15.14 Counterfactual Sensitivity

Counterfactual Sensitivity tests whether the sources and relations claimed as constitutive actually carry the target object and claim. It is a bounded analytical pressure test, not a free counterfactual history and not a causal experiment.

For each claimed constitutive element or relation, the analyst asks:

> If this element were removed, exchanged, reordered, compressed differently, or interpreted under a rival supported frame, would the target boundary, target type, Formation Rule, or licensed claim materially change?

The variation must remain tied to the source field and declared operation. It may test:

- removal of one source occurrence;
- substitution by a source-supported alternative;
- reversal or loosening of one ordering relation;
- exclusion of one claimed constitutive source;
- reclassification of one source from constitutive to supporting;
- restoration of compressed detail where lineage permits;
- rival periodization or target boundary;
- rival composition frame;
- no-composition.

```text
counterfactual composition test
≠ invented alternative history
≠ causal necessity proof
```

#### Constitutive, supporting, and exchangeable load

A source or relation is **constitutive for the declared claim** when its bounded removal or exchange changes the target's identity, boundary, type, or licensed claim. A source may be supporting without being constitutive. Another may be exchangeable with a source-supported equivalent under the same Formation Rule.

```text
important source
≠ constitutive source automatically

constitutive for one claim
≠ constitutive for every claim
```

Constitutive status remains claim-relative. A missed review may be constitutive to a Path about closure conditions but only supporting to a Sequence of activation dates.

#### Overelasticity

A composition is overelastic when the target label and claim remain unchanged under changes that should be material if the stated Formation Rule were genuine.

Overelasticity pressure includes:

- removal of a claimed constitutive transition without target change;
- exchange of a `Λ` occurrence for a positive event without claim change;
- reversal of a claimed constitutive order without claim change;
- removal of branch or asymmetry load without claim change;
- large boundary shifts while the same target remains asserted;
- contradictory frames yielding the same supposedly precise object.

```text
same target after constitutive removal
→ Formation Rule under pressure
```

Overelasticity may show that:

- the target is merely a macro-label;
- the source was supporting rather than constitutive;
- the Formation Rule is too weak;
- the claim must be reduced;
- rival compositions remain unresolved;
- no-composition is preferable.

It does not automatically prove that every target label is invalid. The analyst may revise the constitutive set and rerun the operation as a new testable claim.

#### Sensitivity without determinism

A finding that an element is constitutive does not establish that it caused the outcome, that no other historical route could have produced a similar object, or that future removal will reverse the composite.

```text
composition sensitivity
≠ causal necessity
≠ historical inevitability
≠ reversal guarantee
```

#### `C15-CF-01` — removal exposes an overelastic target

A proposed “institutional recovery Path” is formed from:

- a transition to a temporary workaround;
- a missed repair review;
- two local restoration transitions;
- a later central reopening;
- one documented branch that would have preserved local capacity.

The record calls every element constitutive. Sensitivity testing removes the missed review and the lost branch, reverses the order of the two local restorations, and narrows the period boundary. The target label and full claim remain word-for-word unchanged.

The result exposes overelasticity. A narrower Sequence of restoration events may remain admissible. The stronger “institutional recovery Path” requires `claim_reduction_required` or a new Formation Rule. The test does not establish what would actually have happened under another history.

### 15.15 Composition Failure

A Chapter-15 `COMPOSE` candidate fails when the required operation burdens cannot support the declared target or claim within the Admissibility Band.

Common failure modes include:

- source objects are missing, untyped, or mutually incommensurable under the proposed frame;
- the Selection Rule is endpoint-driven, undisclosed, or materially arbitrary;
- the ordering rule forces unsupported linearity;
- the composition frame conflates reference object, granularity, relative level, or target function;
- the Formation Rule adds no discrimination beyond a list or label;
- claimed constitutive relations are unsupported or overelastic;
- a target-class threshold is not met;
- claim-relevant `Λ`, `Ω`, `Ψ`, branch, alternative, or heterogeneity load is erased;
- compression exceeds the Traceability Ceiling;
- exclusion hides counterpressure or uncertainty;
- constitutive information is irrecoverable;
- the claim exceeds the weakest load-bearing source or relation;
- the operation is used to assign a target function or higher authority.

```text
formal completion
≠ operation success
```

#### Controlled reduction ladder

Failure of the proposed target does not force erasure of the source field. The result should be reduced to the strongest warranted object and claim:

```text
Trajectory claim
→ bounded Path
→ Sequence
→ ordered or partially ordered source field
→ source collection
→ no-composition
```

The exact route depends on what remains supported. The canonical Output Classes remain the only output vocabulary.

Possible mappings include:

- `admissible` — all declared burdens are supported at the licensed scope;
- `admissible_with_bounded_claim` — composition succeeds but stronger claims are withheld;
- `admissible_but_provisional` — material uncertainty remains without defeating the bounded target;
- `resolution_neutral` — a finer or coarser composition remains equally warranted for the claim;
- `partially_admissible` — some source subsets or claim segments compose while others fail;
- `claim_reduction_required` — a weaker target or claim is supported;
- `failed_transformation` — the declared composite cannot be formed;
- `mandatory_stop` — continued use would exceed source, claim, type, function, or authority boundaries;
- `non_capture` — rival compositions remain materially supported and cannot responsibly be collapsed.

`analogy_only` remains available where a proposed composition is only structurally suggestive and does not warrant object formation.

#### Failure inheritance and re-entry

A failed claim remains failed at its original scope. A new frame, boundary, granularity, selection, Formation Rule, target type, or target function creates a new testable claim; it does not retroactively repair the earlier one.

```text
new composition
= new testable claim

later successful composition
≠ retroactive repair of failed composition
```

#### `C15-FAIL-01` — chronology survives, Path formation fails

An archive supports six dated administrative actions but does not establish:

- whether three actions were implemented;
- whether the actions affected the same reference object;
- whether a missed review was expected under the governing frame;
- whether the final state was reached through those actions or through an external change.

The chronology is valid. A connected Path claim is not. The result is `claim_reduction_required`: retain an ordered event record and unresolved relation field. If the analyst insists on a Path or causal story, the result becomes `failed_transformation` or `mandatory_stop`.

### 15.16 COMPOSE Record

A Chapter-15 operation occurrence uses the Shared Transformation Record. It does not create a second record architecture. The existing schema already provides the operation-specific `composeDetails` fields and the shared source, target, claim, loss, alternatives, uncertainty, governance, audit, output, and extension carriers.

The operation-specific core requires:

```yaml
operation:
  type: COMPOSE
  selection_rule:
  compose_details:
    source_objects:
    source_typings:
    ordering_rule:
    formation_rule:
    constitutive_relations:
    source_trace:
    selection_rule_pointer: /operation/selection_rule
    target_reference_object_pointer: /target/reference_object
    target_object_typing_pointer: /target/object_typing
```

The full record must also carry, where applicable:

- transformation and operation-occurrence identity;
- source references, frames, granularity, relative level, lineage, evidence, and uncertainty;
- target reference object, object typing, frame, granularity, relative level, and bounded claim;
- the five canonical Loss fields;
- rival composition and no-composition alternatives;
- counterfactual-sensitivity results;
- claim segmentation and Claim Ceiling;
- canonical Output Class and local rationale;
- Stop, Non-Capture, governance, and authority boundaries;
- references to prior and downstream operation records.

```text
schema-valid COMPOSE record
≠ substantively admissible COMPOSE occurrence
```

#### Record consistency duties

The top-level Selection Rule and target identity/type remain authoritative at their registered pointers. The operation-specific block may not silently restate them differently. Source typings must correspond to the declared source objects. Constitutive relations must point to traceable sources. The target claim must not exceed the Loss and uncertainty declarations.

Where one record carries several claim segments, each segment must be separately auditable. A successful Sequence claim cannot silently validate a failed Path claim.

#### `C15-REC-01` — complete record with bounded operation result

A Shared Transformation Record declares five typed sources, a partial ordering rule, one Formation Rule, four constitutive relations, complete source trace, a bounded Path target, five-part Loss, one rival Sequence composition, one no-composition route, and sensitivity results.

The record is schema-valid. Substantive review finds that one branch-status relation remains unresolved but is not required by the bounded Path claim. The operation maps to `admissible_but_provisional`. A stronger branch-closure claim remains `non_capture`.

The record's completeness supports auditability. It does not resolve the branch status or generate the result automatically.

#### Mandatory Stop

`mandatory_stop` applies where continued composition or use would:

- conceal source absence, uncertainty, exclusion, or irrecoverability;
- convert a source collection into a Path by label or graph layout;
- force unsupported order or causality;
- erase a constitutive Non-Event, asymmetry, binding, branch, or alternative;
- treat sensitivity as proof of causal necessity;
- use `COMPOSE` to assign a target function;
- type or diagnose a person;
- support sanction, legitimacy, irreversible classification, prediction, or application authority beyond the sources;
- reuse a known failed claim under a new label without a new record.

##### `C15-STOP-01` — composite used for automatic sanction

A graph-based composition combines correspondence, delays, role assignments, and missed deadlines into a “non-compliance Path.” The record omits expectation frames for several claimed `Λ` occurrences, treats all delays as intentional, and uses the target to trigger sanctions against named persons.

The operation must stop. Missing expectation warrant, person-level intention inference, function inflation, and sanction use exceed the operation and authority ceilings. The result is `mandatory_stop`. A bounded chronology or structural role-configuration analysis may be re-entered only as a new claim.

#### Non-Capture

`non_capture` applies where materially different compositions remain source-supported and the available material cannot responsibly privilege one target boundary, Formation Rule, ordering, or constitutive set.

Non-Capture is not a weak compromise. It preserves a real limit of the source field.

##### `C15-NC-01` — two irreducible compositions remain

One source family supports a Path organized around technical activation and local workaround persistence. Another supports a Path organized around authorization, responsibility transfer, and review non-realization. The two share several events but differ in boundaries, constitutive relations, and the role of the review `Λ`. Neither can be reduced to a harmless visualization layer, and the sources do not support one as analytically prior for the governing question.

The result is `non_capture`. Both bounded candidate records remain available. A single “overall transformation Path” would conceal the unresolved formation conflict.

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 15.12–15.16 have completed the claim, sensitivity, failure, and record side of PATH-specific `COMPOSE`:

- target object and composition claim remain distinct;
- object, relation, and strength claims are separately testable;
- operation success does not validate every stronger claim;
- source types remain unchanged and no target function is assigned;
- composite-of-composite use preserves prior Formation, Loss, uncertainty, and lineage burdens;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity tests constitutive load through bounded removal, exchange, reorder, recompression, reframing, rival composition, and no-composition pressure;
- overelasticity exposes targets that remain unchanged under materially constitutive variation;
- sensitivity does not establish causal necessity, inevitability, or reversibility;
- Composition Failure preserves controlled reduction, prior failure, Stop, and Non-Capture routes;
- the Shared Transformation Record and existing `composeDetails` carrier are sufficient;
- schema completeness remains distinct from substantive admissibility;
- all seven WP3 Pressure Cases are represented once in canonical prose.

These cases are methodological chapter tests and later `03_cases/*` seeds. They are not produced case artifacts, empirical findings, calibration data, training data, finished Reader graphs, automated decisions, or application outputs.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known WP3 repair requirement. It preserves:

- WP4 integrated synchronization, audit, and Provisional Lock;
- Chapter 16 PATH boundary adjudication;
- Chapter 17 integrated cases and audit;
- Chapter 24 `DECOMPOSE` and non-invertibility;
- Chapter 30 `PROJECT_AS` and RETYPE target functions;
- Chapter 48 general Loss ontology;
- later standalone case production, appendices, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP3 completion
≠ Chapter 15 provisional lock
≠ every COMPOSE candidate admissible
≠ causal necessity proof
≠ counterfactual truth
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 15 — WP4:
Synchronization,
Integrated Audit,
and Provisional Lock
```

#### Chapter 15 completion boundary

Chapter 15 has now operationalized the complete PATH-specific `COMPOSE` procedure without collapsing source plurality into formation, chronology into Path, visual connectedness into constitutive relation, composite-object formation into contextual function, or formal record completeness into substantive admissibility. The chapter retains the generic three-operation grammar from Chapter 4 and supplies the first full PATH procedure for a particular `COMPOSE` occurrence.

```text
many or sequential source structures
→ new composite analytical object
```

The Chapter 15 completion test is satisfied:

1. source objects and target object are explicitly typed, while source occurrences retain their origin types after composition;
2. operation type, operation occurrence, and resulting composite remain distinct;
3. selection, discovery, ordering, narrative sequence, Formation Rule, aggregation, composition frame, and target function remain non-equivalent;
4. the operation entry burden is conjunctive and non-compensatory: more sources, more detail, graph density, cleaner periodization, record completeness, formal elegance, or stable target labels cannot replace missing typed sources, warrant, order, frame, Formation Rule, constitutive relations, praxis purchase, source trace, Loss, or Claim Ceiling;
5. No-Composition remains a positive analytical route where a proposed target would destroy relevant distinctions, exceed the source basis, or provide no praxeological purchase;
6. source roles such as load-bearing, supporting, illustrative, redundant, contested, omitted, or excluded remain analytical roles rather than new source types or operator types;
7. a Selection Rule is explicit, contestable, target-independent, and source-traceable, with rival selections retained where they materially alter the target boundary;
8. temporal and structural ordering may be linear, partial, parallel, overlapping, recurrent, synchronized, uncertain, or retrospectively periodized only where supported; narrative order and visual left-to-right placement do not establish temporal precedence;
9. the Composition Frame declares reference object, source and target-object frames, scope, roles, contexts, granularity, relative level, claim boundary, and Claim Ceiling without functioning as the Formation Rule;
10. the Formation Rule identifies the source-supported relations through which selected and ordered structures form a new composite rather than an archive bundle, list, chronology, cluster, or macro-label;
11. target-class thresholds remain graded: Sequence, Path, Trajectory, branch structure, phase, and other bounded composites must satisfy their own requirements, and no lower target automatically inherits a stronger one;
12. constitutive relations remain claim-relative and may be temporal, configurational, transitional, branching, asymmetrical, binding, residual, or mixed without operator fusion or causal overreach;
13. preservation means reconstructible source dependence rather than copying, total retention, losslessness, or immunity from later audit;
14. compression is declared through a rule, reduced resolution, retained distinctions, lineage, external recoverability, uncertainty impact, and Traceability-Ceiling test; it is neither erasure nor automatically irrecoverable;
15. exclusion is explicit, reasoned, frame-bound, and distinct from disproof, accidental omission, compression, uncertainty, source absence, or universal irrelevance;
16. uncertainty remains visible as unresolved order, relation, occurrence, typing, boundary, or interpretation rather than being forced into order, exclusion, or a stronger target claim;
17. irrecoverability remains distinct from uncertainty and is declared as source-inherited, composition-induced, or mixed; composition-induced irrecoverability cannot be laundered as a property of the original sources;
18. exactly the five canonical Loss fields remain mandatory—`preserved`, `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, and `irrecoverable`—with no sixth category, score, probability, or compensatory aggregate;
19. object claim, constitutive-relation claim, and strength claim remain separately testable, so one admissible operation occurrence may support a bounded composite while stronger claims fail or require reduction;
20. `COMPOSE` does not retroactively retype its components, assign a frame-, macro-event-, attractor-, or other contextual target function, convert a nested composite into a primitive, reset inherited Loss or uncertainty, or confer higher authority;
21. Counterfactual Sensitivity applies bounded removal, source-supported substitution, reorder, recompression, reframing, rival composition, and No-Composition pressure to claimed constitutive elements without becoming free alternative history, causal necessity, historical inevitability, prediction, or inversion guarantee;
22. overelasticity remains visible where target identity and claim survive variations that should be material if the declared Formation Rule and constitutive set were genuine;
23. Composition Failure preserves weaker warranted findings through the controlled ladder from stronger composite claim to bounded Path, Sequence, ordered or partially ordered source field, source collection, or No-Composition;
24. a failed stronger claim does not erase valid sources, ordering, weaker object claims, or independently warranted occurrences, and every revised composition is a new testable claim;
25. the existing Shared Transformation Record and `composeDetails` carrier remain sufficient for source objects, source typings, ordering, Formation Rule, constitutive relations, source trace, target pointers, Selection Rule, complete Loss, alternatives, sensitivity, uncertainty, governance, audit, and canonical output mapping;
26. schema-validity, graph cohesion, complete source trace, record completion, or successful package validation establish auditability only and cannot determine substantive formation, constitutive status, operation success, output class, target function, causality, legitimacy, sanction, person judgment, or authority;
27. Mandatory Stop applies where a known failed or overelastic composition is reused for person-level inference, sanction, legitimacy, prediction, irreversible classification, target-function assignment, or authority inheritance;
28. Non-Capture remains available where two or more materially supported compositions are irreducible, where target identity depends on unresolved selection or frame choices, or where the source field cannot responsibly support one formation without destroying another;
29. Chapter 16 receives the complete `COMPOSE` procedure for local lower- and upper-boundary testing; Chapter 17 receives all nineteen chapter-level Pressure Cases and integrated audit duties; Chapter 24 retains `DECOMPOSE`; Chapter 48 retains the general Loss ontology; RETYPE retains `PROJECT_AS` and contextual target functions;
30. all nineteen prepared Chapter-15 Pressure Cases are represented in canonical prose without being misreported as produced `03_cases/*` artifacts, empirical evidence, calibration data, training data, finished Reader graphs, application outputs, or automatic decisions;
31. no graph, revised frame, new label, target class, operation name, extension, schema, later projection, or application context may erase a failed composition claim or inherit missing source, order, formation, sensitivity, Loss, or authority warrant;
32. no part of Chapter 15 changes `PMS.yaml`, the Δ–Ψ inventory, operator order, operator dependencies, the three-operation grammar, the ten Output Classes, the sixteen Admissibility Rules, or the twelve audit stages.

##### Integrated contract example — source-traceable Trajectory composition

A regional service network supplies four typed configurations, three warrantable transitions, one repeated review `Λ`, one asymmetry-bearing access occurrence, one binding-bearing reliance occurrence, and a later repair transition. The Selection Rule retains the review `Λ`, unequal access load, reliance relation, implementation transitions, and repair; it excludes unrelated annual-budget events from the bounded service-access frame and leaves one disputed local pilot uncertain. The ordering rule preserves two parallel local subpaths before a documented synchronization transition rather than forcing a total chronology.

The Formation Rule states that repeated missed review, uneven access costs, reliance-bearing commitments, and later partial repair jointly form a Path whose earlier structure remains carried into the present through persistent workaround cost, changed role exposure, and altered continuation possibilities. Under the Chapter-11 sedimentation threshold, this supports a bounded Trajectory candidate rather than only a Sequence or Path.

The composition declares:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
    - source type and lineage
    - parallel order and synchronization
    - review Non-Event, access asymmetry, reliance binding, and repair relation
    - present-bearing workaround cost and changed continuation corridor
  compressed:
    - repeated local implementation logs into source-linked transition classes
  excluded:
    - unrelated budget events outside the declared service-access frame
  uncertain:
    - one disputed pilot's relation to the synchronized rollout
  irrecoverable:
    - office-level variation destroyed before the current archive was assembled
```

The rival composition yields only a bounded Path if present-bearing carryover cannot be established. The No-Composition route remains correct if the parallel subpaths cannot be source-traceably related. The example therefore satisfies the required trajectory-composition burden without treating the target label, graph, duration, or source volume as automatic Trajectory proof.

The integrated audit confirms that Chapter 15 is neither an archive bundler nor a graph-cohesion engine. A connected source field is not a formed composite. A complete five-part Loss panel is not losslessness. A target that survives all material variation may be overelastic rather than robust.

```text
connected source field
≠ formed composite

complete Loss declaration
≠ lossless composition

stable label under constitutive removal
≠ robust formation automatically
```

The anti-laundering boundary remains active. A failed or reduced composition may be reopened only as a new testable claim with corrected source typing, selection, order, frame, Formation Rule, constitutive relations, target class, source trace, sensitivity, Loss, uncertainty, rival composition, No-Composition route, Claim Ceiling, or governance boundary. A new graph, macro-label, target function, formal extension, application context, or completed record cannot inherit or conceal the missing burden.

```text
new composition
= new testable claim

later PROJECT_AS success
≠ retroactive COMPOSE repair

later DECOMPOSE result
≠ proof that prior composition was lossless or invertible
```

The chapter-level result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known local Chapter-15 repair requirement. It preserves later pressure from Chapter 16 PATH Boundary Conditions, Chapter 17 integrated PATH cases and audit, Chapter 24 `DECOMPOSE`, Chapter 48 Loss, RETYPE, LIMITS, Appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and later Reader graph implementation.

```text
Chapter 15 provisional lock
≠ universal formation engine
≠ lossless composition
≠ causal proof
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

Reopen Chapter 15 only if later work demonstrates a concrete conflict involving source or target typing, Selection Rule, ordering, Composition Frame, Formation Rule, constitutive relation, target-class threshold, preservation, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, irrecoverability, five-part Loss, claim segmentation, no-retyping, Counterfactual Sensitivity, overelasticity, failure/reduction route, Shared Record mapping, Stop/Non-Capture route, Chapter-16/17/24/48 handoff, RETYPE separation, or unchanged PMS Base. Mere preference for a cleaner graph, broader target, stronger conclusion, more sources, more detail, a single total order, or less visible Loss is not a reopening ground.

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 16 — Preparation:
PATH Boundary Conditions
```

<a id="chapter-16-path-boundary-conditions"></a>

## 16. PATH Boundary Conditions

Chapters 9–15 established the temporal object chain, the thresholds for Path, Trajectory, and strong Path Dependence, the treatment of branches and Non-Events, and the complete PATH-specific `COMPOSE` procedure. Chapter 16 now asks whether a proposed temporal reconstruction remains inside the admissible PATH band.

```text
Praxeological Relevance Floor
─────────────────────────────
admissible PATH reconstruction
─────────────────────────────
Praxeological Traceability Ceiling
```

The lower boundary and upper boundary are different tests. The lower boundary asks whether temporal differentiation changes the warranted reconstruction of praxis. The upper boundary asks whether the resulting object and claim remain reconstructibly dependent on the configurations, transitions, Non-Events, asymmetries, alternatives, bindings, residuals, and historical load declared as constitutive.

```text
lower-bound passage
≠ upper-bound passage automatically

upper-bound traceability
≠ lower-bound purchase automatically
```

A chronology may remain fully source-traceable while adding no praxeological purchase. A macro-label may appear practically meaningful while no longer carrying reconstructible path load. Neither failure can be repaired by success on the other boundary.

The tests are non-compensatory. More sources, dates, pages, formal fields, graph nodes, visual polish, historical span, causal vocabulary, or confidence on one axis cannot replace a missing burden on the other.

```text
more dates
≠ stronger PATH

more abstraction
≠ more authority
```

Sections 16.1–16.5 establish the two PATH boundaries, chronology without Trajectory gain, Trajectory without path trace, and excessive temporal compression. They also execute the required same-material three-way contrast. Sections 16.6–16.11 later test artificial directionality, hidden teleology, omitted `Λ` and `Ω` load, and the PATH/SUB/RETYPE boundaries. Sections 16.12–16.15 later establish the Purchase and Trace tests, Claim Reduction, Stop, and Non-Capture. Chapter 17 retains integrated cases, countercases, confusion cases, and the local PATH lock.

<a id="16-1-the-lower-path-boundary"></a>

### 16.1 The Lower PATH Boundary

The lower PATH boundary is crossed only when temporal differentiation creates a warranted praxeological difference for the tested claim.

The question is not whether a reconstruction contains time. The question is whether its temporal order, duration, transition structure, delay, persistence, branching, or sedimented load changes what can responsibly be said about the reference object.

A candidate displays lower-bound purchase where temporal reconstruction changes at least one claim-relevant dimension such as:

- configuration identity;
- transition status or completion;
- role distribution;
- cost, exposure, or reversibility;
- binding or release conditions;
- availability of alternatives;
- persistence of residual load;
- evidential burden;
- reachability of later actions;
- an Action Corridor;
- the warranted target class or Claim Ceiling.

```text
temporal differentiation
+ changed warranted praxis reconstruction
→ lower-bound gain candidate
```

Temporal differentiation falls below the Floor where it adds dates, intervals, labels, or sequence detail but leaves the licensed reconstruction materially unchanged.

Typical lower-bound failures include:

- dates are added while roles, costs, alternatives, bindings, residuals, and Action Corridors remain unchanged;
- order is narrated but removal or reversal of that order would not affect the claim;
- transitions are enumerated without a source-supported relation;
- finer temporal detail repeats the same claim without improving discrimination;
- duration is treated as historical load without sedimentation or continuation effects;
- a timeline functions only as an index to sources;
- graph animation makes order visible but adds no analytical distinction.

#### Local boundary result

Chapter 16 reuses the existing controlled vocabulary:

```text
relevance_floor_boundary_result:
  gain
  neutral
  below_floor
  underdetermined
```

These are local boundary findings, not canonical Output Classes.

- `gain` means that the temporal differentiation materially changes the warranted reconstruction.
- `neutral` means that the added resolution or ordering neither strengthens nor weakens the tested claim.
- `below_floor` means that the proposed PATH claim depends on temporal detail that adds no praxeological purchase.
- `underdetermined` means that the available support does not permit a responsible Floor finding.

A `neutral` result is not automatically a defect. A chronology may be useful for indexing, navigation, provenance, or later testing without qualifying as a stronger PATH object. The boundary finding becomes adverse only where a stronger temporal claim depends on purchase that the chronology does not supply.

```text
neutral temporal resolution
≠ false chronology

below-floor PATH claim
≠ false source record automatically
```

#### Non-compensation at the Floor

No quantity of detail can compensate for absent praxeological purchase.

```text
more temporal resolution
+ unchanged praxis reconstruction
= no automatic Floor passage
```

Likewise, formal completeness does not establish gain. A schema-valid record may declare every date, source, and boundary field while still failing to show why temporal differentiation matters for the claim.

#### `C16-LOW-01` — added dates without changed reconstruction

A service-authority archive documents that an access policy was announced on 4 February, discussed on 18 February, approved on 3 March, published on 8 March, and entered into force on 1 April. The proposed PATH analysis adds the five dates to an already warranted claim: **the policy changed the formal eligibility rule on 1 April**.

For that delimited claim, the added chronology does not change:

- which roles gained or lost eligibility;
- the cost of access;
- available alternatives;
- binding or release conditions;
- the status of the transition;
- the Claim Ceiling.

Removing the intermediate dates leaves the licensed reconstruction unchanged. The dates remain valid and may support provenance or later questions, but they do not create PATH-specific purchase for the tested claim.

```text
valid dates
+ correct order
+ unchanged tested reconstruction
→ relevance_floor_boundary_result: neutral
```

If the analyst nevertheless claims that the five dates establish a developmental Path, the stronger claim falls `below_floor` and requires reduction. The chronology itself remains available. The local canonical mapping is `claim_reduction_required` for the attempted Path claim, while the date index may remain `resolution_neutral` for its narrower function.

<a id="16-2-chronology-without-trajectory-gain"></a>

### 16.2 Chronology without Trajectory Gain

A chronology orders occurrences in time. It may be exact, well sourced, and analytically useful. It does not thereby establish a Path, Trajectory, sedimentation, historical direction, or changed continuation structure.

```text
many dates
+ correct ordering
≠ Trajectory gain
```

Chronology and Trajectory answer different questions.

A chronology asks:

> What occurred, and in what temporal order?

A Trajectory asks:

> How did a warranted Path acquire historical load such that earlier configurations, transitions, Non-Events, asymmetries, bindings, branches, repairs, and residuals remain active in later praxis?

Chapter 16 therefore separates four tests:

1. **Order test** — is the temporal order supported?
2. **Path test** — does the order form a traversed and relationally coherent Path rather than a list?
3. **Sedimentation test** — does earlier structure remain active in later structure?
4. **Gain test** — does the additional temporal reconstruction change the licensed claim?

Success at an earlier test does not imply success at a later one.

```text
chronology
→ may support Sequence
→ may support Path
→ may support Trajectory

but no arrow is automatic
```

#### Chronology as a positive bounded result

A chronology below a stronger PATH threshold is not analytically worthless. It may:

- establish precedence;
- identify an open or closed window;
- locate a transition candidate;
- expose an archive gap;
- support a later partial order;
- preserve uncertainty;
- delimit a source domain;
- show that a Trajectory claim cannot yet be made.

The correct result may therefore be `resolution_neutral`, `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, `admissible_but_provisional`, or `claim_reduction_required`, depending on the actual claim and audit. It is not automatically `failed_transformation`.

#### No gain from duration alone

A long interval does not itself create Trajectory gain.

```text
long duration
≠ sedimentation
≠ historical load
≠ Path Dependence
```

Repeated dates also do not establish repeated occurrence architecture. Repetition must remain tied to frames, windows, relations, and later effects. A time series can display persistence while leaving the practical structure unchanged.

#### Source-sensitive gain

Trajectory gain must remain sensitive to the material that supposedly carries it. If removing earlier transitions, one `Λ`, a branch closure, or a repair leaves the claim unchanged, those elements may be supporting rather than constitutive—or the target may be too elastic.

This is not yet the full upper-bound test. It is the lower-bound reminder that a stronger temporal story must add a discrimination that depends on temporal structure.

#### `C16-CHRON-01` — accurate chronology without sedimentation

A municipal procurement file records seven annual renewals of the same maintenance agreement. Each renewal occurred on schedule, used the same authorization procedure, preserved the same provider, retained the same price formula, and left the same exit route available. The archive permits an accurate seven-year chronology.

An analyst labels the series **a trajectory of institutional lock-in**. The chronology does not support that strength claim. The record establishes no material change in:

- practical exit costs;
- role asymmetry;
- binding or reliance load;
- availability of alternatives;
- repair or release conditions;
- later Action Corridors.

The mere recurrence of renewals does not show sedimentation. Current conditions remain sufficient to reconstruct the current agreement.

```text
seven ordered renewals
+ stable present conditions
≠ sedimented Trajectory
```

The attempted Trajectory claim maps to `claim_reduction_required`. A bounded chronology or recurrent Sequence remains admissible. Nothing in the reduction implies that the renewals did not occur or that later evidence could not support a different claim.

<a id="16-3-the-upper-path-boundary"></a>

### 16.3 The Upper PATH Boundary

The upper PATH boundary is crossed only while the resulting object and claim remain reconstructibly dependent on the temporal and structural source load declared as constitutive.

A candidate exceeds the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling when abstraction, compression, periodization, or labeling removes the very relations required to reconstruct the target.

```text
broad significance
+ no reconstructible source-to-result dependency
→ above-Ceiling candidate
```

Typical upper-bound failures include:

- a macro-label replaces the Path;
- periods are homogenized into one phase;
- starting and intermediate configurations disappear;
- local reversals and competing subpaths are removed;
- branch availability and loss are omitted;
- `Λ`, `Ω`, `Ψ`, or residual load is translated into generic development language;
- turning points are asserted but not reconstructible;
- citations remain while source-to-result dependency disappears;
- the target survives removal, reorder, substitution, or reframing of allegedly constitutive sources;
- a graph retains one high-level node while hiding the lineage needed to rebuild it.

#### Local boundary result

Chapter 16 reuses the existing controlled vocabulary:

```text
traceability_ceiling_boundary_result:
  within_ceiling
  claim_reduction
  above_ceiling
  underdetermined
```

These values are not canonical Output Classes.

- `within_ceiling` means that the target and claim remain reconstructibly dependent on their declared source load.
- `claim_reduction` means that a weaker target or claim remains traceable after the stronger one is reduced.
- `above_ceiling` means that the asserted target outruns reconstructible source load.
- `underdetermined` means that available lineage or source support does not permit a responsible Ceiling finding.

#### Traceability is not source citation density

Traceability requires more than attaching references to a target label. The reconstruction must show how source structures and relations support the target boundary and claim.

```text
many citations
≠ source-to-result dependency
```

A source pointer that cannot be connected to a selected occurrence, ordering relation, turning point, branch, Non-Event, asymmetry, binding, residual, or present-bearing load does not by itself keep the target below the Ceiling.

#### Removal and reorder pressure

Upper-bound traceability is tested by bounded source-sensitive pressure:

- remove one claimed constitutive source;
- reorder one claimed constitutive relation where sources permit comparison;
- restore an omitted branch or reversal;
- separate an overcompressed interval;
- reintroduce a preserved `Λ` or `Ω` load;
- compare a rival supported periodization.

If the target identity and claim remain entirely unchanged under every material pressure, the target may be overelastic rather than robust.

```text
source-indifferent target
≠ traceable composite
```

#### `C16-UP-01` — macro-label indifferent to constitutive-source removal

A twenty-year organizational archive includes:

- two governance configurations;
- a failed authorization transition;
- a three-year delay before a second transition;
- a blocked alternative service route;
- unequal regional implementation costs;
- a partial repair;
- a later reversal in one region.

An analyst compresses the entire field into the label **continuous modernization trajectory**. When the failed transition, the blocked branch, the regional reversal, and the unequal costs are removed one by one, the label and its explanatory claim remain unchanged.

The stability of the label does not establish robustness. It shows that the target does not depend on the source load it claims to summarize.

```text
same macro-label
under removal of alleged constitutive load
→ source-indifferent target
```

The candidate receives `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: above_ceiling`. The proposed Trajectory composition maps to `failed_transformation`. A bounded chronology, several regional Paths, or competing periodizations may remain available. Using the macro-label to assign responsibility, justify intervention, or inherit application authority would require `mandatory_stop`.

<a id="16-4-trajectory-without-path-trace"></a>

### 16.4 Trajectory without Path Trace

A Trajectory claim exceeds the upper PATH boundary when historical direction, accumulation, or sedimentation is asserted but the underlying Path cannot be reconstructed.

```text
Trajectory label
+ historical rhetoric
≠ traceable Trajectory
```

The defect is not merely insufficient detail. It is the absence of reconstructible load from the temporal structures said to constitute the Trajectory.

Where material to the claim, a traceable Trajectory must preserve or disclose:

- starting configuration;
- relevant intermediate configurations;
- transitions and their supported order;
- Path boundary and periodization;
- turning points;
- delays and Non-Events;
- asymmetries and binding relations;
- available, rejected, blocked, aborted, deferred, or lost alternatives;
- reversals and repairs;
- historical carriers into later praxis;
- changed Action Corridors;
- Selection Rule, Formation Rule, compression, exclusion, uncertainty, and irrecoverable Loss;
- source-to-result dependency;
- the strength and ceiling of the Trajectory claim.

Not every Trajectory requires every listed feature. Every feature claimed as constitutive or materially present must remain traceable or be declared in Loss.

#### Historical load must be shown, not invoked

Phrases such as “long-term transformation,” “cumulative evolution,” “institutional maturation,” or “historical momentum” do not establish sedimentation. The reconstruction must identify what earlier structure remains active, through which carrier, in which later dimension, and with what source support.

```text
historical vocabulary
≠ historical-load demonstration
```

#### Weaker findings survive

A failed Trajectory claim does not erase:

- valid occurrences;
- an ordered chronology;
- a Sequence;
- a bounded Path;
- a branch record;
- one supported `Λ` or `Ω` finding;
- an archive-gap statement;
- rival periodizations.

The reduction must preserve these findings rather than treating the stronger failure as total historical ignorance.

#### `C16-TRACE-01` — direction asserted without reconstructible Path

A report claims that a public agency followed **a trajectory from discretionary service toward automated governance**. It cites annual reports across fifteen years and identifies the first and last system states. It does not identify:

- the intervening configurations;
- the transitions by which discretion changed;
- a documented turning point;
- the three delayed implementation windows visible in the source archive;
- a branch in which human review remained available;
- unequal exposure across claimant groups;
- a later partial reintroduction of manual review.

The report therefore cannot show how the stated direction was formed or which historical load remains active in the present arrangement.

```text
first state
+ last state
+ broad direction label
≠ reconstructed Trajectory
```

The Trajectory claim receives `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: claim_reduction`. A bounded endpoint comparison and a partial chronology remain supportable, but the report cannot yet warrant one source-traceable Trajectory. The local canonical mapping is `claim_reduction_required`.

<a id="16-5-excessive-temporal-compression"></a>

### 16.5 Excessive Temporal Compression

Temporal compression is necessary whenever an extended and internally differentiated process is represented at reduced resolution. Compression remains admissible only if claim-relevant internal temporality is preserved or externally recoverable and the reduction is fully declared in the canonical Loss structure.

```text
compression
≠ punctualization
```

**Compression** reduces resolution while preserving the distinctions required for the target and claim.

**Punctualization** treats an extended, internally differentiated process as one point, event, or homogeneous phase such that constitutive order, duration, reversal, branching, `Λ`, `Ω`, binding, residual, or repair load disappears.

#### Compression test

A Chapter-16 compression test must declare:

1. the interval or source field compressed;
2. the compression rule;
3. the distinctions retained;
4. the distinctions externally recoverable through lineage;
5. the distinctions excluded;
6. unresolved uncertainty;
7. source-inherited and composition-induced irrecoverability;
8. the effect on order, turning points, branches, alternatives, Non-Events, asymmetries, bindings, residuals, and Action Corridors;
9. the effect on target identity and Claim Ceiling;
10. whether later decompression would require a new `DECOMPOSE` operation.

The five canonical Loss fields remain controlling:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

A summary can remain within the Ceiling even when much material is compressed. The question is whether the target remains reconstructible and whether the reduced resolution still carries the load required for the claim.

#### Punctualization and false turning points

Punctualization may create a false turning point by attaching an extended formation to one date. It may also erase a real turning point by treating contradictory phases as one event.

```text
single date label
≠ single occurrence automatically

long interval
≠ homogeneous phase automatically
```

A later `DECOMPOSE` operation may recover finer structure only where lineage still supports it. It does not reverse composition automatically and cannot recover composition-induced irrecoverability by assertion.

#### `C16-COMP-01` — a heterogeneous interval collapsed into one event

A five-year service transition included:

- an initial pilot in two regions;
- a source-supported `Λ` in which the scheduled equity review did not occur;
- continuation under a temporary workaround;
- withdrawal from one region;
- a blocked alternative procurement route;
- a later repair that reopened human review;
- unequal implementation costs across local offices.

A dashboard represents the entire interval as one node: **digital transition, 2021**.

The node removes the extended order, the missed review, regional reversal, blocked alternative, repair, and unequal costs. The target becomes indifferent to which of these structures occurred.

```text
five-year internally differentiated process
→ one event node
= punctualization
```

The compressed representation receives `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: above_ceiling` for the Path/Trajectory claim and maps to `failed_transformation`. A narrower statement that a digital-transition program was formally initiated in 2021 may remain `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, provided it is not substituted for the five-year Path.

<a id="c16-band-01-the-same-broad-materials-across-the-full-path-band"></a>

#### `C16-BAND-01` — the same broad materials across the full PATH band

One source field concerns a twelve-year public-service access reform. It contains:

- configuration `K0`: locally administered access with discretionary review;
- transition `T1`: centralization of eligibility rules;
- branch `B1`: a source-supported regional opt-out available during the first implementation window;
- `Λ1`: the scheduled accessibility review did not occur within that window;
- `Ω1`: local offices carried unequal migration costs and unequal reversibility;
- transition `T2`: closure of the regional opt-out;
- reversal `R1`: one region restored manual review after documented access failures;
- repair `P1`: a later cross-regional review procedure;
- present load: different evidential burdens and exit costs remain traceable across regions;
- uncertainty: the cross-order between two local pilot adjustments remains unresolved.

The same broad materials support three different representations.

##### Representation A — bounded Trajectory candidate within the band

The first representation preserves:

- source typing and lineage;
- a partial order rather than a false total order;
- `T1`, `Λ1`, `T2`, `R1`, and `P1` as distinct occurrences;
- the availability and later closure of `B1`;
- `Ω1` as unequal cost and reversibility load;
- the reversal and repair rather than one-directional development;
- present-bearing differences in evidence and exit conditions;
- the unresolved pilot cross-order;
- a complete five-part Loss declaration;
- source-sensitive target identity.

Its Formation Rule states that centralization, missed review, opt-out closure, regional reversal, and later repair jointly formed a historically sedimented access-reform Trajectory because earlier structures remain active in current evidential burdens, exit costs, and regional Action Corridors.

Removing `Λ1`, `B1`, `Ω1`, or `R1` materially changes the target boundary and strength claim. The representation therefore displays both praxeological purchase and reconstructible path load.

```text
relevance_floor_boundary_result: gain
traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: within_ceiling
```

Because one local cross-order remains uncertain, the bounded Trajectory maps to `admissible_but_provisional`, not to a claim of causal necessity, strong Path Dependence, prediction, or target function.

##### Representation B — chronology below the Floor

The second representation adds exact dates for every meeting, publication, pilot adjustment, review notice, and implementation memorandum. It lists forty-seven entries but does not distinguish:

- which transitions changed roles or alternatives;
- the `Λ` status of the missed review;
- the practical meaning of the opt-out branch;
- unequal migration costs;
- reversal and repair;
- present-bearing load.

The forty-seven dates improve navigation but do not change the tested reconstruction.

```text
relevance_floor_boundary_result: below_floor
traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: within_ceiling
```

The attempted Trajectory claim requires `claim_reduction_required`. The chronology remains available as a source index and may be `resolution_neutral` for that bounded purpose.

##### Representation C — macro-label above the Ceiling

The third representation compresses all twelve years into **progressive national modernization**. It omits the opt-out branch, missed review, unequal costs, reversal, repair, unresolved cross-order, and regional present load. The label remains unchanged when any of those materials is removed or reordered.

```text
relevance_floor_boundary_result: underdetermined
traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: above_ceiling
```

The macro-label cannot be evaluated as a PATH object because its practical significance is asserted at a level detached from reconstructible source load. The proposed Trajectory maps to `failed_transformation`. If the label is used to justify intervention, rank regions, attribute intent, establish inevitability, or inherit application authority, continuation maps to `mandatory_stop`.

##### Three outcomes are not stylistic equivalents

```text
same broad source field
≠ same analytical result

admissible Trajectory candidate
≠ chronology below Floor
≠ macro-label above Ceiling
```

The difference lies in praxeological purchase, source-to-result dependency, preserved internal temporality, declared Loss, and Claim Ceiling—not in visual preference. A Reader may render all three for comparison, but it must not present them as equally warranted views or allow the cleaner macro-node to conceal its failed trace burden.

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 16.1–16.5 now establish:

- the PATH-specific lower boundary;
- the distinction between correct chronology and Trajectory gain;
- the PATH-specific upper boundary;
- Trajectory failure through absent path trace;
- compression versus punctualization;
- the required same-material three-way Band contrast;
- six Chapter-16 Pressure Cases.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality preserves pressure from Sections 16.6–16.15, Chapter 17 cases and local audit, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, appendices, standalone `03_cases/*` production, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP1 completion
≠ Chapter 16 provisional lock
≠ full PATH boundary passage
≠ teleology audit
≠ PATH/SUB or PATH/RETYPE adjudication
≠ completed Chapter-17 case corpus
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 16 — WP2:
Artificial Directionality,
Hidden Teleology,
Omitted Non-Events,
Omitted Asymmetries,
PATH versus RETYPE,
and PATH versus SUB
```

<a id="16-6-artificial-directionality"></a>

### 16.6 Artificial Directionality

A temporal reconstruction displays **artificial directionality** when a retrospectively ordered source field is presented as if the observed sequence already carried one intrinsic developmental vector. The defect is not that the analyst reconstructs order after the fact. Every historical analysis is retrospective in that minimal sense. The defect arises when retrospective arrangement removes the contingency, reversals, parallel subpaths, alternative continuations, or frame dependence required to reconstruct how the Path actually remained open or divided.

```text
retrospective order
≠ original direction

ordered source field
≠ one directional development automatically
```

Chapter 11 permits bounded directionality where successive sedimented relations alter specified continuation conditions in a recurrent or cumulative orientation. Chapter 16 tests whether that direction remains source-sensitive under boundary pressure. A directionality claim must therefore identify:

- the dimension in which direction is asserted;
- the starting and relevant intermediate configurations;
- the transitions and relations carrying the claimed orientation;
- local reversals, pauses, repairs, and counter-movements;
- parallel or regionally divergent subpaths;
- alternatives that remained available at relevant historical cuts;
- the periodization on which the direction depends;
- the extent to which the apparent orientation survives a rival source-supported periodization;
- the Loss created by compression or exclusion;
- the ceiling of the directional claim.

Directionality is not all-or-nothing. A source field may support movement toward lower local discretion in one procedural dimension while simultaneously supporting increased appeal capacity, regional divergence, or later reversal in another. The proper result may therefore be a bounded, dimension-specific direction rather than one global vector.

#### Periodization sensitivity

A directional pattern is under pressure where it appears only because boundaries were chosen around a convenient endpoint or because counter-movements were assigned to a different period. The test asks:

1. Would another source-supported starting point alter the apparent orientation?
2. Does moving one boundary include an earlier reversal or a later repair?
3. Are parallel subpaths forced into one line?
4. Does the claim survive restoration of excluded transitions?
5. Is the direction carried by relations among occurrences, or only by the analyst's final ordering label?

```text
periodization-sensitive direction
≠ false automatically

undeclared periodization sensitivity
→ Claim Ceiling pressure
```

Sensitivity may warrant `admissible_but_provisional`, competing constructions, or Claim Reduction. It does not automatically require total rejection. What is prohibited is presenting a boundary-dependent orientation as an invariant property of the entire reference object.

#### Reversal preservation

A reversal is not noise merely because the later arrangement again resembles the earlier direction. Where a reversal changes costs, roles, alternatives, evidence, or subsequent repair conditions, it remains part of the Path load. Collapsing it into a temporary deviation can exceed the Traceability Ceiling even if the final endpoint is described accurately.

```text
later restabilization
≠ earlier reversal erased
```

The same applies to counter-movements that remain local. A national aggregate may move in one direction while municipal or regional subpaths diverge. The analyst may compose a bounded aggregate only if local heterogeneity remains traceable or is declared in Loss.

#### Graph boundary

A Reader or Graph view may draw a dominant orientation, but visual prominence is not warrant. Arrow thickness, layout, animation, or a preferred left-to-right route must not convert:

- parallel paths into stages of one path;
- a repair into completion of an original plan;
- a blocked branch into an irrelevant side node;
- a reversal into noise;
- a retrospective sequence into original purpose.

A lawful graph keeps supported reversals and alternatives addressable and marks the analytical status of the directional overlay.

#### `C16-DIR-01` — retrospective line suppresses supported reversals

A public-service network introduced digital intake over nine years. The archive supports:

- an early optional pilot in three districts;
- a later centralized intake rule;
- two districts temporarily returning to in-person triage after documented access failures;
- one district retaining a parallel paper route;
- a revised national standard that reintroduced local review;
- unequal regional implementation costs;
- no single original plan covering the full period.

An analyst arranges the occurrences as **pilot → centralization → optimization → mature digital service**. The dates are broadly correct, but the ordering converts a contested and partially reversible field into one original direction. The temporary return to in-person triage is described as implementation noise, the paper branch is omitted, and the later local-review repair is treated as completion of centralization rather than a partial reversal of it.

Restoring the two reversals and the surviving paper branch materially changes the target. The sources support a bounded direction toward greater digital mediation during part of the period, but not one continuous maturation vector.

```text
supported partial direction
+ omitted reversals and parallel branch
≠ one continuous developmental vector
```

The broad directional claim receives `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: claim_reduction`. A bounded, dimension-specific Path remains supportable and maps to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. Treating the original linear label as inevitable development or as grounds for intervention would exceed the chapter boundary.

<a id="16-7-hidden-teleology"></a>

### 16.7 Hidden Teleology
