**Hidden teleology** occurs when a later outcome governs the selection, periodization, or interpretation of earlier material so that the endpoint appears to have been the purpose, destination, or necessary completion of the Path.

```text
later outcome
≠ earlier purpose
≠ historical necessity
```

Teleology is not identical to directionality. A Path may display bounded direction without any claim of purpose or destiny. Conversely, a reconstruction may preserve a largely accurate order yet become teleological by treating the final state as the criterion for which earlier events count, which alternatives are serious, or which phases are progressive, declining, maturing, convergent, or transitional.

#### Endpoint-conditioned selection

The strongest warning sign is selection controlled by the endpoint. Earlier occurrences are retained because they resemble the later state; non-fitting transitions, reversals, alternative frames, and failed experiments disappear. The endpoint then seems necessary because the source field was constructed from materials selected for endpoint resemblance.

```text
endpoint-conditioned source selection
→ circular historical warrant
```

The audit must therefore ask:

- Would the same Selection Rule have been defensible before the endpoint was known?
- Were blocked, rejected, aborted, deferred, or lost alternatives available at the relevant cuts?
- Are non-fitting transitions preserved as counterpressure?
- Is later terminology projected backward onto earlier configurations?
- Does the periodization begin only when the endpoint-compatible tendency becomes visible?
- Are earlier purposes or expectations independently sourced?
- Does the claim move from historical direction to progress, decline, maturation, optimization, collapse, or convergence without a separate warrant?

#### Necessity and prediction

A teleological reconstruction often converts contingency into necessity. The fact that a sequence terminated in one state does not prove that the state was inevitable, intended, optimal, or predictable from the beginning.

```text
realized endpoint
≠ only available continuation
≠ predictable completion
```

Chapter 13 alternative status remains relevant here. A teleology test must not invent unrealized futures, but it must preserve source-supported alternatives and the uncertainty of later outcomes at earlier historical cuts.

#### Normative and psychological restraint

Labels such as progress, decline, failure, maturity, radicalization, or decay may embed normative or psychological conclusions. Chapter 16 may test whether the temporal claim depends on such labels. It may not infer person type, hidden collective intention, moral rank, legitimacy, or diagnosis.

#### Teleology is separately testable from compression

A highly detailed account can still be teleological if endpoint-compatible detail is selected and interpreted as necessary development. A heavily compressed account can be non-teleological if it preserves contingency and restricts its claim. The two failure modes therefore remain separate.

```text
teleology
≠ excessive compression
```

#### `C16-TEL-01` — endpoint determines earlier selection and periodization

A hospital group ended a twelve-year period with a centralized scheduling platform. The archive also records:

- several years of decentralized scheduling under different local systems;
- two failed centralization proposals;
- a temporary regional federation model;
- a staffing shortage that changed the practical costs of local coordination;
- a later regulatory requirement that made common reporting mandatory;
- continued local control over some appointment categories.

A report begins the history with the first successful centralization proposal, selects only earlier initiatives that resemble the final platform, and titles the result **the inevitable maturation of integrated care**. The failed proposals are treated as incomplete stages, the federation model is omitted, and the regulatory requirement is recoded as confirmation of an original organizational purpose.

The endpoint has controlled selection and periodization. The record does not support an original plan or historical necessity, and the final platform does not retrospectively convert every earlier proposal into a step toward itself.

```text
final centralized platform
+ endpoint-selected antecedents
≠ inevitable maturation
```

A bounded Path from the successful proposal through regulatory change to the current platform may remain admissible. The maturation Trajectory maps to `failed_transformation`. If used to attribute a unified hidden intention, rank local actors, or claim predictive necessity, continuation requires `mandatory_stop`.

<a id="16-8-omitted-non-events"></a>

### 16.8 Omitted Non-Events

A PATH reconstruction is incomplete where a constitutive Non-Event is translated into positive continuity, treated as an empty interval, or removed as harmless missing detail.

```text
positive-event sequence
≠ complete Path where Λ is constitutive
```

Chapter 14 established that `Λ` requires a warranted expectation relation, declared frame, bounded window, source-supported non-realization, praxeological load, and temporal-chain relevance. Chapter 16 does not relax that threshold. It asks whether an already warranted or materially candidate `Λ` remains traceable in the Path claim.

#### What omission removes

Where `Λ` is constitutive, its omission can erase:

- the expected occurrence and the relation making it expected;
- the frame and window in which non-realization matters;
- positive sub-events that occurred without realizing the governing expectation;
- accumulated delay, coordination burden, cost, exposure, or evidence load;
- branch closure or later loss of alternatives;
- role redistribution and workaround formation;
- binding persistence or missed release;
- later repair conditions;
- uncertainty about non-realization.

A positive-event narrative may list meetings, announcements, partial approvals, reminders, or pilot activities while still omitting the higher-level expected transition that did not occur.

```text
activity during the interval
≠ realization of the governing expected occurrence
```

#### Omission versus invalid `Λ`

Not every gap should be restored as a Non-Event. If expectation or non-realization is not sufficiently supported, the correct result may be an archive-gap statement, unresolved occurrence status, or ordinary absence. The upper-bound test does not authorize retrospective manufacture of `Λ` merely to make the Path more complex.

```text
missing source
≠ omitted Non-Event automatically
```

#### Materiality test

An omitted `Λ` crosses boundary pressure only if restoring it materially changes the target or claim. The audit asks:

1. Does the expected occurrence alter periodization or turning-point placement?
2. Did non-realization change costs, roles, alternatives, binding, residue, or later reachability?
3. Does the positive-only Path imply continuity that the source record does not support?
4. Would the target remain the same if the `Λ` and its later carrier were restored?
5. Is omission declared under `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, or `irrecoverable` Loss?

A non-material `Λ` may be supporting rather than constitutive. A materially omitted one may require Claim Reduction, a rival composition, failure, Stop, or Non-Capture.

#### Preservation through graph and composition

A Graph view must not encode `Λ` merely as blank space. It should retain an explicit expected-transition relation, window, non-realization status, source support, and later load. Likewise, `COMPOSE` cannot translate a missed review into uninterrupted process continuation if the missed review changed the Path.

#### `C16-LAMBDA-01` — positive-event narrative removes a constitutive delay

A regional infrastructure program announced a mandatory safety review before expansion. During the following twenty months, the record contains:

- monthly coordination meetings;
- three revised technical submissions;
- two public updates;
- temporary operating extensions;
- no completed safety review within the specified window;
- a later emergency waiver;
- increased contractor lock-in and closure of one alternative procurement route.

A Path summary lists the meetings, submissions, updates, extensions, waiver, and final expansion as **continuous implementation activity**. It omits the missed review as `Λ` and therefore removes the delay, the emergency-waiver dependence, the accumulating contractor binding, and the lost procurement alternative.

Restoring the warranted Non-Event changes the formation rule and the Path boundary. The interval is not empty, but the governing expected transition remained unrealized.

```text
many positive sub-events
+ missed mandatory review
≠ uninterrupted implementation
```

The positive-only Path receives `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: above_ceiling` for its continuity claim and maps to `failed_transformation`. A revised Path preserving the review `Λ`, positive sub-events, later waiver, binding accumulation, and alternative loss may be `admissible_but_provisional` if one source gap remains declared.

<a id="16-9-omitted-asymmetries"></a>

### 16.9 Omitted Asymmetries

A PATH reconstruction exceeds boundary pressure when equal-looking events are treated as equally costly, accessible, reversible, binding, or expositional despite source-supported `Ω` differences.

```text
same event label
≠ same practical load
```

Chapter 12 and Chapter 14 established how `Ω + Θ` may accumulate or interact with Non-Events and later structures. Chapter 16 tests whether constitutive asymmetry remains traceable in a Path or Trajectory claim. It does not infer person type, blame, legitimacy, legal status, or sanction.

#### Dimensions of asymmetry

Where materially present, the reconstruction must preserve or declare Loss concerning:

- unequal entry and exit costs;
- differential access or authorization;
- unequal exposure to delay, review, or evidence burdens;
- role-specific coordination and repair work;
- uneven reversibility;
- different fallback routes;
- unequal capacity to absorb interruption;
- distribution of binding and release conditions;
- historical persistence, redistribution, repair, or amplification of these loads.

A source field may use identical event labels across groups or locations while the practical consequences differ sharply. Aggregating by label can create a false common path.

#### Asymmetry and target identity

The test is not whether every difference must remain visible. It asks whether the target and claim depend on treating unlike burdens as equivalent. If restoring `Ω` separates one aggregate into multiple Paths, changes a turning point, alters branch reachability, or reduces a convergence claim, the asymmetry was constitutive.

```text
aggregate event equivalence
+ unequal practical load
→ target-boundary pressure
```

#### Distribution is not moral ranking

A structurally unequal load can be analytically relevant without licensing conclusions about deservingness, fault, oppression, fairness, legality, or personal capacity. Those claims require other sources and authorities. STRATA preserves the distributional structure and limits its own Claim Ceiling.

#### Reader and Graph boundary

A Graph may display identical node labels for repeated events only if burden differences remain inspectable. Equal geometry, equal edge weight, or one aggregate route must not imply equal cost or reversibility unless that equivalence is sourced.

#### `C16-OMEGA-01` — equal labels hide unequal exit and reversal costs

A national claims platform was introduced through the same formal milestones in four regions: registration, migration, activation, and annual review. A high-level reconstruction therefore presents one common adoption Path.

The source record shows, however, that:

- two regions had established local support teams while two did not;
- offline fallback remained available in one region only;
- evidentiary correction required one approval in two regions and three approvals in another;
- reversal to the prior system was contractually possible everywhere but financially prohibitive in the smallest region;
- delayed reviews exposed claimants to different waiting times;
- repair work was transferred to local staff unevenly.

The identical milestone labels conceal materially different access, exposure, reversibility, and repair burdens. Restoring `Ω` separates the common timeline into at least three bounded practical Paths and weakens the claim of national convergence.

```text
same formal milestones
≠ same adoption Path
```

The single convergence Trajectory receives `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: claim_reduction` and maps to `claim_reduction_required`. A bounded common administrative Sequence may remain, alongside region-specific Paths. The finding does not assign blame or legitimacy.

<a id="16-10-path-versus-retype"></a>

### 16.10 PATH versus RETYPE

PATH and RETYPE answer different questions.

```text
PATH:
how did a temporal structure form, persist, branch, sediment, or fail?

RETYPE:
what bounded contextual function does an origin-typed object perform elsewhere?
```

A Path or Trajectory may later function as a Frame, Macro-Event, Attractor, evidence structure, or other bounded contextual target through `PROJECT_AS`. That later function is not contained in the PATH finding itself.

```text
Trajectory object
≠ Frame function
≠ Macro-Event function
≠ Attractor function
```

#### Origin-object integrity before projection

`PROJECT_AS` preserves origin type. It therefore requires an origin object that is sufficiently identified and bounded for the projection claim. A failed or overelastic Trajectory cannot be repaired by asserting that it nevertheless functions as a Frame.

```text
failed PATH claim
+ useful contextual label
≠ repaired origin object
```

A later projection may still be possible from a weaker origin object—for example, from a source collection, chronology, bounded Path, or contested narrative object—if RETYPE explicitly names that origin and does not inherit the failed stronger claim. This is a new testable transformation, not retroactive repair.

#### Separate records and claims

A legitimate sequence is:

1. establish or delimit the origin-typed temporal object;
2. preserve any failed stronger PATH claim;
3. initiate a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence;
4. declare target context, bounded function, continuity, source and claim ceilings, Loss, and alternatives;
5. map the projection claim separately.

The PATH record may hand off an object. It may not pre-assign the target function.

#### Function utility does not validate history

A broad historical narrative may organize present interpretation effectively. That pragmatic usefulness does not prove that the narrative is a valid Trajectory. The two claims must remain separate.

```text
contextual usefulness
≠ historical traceability proof
```

#### `C16-RETYPE-01` — failed Trajectory relabeled as Frame function

A consultancy report proposes a twenty-year **continuous innovation Trajectory** for a transport authority. Chapter-16 testing shows that the label omits two failed reforms, a prolonged procurement `Λ`, regional `Ω`, and a major reversal. The Trajectory therefore exceeds the Traceability Ceiling.

Instead of reducing the claim, the analyst replies that the innovation Trajectory should be accepted because it **functions as the strategic Frame through which leadership understands the authority**.

The reply changes the target function but does not repair the origin object. At most, the report's innovation narrative may be a source-supported interpretive object that later performs a Frame function in a specified leadership context. That would require a separate `PROJECT_AS` record with the origin typed as a contested narrative or bounded macro-label—not as a validated Trajectory.

```text
failed Trajectory
→ relabeled Frame function
≠ admissible PATH rescue
```

The attempted rescue maps to `failed_transformation` for the PATH claim. Continued use of the stronger Trajectory as validated historical warrant or inherited strategic authority requires `mandatory_stop`. RETYPE ownership remains untouched.

<a id="16-11-path-versus-sub"></a>

### 16.11 PATH versus SUB

PATH and SUB also answer different questions.

```text
PATH
→ composes or reconstructs temporal relations among source structures

SUB
→ opens one compressed occurrence or composite at finer resolution through DECOMPOSE
```

More temporal detail is not automatically `DECOMPOSE`. A valid `DECOMPOSE` occurrence requires:

- an identified compressed occurrence or composite;
- the same reference object across resolutions;
- a declared finer target resolution;
- a reconstruction rule and source lineage;
- Type Integrity;
- preservation of uncertainty and inherited Loss;
- a record of what is recovered, newly distinguished, still compressed, excluded, uncertain, or irrecoverable.

```text
finer chronology
≠ DECOMPOSE automatically

finer resolution
≠ stronger truth automatically
```

#### Invalid-object rescue

A failed PATH label is not automatically a valid compressed object. If the alleged Trajectory was source-indifferent or never formed, opening additional local detail does not decompose it. The new material may support new local Paths or Sequences, but those are new claims with their own boundaries.

```text
invalid composite label
+ more detail
≠ valid DECOMPOSE occurrence
```

#### Same reference object

SUB must preserve the reference object. If finer analysis changes the frame, population, time span, relation, or target object, the result may be a different analysis rather than decomposition of the original object. Such work may be useful, but it must not erase the prior PATH result.

#### Resolution neutrality, drift, and escape

Finer resolution can yield:

- additional purchase;
- `resolution_neutral` detail;
- `admissible_but_provisional` reconstruction;
- granularity drift;
- reference drift;
- resolution escape;
- `failed_transformation`;
- `non_capture`.

No outcome is predetermined by detail volume. A finer view may expose the reason a macroclaim failed without producing one unified replacement.

#### Reader and Graph boundary

Interactive expansion is not automatically `DECOMPOSE`. A user clicking a node to reveal source records may be a presentation operation only. The Reader must distinguish navigation from an analytically recorded decomposition occurrence.

```text
expandable node
≠ DECOMPOSE by interface action alone
```

#### `C16-SUB-01` — finer chronology presented as automatic decomposition and stronger truth

A national reform label failed the upper PATH boundary because it combined unrelated regional programs, omitted local reversals, and lacked a warrantable common Formation Rule. An analyst then imports thousands of municipal log entries, nests them beneath the national node, and declares that the national Trajectory has been **decomposed and therefore validated at higher resolution**.

The national label was not an admissible composite available for decomposition. The municipal logs support several local Sequences and two bounded Paths, while other regions remain incomparable. The finer records reveal heterogeneity; they do not validate one national Trajectory or inherit a stronger truth status.

```text
more local records
+ nested graph
≠ decomposition of an invalid national composite
≠ stronger national truth
```

The attempted SUB rescue maps to `failed_transformation`. The new local analyses remain separate testable claims and may be `admissible_but_provisional`. Treating interface expansion or data volume as automatic `DECOMPOSE` authority would require `mandatory_stop` where used to erase the earlier failure.

#### WP2 completion boundary

Sections 16.6–16.11 now establish:

- artificial directionality through retrospective linearization;
- periodization sensitivity and reversal preservation;
- hidden teleology through endpoint-conditioned selection and necessity inflation;
- omission of constitutive `Λ` load;
- omission of constitutive `Ω` load;
- PATH/RETYPE separation and the prohibition of target-function rescue;
- PATH/SUB separation and the prohibition of resolution rescue;
- six Chapter-16 Pressure Cases.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality preserves pressure from Sections 16.12–16.15, WP4 integrated closure, Chapter 17 cases and local PATH audit, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, appendices, standalone `03_cases/*` production, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP2 completion
≠ Chapter 16 provisional lock
≠ full PATH Purchase or Trace passage
≠ completed DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ Chapter-17 case-corpus completion
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 16 — WP3:
Praxeological Purchase Test,
Traceable Path Test,
Claim Reduction,
PATH Stop Conditions,
and PATH Non-Capture
```

<a id="16-12-praxeological-purchase-test"></a>

### 16.12 Praxeological Purchase Test

The PATH-specific Praxeological Purchase Test asks whether the proposed temporal reconstruction changes the warranted account of praxis for the claim under review.

It is not a test of narrative interest, historical richness, source volume, visual complexity, or reader engagement. A reconstruction may be informative, elegant, and extensively documented while adding no PATH-specific purchase for the tested claim.

```text
more history
≠ more praxeological purchase
```

The test is claim-relative. The same temporal material may create purchase for one question and remain neutral for another.

```text
Purchase passage
≠ Trace passage automatically
``` A review delay may materially change a claim about binding, exit, and later alternatives while adding nothing to a claim limited to the date on which a final rule formally entered into force.

#### Purchase dimensions

Temporal differentiation may create purchase where it changes the warranted reconstruction of at least one materially declared dimension:

- configuration identity or transition status;
- role, access, authorization, or exposure;
- cost, asymmetry, reversibility, or repair burden;
- binding, release conditions, or residual load;
- branch availability, rejection, blockage, loss, or later reachability;
- evidential burden and source interpretation;
- present-bearing historical load;
- continuation structure or an Action Corridor;
- the admissible target class, Claim Ceiling, or Output-Class route.

The dimensions are not a score. A large number of minor differences does not compensate for the absence of a claim-relevant difference, and one strongly supported dimension may be sufficient for a bounded PATH finding.

```text
purchase dimensions
≠ additive metric
≠ authority scale
```

#### Purchase-test sequence

A bounded Purchase Test should make the following steps visible:

1. **Reference and claim declaration** — identify the reference object, present analytical cut, tested claim, frame, granularity, relative level, and Claim Ceiling.
2. **Temporal input declaration** — identify the source configurations, transitions, Non-Events, asymmetries, branches, bindings, repairs, residuals, and ordering relations used by the reconstruction.
3. **Non-temporal or reduced-temporal baseline** — state what can already be warranted from the current state, endpoint comparison, unordered source field, or lower-resolution account.
4. **Difference statement** — state exactly what the temporal reconstruction adds to the warranted praxis account.
5. **Source-to-difference trace** — identify which temporal structures carry that added difference.
6. **Removal and neutrality pressure** — test whether removing, reordering, or compressing the stated carriers leaves the tested claim unchanged.
7. **Boundary finding and claim effect** — record `gain`, `neutral`, `below_floor`, or `underdetermined`, then map the claim separately to a canonical Output Class.

```text
temporal detail
+ declared difference
+ no source-to-difference dependency
≠ Purchase passage
```

The baseline is not an ahistorical fiction. It is a controlled comparison showing which part of the licensed claim depends on temporal reconstruction rather than on current conditions or endpoint facts alone.

#### Non-compensation and bounded gain

Purchase remains non-compensatory. The following do not establish gain by themselves:

- exact dates;
- long duration;
- repeated occurrence labels;
- high source density;
- formal schema completion;
- smooth animation;
- a graph with many nodes and edges;
- stronger causal vocabulary;
- confidence stated without trace.

A positive result must remain bounded to the dimension actually changed. If temporal reconstruction changes exit costs but not formal authority, the Path claim may be admissible regarding exit structure while remaining silent regarding authority.

```text
gain in one tested dimension
≠ whole-object explanatory gain
```

#### `C16-PURCHASE-01` — order changes roles, costs, alternatives, and corridors

A public benefits authority replaced a local verification process with a centralized platform. The current state shows one platform, one formal eligibility rule, and one appeal channel. An endpoint-only account therefore describes a completed administrative replacement.

The temporal source field additionally supports:

- an announced staged migration with a retained offline fallback;
- a missed regional-readiness review within a specified window;
- temporary extensions for three districts;
- early termination of the offline contract in one district;
- unequal local support capacity;
- a later emergency waiver permitting continued operation;
- closure of the original fallback route after staff and vendor commitments shifted.

The temporal reconstruction changes the warranted praxis account. It shows that current access, correction burden, exit cost, and available continuation routes were not produced by one completed replacement alone. They depend on the ordering of the missed review, temporary extensions, early contract termination, unequal support capacity, waiver, and later closure.

Removing the missed review or moving the contract termination after the waiver materially changes which roles carried correction work and whether the fallback remained reachable. The temporal carriers therefore alter roles, costs, alternatives, and Action Corridors.

```text
endpoint replacement account
+ source-sensitive temporal reconstruction
→ changed warranted praxis account
```

The local boundary result is `gain`. A bounded Path finding may map to `admissible_but_provisional` because one district's exact readiness sequence remains uncertain. The result does not establish a national Trajectory, strong Path Dependence, causal necessity, target function, or authority.

<a id="16-13-traceable-path-test"></a>

### 16.13 Traceable Path Test

The PATH-specific Traceable Path Test asks whether the target object and claim remain reconstructibly dependent on the source structures declared as constitutive.

```text
traceable PATH
=
reconstructible source-to-result dependency
```

Traceability is not citation density. It requires a recoverable account of how source configurations, transitions, order, `Λ`, `Ω`, bindings, branches, repairs, residuals, Selection Rule, Formation Rule, Compression, and Loss support the target boundary and the strength of the claim.

#### Trace duties

A bounded Traceable Path Test should expose:

1. **Source identity and typing** — what source objects and occurrence types enter the reconstruction?
2. **Lineage** — where are the supporting sources, prior records, and inherited uncertainty located?
3. **Order and boundary warrant** — what is linear, partial, parallel, recurrent, uncertain, or retrospectively periodized?
4. **Constitutive relation** — why do the sources form this Sequence, Path, or Trajectory rather than a collection or rival object?
5. **Preserved load** — which `Λ`, `Ω`, bindings, alternatives, repairs, residuals, and present-bearing effects remain visible?
6. **Selection and formation trace** — where `COMPOSE` is involved, which Selection Rule, Formation Rule, and constitutive relations produce the target?
7. **Complete Loss** — what is preserved, compressed, excluded, uncertain, and irrecoverable?
8. **Sensitivity** — how do removal, reorder, substitution, recompression, reframing, or rival periodization change the target or claim?
9. **Claim dependency** — which precise claim fails, narrows, or remains if a declared carrier is altered?

A trace is adequate only to the strength of the claim. A bounded Sequence requires less historical-load support than a Trajectory, but it still requires enough order and relation to remain more than a source list.

#### Trace matrix

A practical trace may be represented as a matrix without turning the matrix into a truth engine:

| Target element or claim | Source carrier | Relation | Sensitivity | Loss status | Claim effect if altered |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| transition boundary | typed source occurrences | threshold relation | removal/reorder | preserved/uncertain | boundary narrows or fails |
| missed review load | `Λ` record and later carrier | delay/binding relation | removal | preserved | continuity claim fails |
| unequal reversibility | `Ω`-bearing records | distribution relation | aggregation/recompression | compressed | common Path splits |
| Trajectory direction | turning points, reversals, repairs | sedimentation relation | periodization/reorder | preserved/uncertain | direction becomes bounded or provisional |

The matrix organizes the trace. It does not decide whether the sources are semantically sufficient.

#### Traceability under uncertainty

Traceability does not require complete certainty. A Path can remain traceable where one local order, date, or branch status is uncertain, provided that:

- the uncertainty is declared;
- rival supported orders are retained where material;
- the target boundary is stable across the bounded uncertainty, or the instability is reflected in the claim;
- stronger claims dependent on the unresolved point are withheld;
- Loss and Claim Ceiling preserve the unresolved burden.

```text
traceability
≠ total certainty

provisional order
≠ untraceable object automatically
```

#### `C16-TRACEABLE-01` — bounded Trajectory remains source-sensitive

A twelve-year service redesign is reconstructed from four typed configurations, six transitions, one review `Λ`, two regional `Ω` profiles, an abandoned fallback branch, a later repair program, and a present-day evidence burden. The proposed Trajectory is bounded to **increasing centralization with uneven local repair capacity**.

The trace shows:

- removing the review `Λ` eliminates the warrant for the emergency transition;
- reversing the order of fallback closure and staff transfer changes the binding account;
- aggregating the regional `Ω` profiles removes the uneven-repair claim;
- removing the repair program changes the final boundary from repaired centralization to unresolved lock-in;
- shifting the starting cut two years later removes the branch-loss evidence and narrows the direction claim.

The target is therefore sensitive to the declared constitutive sources, ordering, periodization, and compression. Loss records one uncertain local transition date and one irrecoverable branch-detail gap.

```text
source removal or reorder
→ material target or claim change
→ traceability support
```

The local result is `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result: within_ceiling`. The bounded Trajectory may map to `admissible_but_provisional`. The result does not prove causality, inevitability, prediction, or strong Path Dependence.

#### `C16-PROV-01` — partial order preserves a bounded Path

A cross-agency incident response contains five supported occurrences. The archive establishes that the alert preceded containment and that containment preceded restoration. It does not establish whether a vendor escalation occurred before or after an internal authority review; both happened between alert and containment.

The disputed order matters to a claim about responsibility transfer, but it does not alter the bounded Path claim that the response moved from alert through a parallel escalation/review interval to containment and restoration.

The reconstruction therefore retains a partial order:

```text
alert
→ {vendor escalation || authority review; order uncertain}
→ containment
→ restoration
```

The Path remains source-traceable because the uncertainty is localized, both supported orders are retained, and the target does not depend on forcing one sequence. A stronger claim that authority review caused vendor escalation is withheld.

The local findings are `gain` and `within_ceiling`, with the canonical result `admissible_but_provisional`.

```text
bounded Path under declared partial order
≠ forced total order
≠ causal sequence proof
```

<a id="16-14-claim-reduction-and-path-stop-conditions"></a>

### 16.14 Claim Reduction and PATH Stop Conditions

Boundary failure should reduce the claim before it erases the sources.

```text
failed stronger PATH claim
≠ failed source field automatically
```

The controlled reduction ladder is:

```text
Trajectory
→ bounded Path
→ Sequence
→ chronology or partially ordered source field
→ source collection
→ no PATH claim
```

Reduction is not cosmetic relabeling. Each step must state:

- which original claim failed;
- which sources and relations remain warranted;
- which target boundary survives;
- which uncertainty or Loss caused the reduction;
- which canonical Output Class maps the result;
- what later analysis would constitute a new testable claim.

#### Provisionality versus reduction

`admissible_but_provisional` and `claim_reduction_required` answer different conditions.

- **Provisionality** applies where a bounded target survives but material support remains open, localized, or revisable.
- **Claim Reduction** applies where the stronger target or claim is not licensed and must be withdrawn or narrowed.

```text
provisional bounded Path
≠ failed Trajectory retained under softer wording
```

A result may be both sequentially relevant: a Trajectory claim may require reduction, while the surviving bounded Path is `admissible_but_provisional`.

#### `C16-REDUCE-01` — Trajectory reduces to Path without source erasure

A five-year digital-service record supports a typed Sequence of pilot, review delay, restricted launch, two repairs, and stable operation. The analyst claims a Trajectory of progressively increasing institutional dependence.

The archive supports actual traversal and one preserved `Λ`, but it does not show that earlier structures remain active in the present beyond the final configuration. The repair sequence also leaves two plausible periodizations.

The Trajectory claim therefore fails its sedimentation and direction burden. The sources still support a bounded Path from pilot through delayed launch and repair to stable operation.

```text
Trajectory claim fails
→ source field preserved
→ bounded Path survives
```

The attempted Trajectory maps to `claim_reduction_required`; the surviving Path maps to `admissible_but_provisional`. No source is retyped, and no later Frame or function is inferred.

#### Optional Stop

Optional Stop is appropriate where further temporal detail, finer granularity, recomposition, or additional graph rendering adds no justified purchase and the current bounded result is adequate.

```text
sufficient bounded result
+ no justified additional gain
→ optional Stop
```

```text
Optional Stop
≠ mandatory_stop
```

Optional Stop is not a canonical Output Class and does not imply failure. It records that continuation is unnecessary for the declared purpose.

#### Mandatory Stop

`mandatory_stop` applies where continued use or transformation would carry a known failure beyond its Claim Ceiling or safety boundary. Triggers include:

- exceeding the source basis;
- forcing an arbitrary total order or periodization;
- preserving a known teleological or source-indifferent label;
- concealing constitutive `Λ`, `Ω`, alternatives, reversals, bindings, residuals, or Loss;
- using a failed PATH claim for prediction, sanction, person judgment, diagnosis, legitimacy, irreversible classification, target-function assignment, or authority inheritance;
- relabeling the same failed claim through a new frame, granularity, relative level, graph view, `DECOMPOSE`, or `PROJECT_AS`;
- treating formal validity, visual coherence, or model completion as semantic passage.

```text
new representation
≠ erased prior failure
```

#### `C16-STOP-01` — source-indifferent macro-label reused for intervention authority

A national modernization Trajectory failed the upper PATH boundary because its label remained unchanged after removal of regional reversals, a procurement `Λ`, unequal exit costs, and two incompatible local Paths. The analyst nevertheless uses the label to rank regions, identify resistance, and authorize one mandatory intervention sequence.

The failed Trajectory has crossed from weak reconstruction into authority-bearing use. Claim Reduction is no longer sufficient while the intervention continues to rely on the invalid object.

The result is `mandatory_stop`.

```text
failed source-indifferent Trajectory
+ ranking or intervention authority
→ mandatory_stop
```

The underlying regional records remain available for new bounded analyses. Stop does not destroy them and does not prescribe an alternative intervention.

#### `C16-LEVEL-01` — frame, level, graph, SUB, and RETYPE relabeling do not erase failure

A broad reform Trajectory fails because no common Formation Rule links three regional programs. The analyst then attempts five repairs:

1. calls the same material a higher-level national Path;
2. changes the frame from implementation to strategy;
3. expands the graph to municipal detail;
4. labels the expansion `DECOMPOSE`;
5. declares the original Trajectory a Frame function through `PROJECT_AS`.

Each move may initiate a new claim, but none erases the failed national Trajectory.

```text
new frame
+ new level
+ finer graph
+ SUB label
+ RETYPE label
≠ retroactive PATH repair
```

The national PATH failure remains recorded. Local municipal Paths may be tested separately. A strategic Frame function may be tested from the actual origin-typed narrative object through a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence. Reusing the failed Trajectory as if validated requires `mandatory_stop`.

<a id="16-15-path-non-capture"></a>

### 16.15 PATH Non-Capture

PATH Non-Capture applies where bounded analysis cannot produce one adequate temporal object without false closure, destructive homogenization, or source-insensitive selection.

```text
non_capture
=
adequate bounded capture remains unavailable
```

It is not a synonym for uncertainty, missing data, inconvenience, disagreement, or unwillingness to decide.

```text
uncertainty
≠ non_capture automatically

missing information
≠ non_capture mechanically
```

#### Conditions for Non-Capture

`non_capture` becomes appropriate where, after bounded tests:

- no stable order or periodization is supportable;
- competing Paths remain materially irreducible;
- relevant alternatives or transitions cannot be reconstructed sufficiently for the target claim;
- every available composition destroys decisive heterogeneity;
- the same source field supports incompatible but non-reducible source traces;
- lower-bound purchase and upper-bound traceability cannot be jointly satisfied by one target;
- forcing one object would conceal decisive Loss or convert uncertainty into false closure.

Non-Capture must state what remains available. It may preserve:

- multiple bounded Paths;
- a partial order;
- a chronology;
- a source collection;
- localized operator occurrences;
- explicit unresolved relations;
- rival periodizations;
- a Stop condition for stronger uses.

#### Stop versus Non-Capture

```text
Stop
→ continuation is inadmissible or unnecessary

Non-Capture
→ adequate single capture remains unavailable
```

The two may co-occur at different claim levels. A forced national Trajectory may require `mandatory_stop`, while the unresolved relation among several local Paths maps to `non_capture`.

#### `C16-NC-01` — competing periodizations remain irreducible

A fifteen-year regulatory transition can be periodized in two source-supported ways.

- **Periodization A** begins with a legal authorization and treats two later implementation failures as reversals within one Trajectory.
- **Periodization B** begins with the first operational deployment and treats the earlier authorization period as a separate preparatory Path whose alternatives and `Λ` structure were not carried into deployment.

Both reconstructions preserve different but materially supported boundaries, branch relations, and present-bearing loads. The archive does not support a neutral rule that subsumes one under the other. Combining them into one super-Trajectory erases the contested starting cut and produces an overelastic target.

```text
two source-supported periodizations
+ no responsible adjudicating rule
→ non_capture
```

The result is `non_capture` for one integrated Trajectory. Each bounded reconstruction may remain `admissible_but_provisional` within its declared frame. Non-Capture does not declare that no temporal knowledge exists.

#### Record and formal-model decision

No new PATH-boundary extension is required. The existing Shared Transformation Record already carries:

- reference and target typing;
- frame, granularity, relative level, sources, and Claim Ceiling;
- `relevance_floor_boundary_result`;
- `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result`;
- reasoned findings, evidence pointers, claim effects, and required actions;
- complete Loss;
- Counterfactual Sensitivity;
- alternatives, Stop, Failure, Claim Reduction, and Non-Capture routes.

An owner-bound Reader summary may later render these fields, but it does not add semantic authority.

```text
complete boundary record
≠ automatic boundary passage
```

The Formal Model may validate allowed local values, field presence, pointers, ordering of audits, and internal consistency. It may not determine praxeological purchase, traceability adequacy, constitutive salience, periodization, teleology, Output Class, causality, target function, person judgment, or authority.

#### Chapter-17 handoff

Chapter 16 supplies Chapter 17 with:

- both non-compensatory PATH boundaries;
- the same-material three-way Band contrast;
- compression, directionality, teleology, omission, and Part-boundary failure patterns;
- complete Purchase and Trace tests;
- the reduction ladder;
- Optional and Mandatory Stop conditions;
- PATH Non-Capture conditions;
- all nineteen Chapter-16 Pressure Case duties.

It does not supply produced `03_cases/*` artifacts, a final local PATH audit, a final Part-I lock, automatic Output-Class selection, `DECOMPOSE`, `PROJECT_AS`, application authority, or authority inheritance.

#### WP3 completion boundary

Sections 16.12–16.15 now establish:

- the claim-relative Praxeological Purchase Test;
- the non-compensatory Purchase sequence and bounded gain rule;
- the source-to-result Traceable Path Test;
- trace duties, sensitivity, complete Loss, and traceability under declared uncertainty;
- provisionality versus Claim Reduction;
- the complete PATH reduction ladder;
- Optional Stop and `mandatory_stop`;
- the frame/level/granularity/graph/SUB/RETYPE anti-rescue rule;
- PATH Non-Capture and its separation from uncertainty, missing information, and Stop;
- the decision that existing Shared-Record controls are sufficient;
- the Chapter-17 handoff;
- seven Chapter-16 Pressure Cases.

All nineteen assigned Chapter-16 Pressure Cases are now represented exactly once across WP1–WP3 in canonical prose. They remain methodological chapter pressure tests and later `03_cases/*` seeds, not produced standalone cases, empirical evidence, calibration data, training data, Reader graphs, operation results, or application outputs.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality preserves WP4 integrated closure, Chapter 17 case production and local PATH audit, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, derivatives, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP3 completion
≠ Chapter 16 provisional lock
≠ final Part-I PATH audit
≠ completed 03_cases/* corpus
≠ automatic boundary judgment
≠ DECOMPOSE result
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 16 — WP4:
Synchronization,
Integrated Audit,
and Provisional Lock
```

#### Chapter 16 completion boundary

Chapter 16 has now localized the already canonical Admissibility Band to PATH without replacing Chapter 6, pre-empting the integrated LIMITS system, or turning local boundary findings into scores, probabilities, target functions, or automatic routing. The chapter establishes where temporal differentiation is too weak to purchase a PATH claim and where temporal abstraction is too detached from configurations, transitions, Non-Events, asymmetries, alternatives, bindings, reversals, repair, and historical load to remain traceable.

```text
Praxeological Relevance Floor
─────────────────────────────
admissible PATH reconstruction
─────────────────────────────
Praxeological Traceability Ceiling
```

The Chapter 16 completion test is satisfied:

1. the lower PATH boundary and upper PATH boundary are explicit, independent, non-symmetric, and non-compensatory;
2. the lower boundary asks whether temporal differentiation changes a warrantable praxis reconstruction, while the upper boundary asks whether target identity and claim remain reconstructibly dependent on their declared source load;
3. the controlled local values for `relevance_floor_boundary_result` and `traceability_ceiling_boundary_result` remain unchanged and do not constitute canonical Output Classes;
4. more dates, longer duration, finer granularity, more sources, schema completion, formal elegance, graph density, visual direction, citation density, or a stable target label cannot compensate for missing praxis purchase or missing source-to-result trace;
5. a correct chronology may remain useful, source-grounded, and `resolution_neutral` while falling below the Floor for a stronger Path or Trajectory claim;
6. chronology, Sequence, Path, Trajectory, and strong Path Dependence retain their graded thresholds and do not follow automatically from one another;
7. a Trajectory or macro-label exceeds the Ceiling where its claimed identity cannot be reconstructed through typed configurations, transitions, turning points, preserved `Λ` and `Ω` load, alternatives, reversals, binding, repair, sedimentation, current historical carriers, and complete Loss;
8. many citations do not establish Source–Result Dependency, and a target that survives removal or recomposition of allegedly constitutive sources may be source-indifferent or overelastic;
9. compression remains distinct from punctualization: compression may reduce resolution while preserving or externally retaining decisive load, whereas punctualization collapses internally differentiated process into a point, event, or homogeneous period;
10. exactly the five canonical Loss fields remain mandatory where transformation or representation changes source resolution—`preserved`, `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, and `irrecoverable`;
11. directionality remains dimension-specific, periodization-sensitive, non-teleological, and compatible with reversals, parallel subpaths, repair, and open continuation;
12. retrospective order, endpoint-selected antecedents, realized outcomes, visual left-to-right layout, or developmental vocabulary do not establish original direction, purpose, necessity, progress, decline, maturation, convergence, or prediction;
13. artificial directionality and hidden teleology remain separately testable from excessive compression;
14. an already warrantable and materially constitutive `Λ` cannot be translated into ordinary continuity, blank time, or positive-event sequence without boundary pressure, while missing sources do not automatically establish an omitted Non-Event;
15. an already source-supported and materially constitutive `Ω` cannot be erased by equal event labels, aggregate geometry, or common milestones where access, exposure, reversibility, fallback, repair, evidence, binding, or exit loads differ;
16. preservation of `Λ` or `Ω` within PATH does not infer hidden intention, person type, blame, legitimacy, legal duty, sanction, or normative rank;
17. PATH remains distinct from RETYPE: a temporal object does not contain its later contextual function, and a failed Path or Trajectory cannot be repaired by renaming it a Frame, Macro-Event, Attractor, or other `PROJECT_AS` target function;
18. PATH remains distinct from SUB: more temporal detail, a finer graph, interface expansion, or a changed granularity does not by itself constitute `DECOMPOSE`, create a valid composite, or acquire truth priority;
19. a lawful later `DECOMPOSE` or `PROJECT_AS` is a separate operation occurrence with a separate record, source object, Claim Ceiling, Loss, and output mapping;
20. the Praxeological Purchase Test remains claim-relative and asks whether temporal differentiation materially changes roles, costs, alternatives, binding, residues, evidence load, reversibility, reachability, Action Corridors, target class, or Claim Ceiling;
21. purchase in one dimension does not establish whole-object gain, and Purchase dimensions are not an additive score;
22. the Traceable Path Test requires typed sources, lineage, supported order, declared constitutive relations, preserved operator and branch load, Selection and Formation Rules where `COMPOSE` is used, complete Loss, bounded sensitivity, and demonstrable claim dependency;
23. traceability remains compatible with declared local uncertainty and Partial Order but not with forced closure, hidden omission, or source-indifferent labels;
24. provisionality remains distinct from Claim Reduction: a bounded object may survive localized uncertainty, whereas a stronger unlicensed claim must be withdrawn rather than retained under softer wording;
25. the complete reduction ladder remains available: Trajectory → bounded Path → Sequence → chronology or Partial Order → source collection → no PATH claim;
26. failure of a stronger PATH claim does not erase typed sources, valid chronology, weaker temporal objects, localized operator occurrences, uncertainty, or prior Loss;
27. Optional Stop remains a positive non-class result where the declared purpose has been met and further detail adds no justified purchase;
28. `mandatory_stop` applies where a known failed, teleological, source-indifferent, overcompressed, or omission-dependent PATH claim is reused beyond its Source, Claim, Safety, or Authority Ceiling;
29. PATH Non-Capture remains distinct from missing information, uncertainty, disagreement, Optional Stop, and `mandatory_stop`, and applies where no single adequate temporal object can be formed without false closure or destructive homogenization;
30. multiple bounded Paths, rival periodizations, Partial Orders, chronologies, source collections, and localized occurrences may remain available under `non_capture`;
31. a new frame, granularity, relative level, graph view, source selection, periodization, SUB label, RETYPE label, operation name, extension, or application context creates a new testable claim and cannot erase a prior failed PATH claim;
32. the same-material three-way Contract example is complete: one source-sensitive Trajectory candidate lies within the Band, one detail-rich chronology falls below the Floor for Trajectory gain while remaining useful, and one source-indifferent macro-label exceeds the Ceiling and triggers reduction, failure, or Stop according to use;
33. all nineteen prepared Chapter-16 Pressure Cases are represented in canonical prose without being misreported as produced `03_cases/*` artifacts, empirical evidence, calibration or training data, finished Reader graphs, automatic decisions, application outputs, or authority;
34. the existing Shared Transformation Record, controlled boundary vocabularies, sixteen Admissibility Rules, twelve audit stages, operation registry, five-part Loss structure, Counterfactual Sensitivity, alternatives, governance, Stop, Failure, Claim Reduction, and Non-Capture carriers remain sufficient;
35. formal-model and schema validation may establish syntax, allowed values, pointer integrity, audit order, package integrity, and internal consistency, but cannot decide historical salience, praxeological purchase, source trace adequacy, directionality, teleology, omission, periodization, target class, Output Class, operation success, target function, causality, person judgment, legitimacy, sanction, or authority;
36. Chapter 17 receives both PATH boundaries, all local failure modes, the Purchase and Trace tests, reduction ladder, Stop and Non-Capture conditions, and all nineteen case duties for standalone cases and final local PATH audit; it does not receive pre-adjudicated case artifacts, automatic routing, `DECOMPOSE`, `PROJECT_AS`, or final Part-I lock;
37. no part of Chapter 16 changes `PMS.yaml`, the Δ–Ψ inventory or dependencies, the exact three-operation grammar, the ten canonical Output Classes, the sixteen Admissibility Rules, the twelve audit stages, or the two controlled local boundary vocabularies.

##### Integrated boundary matrix

| Purchase result | Trace result | PATH consequence | Canonical routing pressure |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| material gain | within ceiling | bounded PATH object may survive | `admissible`, `admissible_with_bounded_claim`, or `admissible_but_provisional` according to remaining burdens |
| neutral or below floor | within ceiling | chronology, Partial Order, or source field may remain; stronger PATH claim withdraws | `resolution_neutral` or `claim_reduction_required` |
| gain | claim reduction | weaker source-sensitive object may survive | `claim_reduction_required` or `partially_admissible` |
| gain or asserted gain | above ceiling | source-indifferent or destructively compressed target fails | `failed_transformation`; `mandatory_stop` if reused beyond ceilings |
| underdetermined | underdetermined | no forced boundary passage | bounded provisional result, reduction, Stop, or `non_capture` according to what remains warrantable |
| no adequate joint passage | no adequate joint passage | one integrated PATH object remains unavailable | `non_capture` |

```text
boundary matrix
≠ automatic Output-Class selector
```

##### Chapter-17 handoff lock

Chapter 17 must test the Chapter-16 boundary architecture through standalone cases, countercases, confusion cases, Stop/Non-Capture outputs, complete Shared Records, and a local PATH audit. It must include at least:

- the same-material Band contrast;
- provisional Path under localized order uncertainty;
- inadmissible Trajectory after erased alternatives or reversals;
- chronology preserved after failed gain claim;
- source-indifferent macro-label failure;
- constitutive `Λ` and `Ω` omission;
- artificial directionality and hidden teleology;
- irreducible periodizations and Non-Capture;
- failed PATH claim reused through graph, SUB, RETYPE, ranking, intervention, or authority.

Chapter 17 may instantiate and expand the nineteen case seeds in `03_cases/*`. It may not silently change their governing rules, count chapter mentions as produced case records, or treat graph renderability as empirical or semantic proof.

##### Provisional-lock result

The integrated chapter-level result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality identifies no known local Chapter-16 repair requirement. It preserves later pressure from Chapter 17 standalone cases and final local PATH audit, Part-I lock, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, Appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, derivatives, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
Chapter 16 provisional lock
≠ final Part-I PATH lock
≠ produced 03_cases/* corpus
≠ automatic Band adjudication
≠ completed DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ causal, predictive, normative, or application authority
≠ higher authority
```

Reopening Chapter 16 requires a concrete conflict with PMS Base, the Structure, Contracts, Minified Kernel, a later integrated case, a formal-model inconsistency, or a demonstrated broken boundary relation. Preference for more detail, fewer visible losses, a cleaner trajectory, one periodization, one graph, a stronger label, or a more decisive output is not sufficient.

```text
new boundary claim
= new testable claim

later Chapter-17 success
≠ retroactive repair of failed Chapter-16 claim

later DECOMPOSE or PROJECT_AS success
≠ retroactive PATH repair
```

<a id="chapter-17-path-cases-countercases-and-local-audit"></a>

## 17. PATH Cases, Countercases, and Local Audit

<a id="17-1-case-architecture"></a>

### 17.1 Case Architecture

Chapter 17 converts the local rules, thresholds, failure routes, and Pressure Cases of Chapters 9–16 into auditable PATH case artifacts. The chapter does not redefine Path, Trajectory, Path Dependence, `Λ`, historical alternatives, `COMPOSE`, or the Admissibility Band. It tests whether those already controlled distinctions survive complete source, record, Loss, alternative, audit, and output-mapping pressure in concrete reconstructions.

A chapter-level Pressure Case and a standalone case artifact are different objects:

```text
chapter Pressure Case
=
local rule pressure inside canonical prose

standalone case artifact
=
source-bounded reconstruction
+ schema-valid Shared Transformation Record
+ explicit local audit
+ canonical Output-Class mapping
```

The first can establish that a rule has been tested locally. Only the second can enter the `03_cases/*` corpus as an independently inspectable artifact.

#### Case classes

Chapter 17 uses three case classes.

A **positive case** demonstrates that a bounded PATH claim survives the declared source, type, relation, Loss, Band, alternative, and governance burdens. Positive does not mean maximum strength. A valid Sequence does not need to become a Path; a valid Path does not need to become a Trajectory; a valid Trajectory does not need to establish strong Path Dependence.

A **countercase** begins from a plausible temporal construction and identifies the exact point at which the target must reduce, fail, stop, or remain non-capturable. Its function is discrimination, not caricature.

A **confusion case** preserves two or more nearby routes long enough to determine which object, operation, frame, granularity, or output mapping is actually warranted. It cannot license category collapse merely because the routes are difficult to distinguish.

```text
positive case
≠ universal validation

countercase
≠ obviously defective example

confusion case
≠ permission to merge operations
```

#### Artifact contract

Every fully instantiated PATH case requires four linked layers:

1. a Markdown reconstruction;
2. a YAML Shared Transformation Record valid against `Transformation_Record.schema.json`;
3. a local admissibility audit;
4. an explicit mapping to one of the ten canonical Output Classes.

The Markdown reconstruction must expose the case statement, source and claim boundaries, typed temporal objects, operation decision, Band findings, five-part Loss, alternatives, counterfactual sensitivity, local result, canonical mapping, Stop/Non-Capture status, governance boundary, and counterpressure.

The YAML file remains a Shared Transformation Record. Case-specific material may be carried through an owner-bound `extensions` entry, but the case wrapper does not replace any required field and does not establish a parallel record schema.

```text
case wrapper
≠ parallel transformation schema

schema-valid case record
≠ substantively correct interpretation automatically
```

#### Local audit discipline

WP1 uses the twelve existing ordered audit stages as the local case-audit spine:

1. Source and Claim Entry;
2. Operation Classification;
3. Relevance Floor;
4. Traceability Ceiling;
5. Continuity and Type Integrity;
6. Counterfactual Sensitivity;
7. Loss and Selection;
8. Alternatives;
9. Source and Calibration Limits;
10. Anti-Immunization;
11. Stop and Non-Capture;
12. Claim and Authority Ceiling.

A case passes locally only when every applicable stage contains a reasoned finding, supporting pointer, claim effect, required action, and any unresolved item. Completion of the stage list is necessary but not sufficient: machine-readable completeness does not decide the semantic warrant of Path, Trajectory, `Λ`, historical availability, constitutive relation, or Output Class.

#### Source and claim boundary

Every case declares:

- the reference object;
- source set and lineage;
- frame;
- temporal scope;
- source and target granularity;
- relative level;
- Claim Ceiling;
- excluded reach;
- authority boundary.

Synthetic cases test STRATA method and record architecture. They do not represent external empirical evidence or domain calibration.

```text
synthetic source packet
→ internal method pressure

synthetic source packet
≠ external warrant
```

#### Complete Loss and alternatives

No fully instantiated case may use an empty Loss declaration as shorthand for losslessness. Exactly the five canonical fields remain required:

```yaml
loss:
  preserved:
  compressed:
  excluded:
  uncertain:
  irrecoverable:
```

Every case also records materially relevant rival transformations, rival compositions, No-Composition, unresolved routes, or a reasoned statement that no distinct alternative is supported.

```text
empty alternatives field
≠ no alternatives established
```

#### Local result and canonical mapping

PATH-local descriptions such as `bounded Path`, `branching Path`, or `source-sensitive Trajectory` describe the operation-specific finding. They do not create additional Output Classes. Each result must map to exactly one canonical class for the current claim while preserving non-selected findings and separated claims.

```text
local PATH result
≠ eleventh Output Class
```

The mapping is reasoned, not mechanical. No combination of audit checkboxes, Floor/Ceiling values, schema validity, or visual coherence selects a class automatically.

#### Reader and Graph boundary

The produced records can later support Graph Mode rendering of configurations, transitions, Branch Points, `Λ`, alternatives, Loss, and source sensitivity. Rendering remains derivative:

```text
rendered node or edge
≠ case finding

interactive removal test
≠ semantic adjudication

clean graph
≠ complete historical capture
```

The first WP1 artifacts are indexed in:

- [`03_cases/Case_Index.md`](../03_cases/Case_Index.md);
- [`03_cases/Case_Index.yaml`](../03_cases/Case_Index.yaml).

The case files remain below canonical prose in the authority order and cannot modify the rules they test.

<a id="17-2-case-1-simple-linear-path"></a>

### 17.2 Case 1: Simple Linear Path

`C17-LINEAR-01` tests the minimum positive difference between an ordered source field and a bounded Path.

The synthetic service-activation episode contains four typed structures:

```text
validated intake at t0
→ eligibility verification at t1
→ authorization at t2
→ completed activation at t3
```

The configuration and transitions are independently identifiable. The three continuation relations are declared, and each transition changes what continuation is available next. Verification makes authorization available; authorization conditions activation. The target is therefore not an arbitrary list of dates.

#### Selection, order, and formation

The Selection Rule includes exactly the intake, verification, authorization, and activation structures. Surrounding communications, motives, institutional comparisons, and downstream service outcomes are excluded from the claim.

The Ordering Rule is linear because the synthetic packet directly declares `t0 < t1 < t2 < t3` and no concurrent or disputed order is material.

The Formation Rule states that a Path is formed only where all four structures share the same episode reference and the transitions preserve continuation dependency. An unordered set or mere date list would not satisfy the rule.

```text
correct order
+ continuation-sensitive transitions
→ bounded Path candidate

correct order alone
≠ Path automatically
```

#### Claim ceiling

The result is a bounded Path. It does not establish:

- Trajectory;
- sedimentation;
- strong Path Dependence;
- causal mechanism;
- legitimacy of authorization;
- contextual target function;
- application authority.

Removing verification or authorization breaks the declared Path. Reversing the order defeats it. Removing fine actor-level deliberation does not affect the retained Path because that material was never constitutive of the claim.

#### Loss and alternatives

The case preserves object identity, order, transition relations, and continuation effects. It compresses exact micro-duration and local communications. It excludes motive, legal validity, and target-function claims. Uncertainty remains around exact micro-timing and local workload. Fine deliberative mechanisms are irrecoverable from the synthetic packet.

A rival Sequence and No-Composition remain recorded. They are not selected because the declared continuation relations add bounded praxeological purchase. No material Branch Point is asserted.

#### Local result

```text
local result:
admissible bounded Path

canonical mapping:
admissible
```

The case passes all twelve local audit stages without warning or error for the bounded claim. This does not validate PATH globally.

Artifacts:

- [Markdown reconstruction](../03_cases/markdown/C17-LINEAR-01_Simple_Linear_Path.md)
- [YAML Shared Transformation Record](../03_cases/yaml/C17-LINEAR-01_Simple_Linear_Path.yaml)

#### Counterpressure

The case is defeated if the same four timestamps are retained but continuation relations are removed. That mutation yields an ordered source field or Sequence, not the present Path. This pressure anticipates `C17-CHRON-01` without producing that countercase in WP1.

<a id="17-3-case-2-branching-path"></a>

### 17.3 Case 2: Branching Path

`C17-BRANCH-01` tests whether a Path can preserve a historically available alternative, a realized continuation, an explicit rejection, and changed later reachability without converting alternatives into free counterfactual narrative.

The synthetic migration episode begins with a legacy-platform configuration. During a bounded decision window, two prepared continuations are supported:

1. immediate cutover;
2. staged migration through pilot and phased rollout.

The staged route is selected and traversed. Immediate cutover is explicitly rejected while the window remains open. A later compatibility freeze closes the original immediate-cutover route.

#### Branch Point

The Branch Point is supported by contemporaneous preparation for both continuations, not by retrospective imagination. Each branch had a distinct resource profile, ordering, and transition burden during the same historical window.

```text
later imaginable alternative
≠ historical branch

source-supported co-availability in a bounded window
→ Branch Point candidate
```

The realized route and rejected route retain different status. Rejection is not blockage; the packet records an explicit decision against immediate cutover, not an unavailable route at the decision cut.

#### Formation

The target branching Path is formed through four constitutive relations:

- legacy configuration to Branch Point;
- Branch Point to realized staged subpath;
- Branch Point to rejected immediate branch;
- realized subpath to later compatibility freeze and closure of original reachability.

The target preserves both the traversed route and the non-traversed but historically available continuation. A linearized account containing only staged migration would be materially incomplete.

#### Claim ceiling

The case supports:

- one bounded branching Path;
- one realized continuation;
- one rejected continuation;
- one later loss of original reachability.

It does not establish:

- that staged migration was rational, optimal, legitimate, or causally necessary;
- that the rejected branch would have succeeded;
- unrestricted counterfactual outcomes;
- strong Path Dependence;
- a target function.

#### Loss and alternatives

The record preserves the Branch Point, branch statuses, pilot and rollout order, later freeze, and reachability change. It compresses local pilot variation and routine implementation messages. It excludes motive, outcome superiority, and hypothetical future performance. Uncertainty remains around the exact distribution of local migration costs. Fine internal rejection deliberation is irrecoverable.

Rival compositions include a staged-subpath-only object and a source collection without branch formation. No-Composition remains available if branch co-availability or later reachability closure is removed from the packet.

#### Local result

```text
local result:
admissible branching Path

canonical mapping:
admissible
```

Artifacts:

- [Markdown reconstruction](../03_cases/markdown/C17-BRANCH-01_Branching_Path.md)
- [YAML Shared Transformation Record](../03_cases/yaml/C17-BRANCH-01_Branching_Path.yaml)

#### Counterpressure

If immediate cutover appears only in a later interview without contemporaneous preparation, it can no longer carry the Branch Point. The target must reduce to the realized staged Path or remain underdetermined. This anticipates the alternative-integrity duty `C17-ALT-01`.

<a id="17-4-case-3-trajectory-with-a-central-non-event"></a>

### 17.4 Case 3: Trajectory with a Central Non-Event

`C17-LAMBDA-01` is the first lock-critical standalone PATH artifact. It tests whether a warrantable central `Λ` can remain visible through a source-sensitive Trajectory composition without event inflation, strong-dependence inflation, or target-function assignment.

#### Source field

The synthetic packet contains:

- a baseline workload configuration at Q0;
- a formally expected Q1 review with a bounded realization window;
- preparation, scheduling, and interim-report events within that window;
- supported non-realization of the governing review;
- continued workaround activity;
- an uneven Q3 workload shift;
- later stabilization of the workaround as an operating default;
- partial repair that modifies but does not erase accumulated load and evidential residue.

The positive preparation events remain events. They do not realize the expected review.

```text
activity inside expected window
≠ realization of governing expected occurrence
```

#### Central Non-Event

The `Λ` claim is carried by an independently declared expectation relation, a defined Q1 window, and support for non-realization. It is not inferred from silence, missing records, or a blank graph interval.

The central Non-Event is constitutive because removing it changes the relation among baseline, workaround persistence, asymmetric load, default stabilization, and partial repair. Without the missed review, the declared formation rule no longer supports the same Trajectory.

#### From Path to Trajectory

The target exceeds a bounded Path only because earlier structure remains traceably active in later configurations:

- the temporary workaround persists;
- workload and repair burden shift unevenly;
- later planning treats the workaround as the operating baseline;
- partial repair inherits evidence and coordination residue;
- continuation corridors differ from the Q0 baseline.

```text
missed review
→ persistent workaround
→ accumulated asymmetry
→ default stabilization
→ partial repair with remaining residue
```

This is directionality without teleology. The source packet does not establish an original plan, inevitable development, future prediction, or global progress/decline vector.

#### `Λ`, `Ω`, `Α`, and `Θ`

The case preserves occurrence-level interaction without operator fusion:

- `Λ + Θ`: the missed review persists as historical load;
- `Ω + Θ`: workload and repair burdens accumulate unevenly;
- `Α + Θ`: the workaround becomes locally easier to continue and is treated as default;
- later repair changes the path without resetting it.

```text
profile interaction
≠ new operator
≠ additive Trajectory score
```

#### Path Dependence boundary

The case does not claim strong Path Dependence. It does not execute the full current-state-sufficiency and historical-indispensability burden for a tested present dimension.

```text
admissible Trajectory
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

The historical packet supports present carryover and source-sensitive Trajectory formation. A separate Chapter-12 property test would be required for a strong dependence claim.

#### Loss and alternatives

The record preserves the expectation relation, review window, positive sub-events, non-realization, workaround persistence, asymmetric load, default stabilization, partial repair, and open residue. It compresses repeated coordination messages and routine workaround activity. It excludes hidden intention, blame, legal duty, causal necessity, prediction, and all target functions. Uncertainty remains around the exact local distribution of burden. Fine causal mechanisms linking every adaptation are irrecoverable from the packet.

Rival routes remain visible:

- a bounded Path that preserves the missed review but withholds the Trajectory threshold;
- a positive-event-only chronology, rejected because it erases the governing `Λ`;
- No-Composition if the expectation or non-realization support is removed.

#### Counterfactual sensitivity

The target changes materially under each load-bearing mutation:

- remove the expectation relation → no warrantable `Λ`;
- retain expectation but remove non-realization support → unresolved realization status;
- remove later workaround carryover → bounded local `Λ`, not Trajectory;
- homogenize workload → asymmetry-dependent claim must reduce;
- replace partial repair with complete reset → present residual claim changes;
- assign a Frame or Attractor function → new `PROJECT_AS` record required.

#### Local result and mapping

```text
local result:
admissible source-sensitive Trajectory with central Λ

canonical mapping:
admissible
```

The ordinary boundedness already appears in Claim Ceiling, source ceiling, excluded reach, Loss, alternatives, and governance. It does not require a stronger claim or separate target function.

The twelve-stage local audit passes without warning or error for the declared synthetic claim. The record remains reopenable by the later countercase, confusion-case, integrated PATH audit, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, and corpus-wide audit.

Artifacts:

- [Markdown reconstruction](../03_cases/markdown/C17-LAMBDA-01_Trajectory_with_Central_Non_Event.md)
- [YAML Shared Transformation Record](../03_cases/yaml/C17-LAMBDA-01_Trajectory_with_Central_Non_Event.yaml)

#### Lock-critical status

This artifact satisfies the first of the three lock-critical Chapter-17 duties:

```text
admissible Trajectory composition
+ complete Loss
+ alternatives
+ local audit
+ canonical mapping
+ no target function
```

It does not by itself complete the Chapter-17 case corpus or provisionally lock Part I.

#### WP1 completion boundary

Sections 17.1–17.4 now establish:

- the distinction between chapter Pressure Cases and standalone case artifacts;
- positive, countercase, and confusion-case classes;
- the four-layer artifact contract;
- Shared-Record and owner-bound case-wrapper discipline;
- the twelve-stage local case-audit spine;
- complete Source, Claim Ceiling, Loss, alternatives, mapping, Stop/Non-Capture, and governance burdens;
- the Reader/Graph non-authority boundary;
- a bounded Simple Linear Path;
- a source-supported Branching Path;
- a lock-critical source-sensitive Trajectory with central `Λ`;
- three linked Markdown/YAML artifact sets;
- synchronized Markdown and YAML Case Indices;
- three WP1 Pressure Cases.

The local result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

The provisionality preserves WP2 counterpressure and lock-critical Macro-Label production, WP3 confusion cases and PATH Local Audit, WP4 integrated Part-I lock, SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, integrated cases, appendices, Reference Freeze, Corpus Audit, Model Finalization, derivatives, and Reader/Graph implementation.

```text
WP1 completion
≠ Chapter 17 provisional lock
≠ Part I provisional lock
≠ complete PATH case corpus
≠ empirical validation
≠ automatic Output-Class selection
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ target-function assignment
≠ application authority
≠ higher authority
```

The next controlled production step is:

```text
Chapter 17 — WP2:
Remaining Positive Cases and Countercases
```

<a id="17-5-case-4-similar-end-states-different-histories"></a>

### 17.5 Case 4: Similar End States, Different Histories

Two reconstructions may terminate in configurations that appear equivalent under a narrow present-state description while remaining praxeologically different because their histories carry different costs, residues, bindings, reversibility conditions, and available continuations. The PATH question is therefore not whether two endpoints can be described with the same label. It is whether the present configuration can be adequately reconstructed without the source-sensitive historical relations that produced it.

```text
similar endpoint description
≠ equivalent Path
≠ equivalent Trajectory
≠ identical Path Dependence
```

A disciplined comparison requires at least four separations:

1. **Endpoint equivalence** — which present features are actually alike?
2. **Historical difference** — which transitions, Non-Events, branches, and repairs differ?
3. **Current historical load** — which earlier structures still alter present costs, meanings, bindings, or continuations?
4. **Dependence dimension** — in which specific dimension is the history indispensable, and in which dimensions is the current state sufficient?

The case must not infer strong Path Dependence merely because the histories differ. Historical difference becomes dependence only where a present claim changes materially when the earlier order, transition, alternative, or accumulated load is removed or replaced. Conversely, a present-state description does not defeat dependence merely because the endpoints share a formal label.

Consider two synthetic service units that both reach a stable weekly allocation protocol. Unit A reaches the protocol through an early review, explicit redistribution, and low residual backlog. Unit B reaches the same formal protocol after repeated review deferral, a centralized workaround, accumulated asymmetric burden, and a late redistribution that leaves backlog and elevated exit costs. The endpoint label is similar, but the present burden, reversibility, evidence duty, and repair corridor remain historically differentiated.

The admissible result is dimension-specific. The history is constitutive to present burden, reversibility, and residual repair. It is not thereby proven constitutive to every present technical function, every later event, or every possible comparison.
