The selected Output Class therefore reflects material narrowing rather than a universal historical determination.

The standalone record `C17-HISTORY-01` forms a paired comparative Path object from two separately traceable histories. It preserves endpoint similarity, divergent transitions, the deferred review, accumulated Ω-load, the late repair, and the current residual difference. Its bounded claim is admissible only for the declared dependence dimensions; motive, causal necessity, global institutional quality, and target function remain outside the claim.

```text
historical indispensability in one dimension
≠ total historical determination
```

A comparison of similar endpoints is therefore strongest when it states both what the present state explains adequately and what it does not. This prevents two opposite errors: erasing history because the endpoint labels match, and inflating every historical difference into strong Path Dependence.

---

<a id="17-6-case-5-repeated-pattern-with-weak-path-dependence"></a>

### 17.6 Case 5: Repeated Pattern with Weak Path Dependence

Repeated structure may support a Path, an Attractor-bearing pattern, or a bounded Trajectory without supporting strong Path Dependence. Repetition shows that a configuration recurs. It does not by itself show that the current configuration is historically determined in a way that cannot be reconstructed from current conditions.

```text
repetition
+ duration
+ visible regularity
≠ strong Path Dependence automatically
```

The critical test compares two burdens:

- **historical contribution:** does earlier order change present meaning, cost, access, role, or continuation?
- **current-state sufficiency:** can the present configuration and its immediate governing conditions adequately explain the current pattern without detailed historical reconstruction?

A pattern may satisfy both in unequal measure. Earlier cycles can explain how a routine was learned or stabilized while the current rule, interface, resource distribution, and role arrangement now explain most of its continuation. In that case, PATH may retain a bounded repeated-pattern Path and weak order dependence while rejecting a stronger claim that the current state is unintelligible without the full history.

The synthetic case concerns eight weekly queue-allocation cycles. The same assignment pattern recurs, but the current scheduling rule, role access, and queue state are sufficient to reconstruct the next assignment. Removing the first four cycles reduces historical description but does not materially change the current role distribution, cost structure, or reachable continuation. Reordering two early cycles changes local narrative detail but not the present configuration.

This result is not `resolution_neutral`: the history still identifies the repeated pattern, its initial stabilization, and a bounded order effect. Nor is the strong dependence claim retained. The correct result is an admissible claim whose historical reach is materially narrowed.

The standalone record `C17-WEAKPD-01` therefore maps the repeated-pattern Path to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. It preserves recurrence and weak order dependence, excludes strong historical indispensability, and records the present rule set as the dominant current carrier. The result does not retype the pattern as an Attractor function and does not infer prediction, necessity, or organizational authority.

```text
weak dependence
≠ no history

current-state sufficiency
≠ history irrelevant
```

The counterpressure is important for the entire PATH part. Without it, any repeated structure could be made historically indispensable merely by narrating enough prior cycles. Chapter 17 instead requires a difference that the history makes to the present claim.

### 17.7 Countercase 1: Chronology Presented as Path

Chronology is necessary for PATH but insufficient for Path formation. An ordered source field may preserve dates, succession, and provenance while failing to establish the continuation-sensitive relations that make the target more than a sequence.

```text
accurate chronology
≠ formed Path
```

`C17-CHRON-01` begins with four dated implementation updates. Their order is directly supported, and the chronology remains useful for navigation. The proposed target nevertheless calls the updates an implementation Path without showing how one configuration changes the practical availability of the next, which alternatives were selected or foreclosed, or why removal of an intermediate update would alter the target identity.

The defect lies at the Praxeological Relevance Floor. The source field is traceable, but the additional Path label produces no warranted gain beyond the chronology. Correct dates cannot compensate for a missing Formation Rule.

The current stronger claim therefore maps to `claim_reduction_required`. The source field, dates, and ordered sequence remain recorded. A weaker chronology claim is concrete enough to justify a new test, but this record does not pre-authorize it as admissible.

```text
failed Path claim
≠ failed dates
≠ failed source field
```

The distinction protects both sides of the boundary. PATH must not retain a stronger label merely because the chronology is accurate; nor may failure of the Path claim erase a useful ordered source field. Mandatory Stop is not reached by the explicit reduction itself. It would become relevant only if the failed Path claim were nevertheless used for intervention, ranking, target-function assignment, or authority inheritance.

- [YAML Shared Transformation Record](../03_cases/yaml/C17-CHRON-01_Chronology_Presented_as_Path.yaml)

### 17.8 Countercase 2: Macro-Label without Traceable Path

A macro-label may summarize a long period while remaining analytically detached from the source structures that should constitute it. The Traceability Ceiling is crossed where the target survives decisive changes to transitions, branches, `Λ`, asymmetries, reversals, or repairs that are presented as its historical basis.

```text
broad period label
+ many sources
≠ traceable Path
```

`C17-MACRO-01` uses a twelve-year synthetic source field containing regional adoption, a documented branch divergence, delayed review, reversal, fallback, and partial repair. The proposed target is a single “progressive national modernization trajectory.” Its Formation Rule does not distinguish this source field from materially different histories. Removing the reversal, replacing the delayed review with an on-time transition, or reversing regional order leaves the label unchanged.

The defect is not merely insufficient detail. It is absent source-to-result dependency. The target is source-indifferent and overelastic. It therefore sits above the Praxeological Traceability Ceiling.

The attempted `COMPOSE` maps to `failed_transformation`. Source identity, heterogeneity, order, Loss, and rival bounded compositions remain preserved; the asserted macro-Trajectory is not formed. Narrower regional Paths or a chronology remain possible only through new target objects and separate records.

```text
source material preserved
≠ failed macro target retained
```

This is the second lock-critical Chapter-17 artifact set. It demonstrates that citation volume, visual coherence, period breadth, or a connected graph cannot substitute for traceable load. Use of the failed macro-label for regional ranking, intention attribution, intervention, or authority inheritance would trigger `mandatory_stop`; the present record classifies the attempted operation itself as failed and preserves that prospective Stop boundary separately.

Artifacts:

- [Markdown reconstruction](../03_cases/markdown/C17-MACRO-01_Macro_Label_without_Traceable_Path.md)
- [YAML Shared Transformation Record](../03_cases/yaml/C17-MACRO-01_Macro_Label_without_Traceable_Path.yaml)

### 17.9 Countercase 3: Teleological Composition

Teleological composition occurs when a later endpoint determines which earlier sources count, which branches remain visible, and which reversals are redescribed as steps toward the realized result. The endpoint is then not merely the end of the reconstruction. It becomes an unacknowledged Selection Rule.

```text
realized endpoint
≠ original purpose
≠ historical necessity
```

`C17-TEL-01` concerns a centralized platform adopted after several heterogeneous pilots. The proposed Trajectory selects the adoption-aligned episodes, redescribes failed pilots as preliminary learning, and excludes a source-supported federated branch because it did not become the final architecture. The resulting direction appears continuous only because the endpoint has already determined the constitutive source set.

A valid PATH reconstruction could still describe the realized centralized Path. It would need to preserve the failed pilots as failures, the federated branch as historically available where supported, the reversal points, and uncertainty around later selection. It could not infer that the final platform was the latent goal of the earlier episodes.

The current `COMPOSE` maps to `failed_transformation`. Selection and formation are endpoint-conditioned, alternatives are removed, and Counterfactual Sensitivity is circular: sources count as constitutive only when they resemble the known result.

```text
softer teleological language
≠ non-teleological formation
```

Replacing “necessary” with “likely,” “mature,” “natural,” or “progressive” does not repair the occurrence while the same selection logic remains. The source episodes remain available for a new bounded Path record. The current Trajectory claim does not.

- [Markdown reconstruction](../03_cases/markdown/C17-TEL-01_Teleological_Composition.md)
- [YAML Shared Transformation Record](../03_cases/yaml/C17-TEL-01_Teleological_Composition.yaml)

### 17.10 Countercase 4: Composition through Omitted Asymmetry

Equal event labels and matching formal milestones do not establish equivalent Paths or a common Trajectory where access, exposure, exit cost, repair burden, or role load differ materially.

```text
same milestone labels
≠ same practical load
≠ Path equivalence
```

`C17-OMEGA-01` contains two implementation tracks that pass through the same authorization, deployment, and stabilization milestones. Track A has direct access, reversible commitments, and low interruption cost. Track B has restricted access, cumulative delay exposure, approval dependency, higher exit cost, and additional repair and evidence burden.

The attempted target forms one uniform rollout Trajectory from milestone equivalence alone. Its Selection Rule includes the shared events while its Formation Rule omits the occurrence-level `Ω` structures that make the endpoint practically different. Equalizing or removing the high-exposure, exit-cost, or repair-load sources leaves the target unchanged. The target is therefore source-indifferent in the dimension presented as constitutive.

The attempted `COMPOSE` maps to `failed_transformation`. The shared milestones remain valid; the differentiated source Paths, Loss, and alternatives remain available. The uniform Trajectory does not. A dual-Path comparison or milestone chronology would require new target objects and records.

```text
omitted asymmetry
+ correct event order
≠ admissible Trajectory
```

The case remains structural. It does not infer blame, legitimacy, responsibility, motive, institutional quality, or intervention authority. Use of the failed uniform label for ranking, responsibility attribution, intervention, or authority inheritance would trigger `mandatory_stop`; that prospective Stop is not the current operation result.

### 17.11 Countercase 5: False Central Non-Event

A gap in the record does not become a structured Non-Event merely because a later event makes the interval narratively salient. `Λ` requires a supported expectation frame, expected window, non-occurrence, and praxeological consequence.

```text
missing information
≠ Non-Event
```

`C17-FALSEL-01` contains intake, status communication, a later escalation, and an interval without a review record. The attempted Trajectory treats the interval as an expected-but-unrealized review and makes it the central `Λ` that generated escalation. The source packet, however, establishes no review obligation, expected window, responsible role, affected alternative, or practical consequence of non-review.

The stronger claim therefore maps to `claim_reduction_required`. Positive event identities, order, and the explicit source gap remain preserved. The central `Λ`, its asserted causal role, and the resulting Trajectory are withdrawn. A chronology or bounded event Path remains a new testable claim and receives no inherited admissibility.

```text
later escalation
≠ proof of prior expected review
```

The case is not `non_capture`: a precise weaker source-field formulation remains available. It is not `mandatory_stop` for the explicit reduction. Mandatory Stop becomes relevant only if the unsupported `Λ` is retained for responsibility attribution, intervention, or authority inheritance.

### 17.12 Confusion Case: Path or Projection?

A historical object may later matter inside another context. That fact does not collapse its temporal formation into its contextual function.

```text
PATH reconstructs how an object developed.
RETYPE tests what bounded function that origin-typed object performs elsewhere.
```

`C17-PROJ-01` reconstructs a service-coordination history from ad hoc allocation through review, repeated rotation, redistributed burden, and a stable endpoint with residual commitments. The source field supports one source-sensitive Trajectory. Removal of the review, failure of the rotation, or retention of concentrated burden changes the target identity.

A later governance meeting may interpret present allocation through this history. The proposition that the Trajectory functions as a Frame in that later scene is nevertheless not a PATH result. It requires a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence with origin type, target context, target function, source trace, Counterfactual Sensitivity, Loss, alternatives, and an independent output mapping.

```text
COMPOSE(history → Trajectory)
≠
PROJECT_AS(Trajectory → Frame-function)
```

The current case therefore retains the Trajectory and separates the function claim. It maps to `admissible_with_bounded_claim` because the combined trajectory-as-frame formulation is materially narrowed to the origin Trajectory. The pending projection claim receives no inherited admissibility and is not silently instantiated.

This is the third lock-critical Chapter-17 artifact set. Its lock value lies not in proving a Frame-function, but in demonstrating that PATH can finish without preempting RETYPE.

### 17.13 Confusion Case: Path or Resolution Drift?

Additional temporal detail can be valid, source-supported, and still fail to produce a new PATH finding. It also does not become `DECOMPOSE` merely because an interface exposes more timestamps. `C17-RES-01` therefore distinguishes interface expansion from a separately declared analytical operation.

```text
more timestamps
≠ stronger Path
≠ DECOMPOSE automatically
```

`C17-RES-01` begins with a previously warranted service Path containing intake, review, allocation, stabilization, and an open repair residue. Seventy-two hourly status, queue, acknowledgement, and routing entries are added. The distinctions are accurate and traceable. They do not change a configuration boundary, transition identity, available alternative, burden distribution, residue, source function, target identity, or Claim Ceiling.

The interface expansion alone is not an operation. The case record then separately declares a `DECOMPOSE` occurrence on the prior Path composite: source reference, finer granularity, candidate components, relations, preservation burden, Changed-Reconstruction Test, Loss, and alternatives are explicit. The valid finer reconstruction leaves the warranted Path unchanged and therefore maps to `resolution_neutral`.

```text
resolution_neutral
≠ failed source detail
≠ proof that no future distinction can matter
```

The denser display is not SUB by itself. The **separately declared record is** a valid `DECOMPOSE` test because it opens a composite under finer resolution while preserving reference and source function. A valid DECOMPOSE occurrence may end without changed reconstruction; finer granularity receives no truth priority and generates no new PATH object.

Optional Stop applies to repetition of the identical refinement without changed grounds. Changed grounds require a new record. The completed neutral result does not become `mandatory_stop`.

### 17.14 Confusion Case: Trajectory or Attractor?

A Trajectory may contain repetition, recurrence, or a recognizable course-form. Those features do not by themselves turn the historical object into an Attractor or establish an Attractor-function.

```text
repetition inside one history
≠ Attractor identity
≠ Attractor-function automatically
```

`C17-ATTR-01` reconstructs one maintenance-program history containing two connected escalation-repair cycles, partial rather than resetting repair, residual backlog, and changed continuation cost. Earlier cycles materially alter later repair burden and the endpoint. The source field therefore supports one Trajectory.

The Attractor claim adds a different question: whether the origin-typed Trajectory performs a bounded recurrent stabilizing function in a declared target context. That question belongs to `PROJECT_AS`. It requires more than the presence of repetition inside the source history and cannot be satisfied by label substitution.

The current case maps to `admissible_with_bounded_claim`. The Trajectory is retained; Attractor identity, cross-context recurrence, prediction, and target function are excluded. A later Attractor-function claim must preserve the origin type `trajectory` and receive its own record and test.

```text
Trajectory remains Trajectory.
Projected Attractor-function, if any, remains contextual and bounded.
```

### 17.15 PATH Local Audit

The PATH Local Audit is a chapter-level integration test over the thirteen instantiated Chapter-17 records. It does not replace the twelve-stage audit stored in each record. The record audit tests one declared occurrence; the chapter audit asks whether the case corpus as a whole discriminates the PATH object family, the local boundaries, the operation boundary, and the available failure routes.

```text
thirteen schema-valid records
≠ automatic PATH completion

record-level audit passage
+ chapter-level discrimination
+ lock-critical artifact completeness
= Part-I lock readiness only after all tests pass
```

The corpus under audit contains five positive cases, five countercases, and three confusion cases. Twelve records test `COMPOSE`; `C17-RES-01` declares one bounded `DECOMPOSE` occurrence solely to prevent interface expansion or finer detail from being mislabeled as a new PATH object. No record executes `PROJECT_AS`.

| # | Local audit question | Integrated finding | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Is the Frame declared? | Every record declares a bounded source Frame and claim context. | pass |
| 2 | Is temporal scope determined? | Every case identifies the tested interval, ordering horizon, or bounded synthetic time field. | pass |
| 3 | Are configurations and transitions separated? | Positive cases form explicit transitions; countercases expose where mere event order fails to establish them. | pass |
| 4 | Are events and Non-Events distinguished? | `C17-LAMBDA-01` preserves a warranted central `Λ`; `C17-FALSEL-01` rejects an unsupported one. | pass |
| 5 | Is the Path more than chronology? | `C17-LINEAR-01` and `C17-BRANCH-01` pass; `C17-CHRON-01` reduces the stronger claim while preserving chronology. | pass |
| 6 | Is the Trajectory more than a Path? | `C17-LAMBDA-01` preserves sedimented historical load; macro, teleological, and omitted-`Ω` constructions fail. | pass |
| 7 | Is Path Dependence separately warranted? | `C17-HISTORY-01` bounds dependence by dimension; `C17-WEAKPD-01` blocks repetition-to-strong-dependence inflation. | pass |
| 8 | Are Branches and lost alternatives visible? | `C17-BRANCH-01` records realized and unrealized continuations; all records carry alternatives or an explicit No-Transformation route. | pass |
| 9 | Is COMPOSE selection justified? | Positive cases disclose selection, order, and Formation Rule; countercases fail where selection is narrative, teleological, or source-indifferent. | pass |
| 10 | Is complete Loss documented? | All thirteen records contain `preserved`, `compressed`, `excluded`, `uncertain`, and `irrecoverable`. | pass |
| 11 | Does composition create praxeological purchase? | Positive cases change warranted reconstruction; chronology-only and source-indifferent targets do not. | pass |
| 12 | Does the Path remain reconstructible? | Passing objects retain source-to-transition trace; `C17-MACRO-01` fails precisely because the macro-target does not. | pass |
| 13 | Is the target counterfactually sensitive? | Constitutive source changes alter passing targets; overelastic targets fail or are reduced. | pass |
| 14 | Is teleology avoided? | `C17-TEL-01` makes endpoint-conditioned selection a failed transformation rather than a direction claim. | pass |
| 15 | Is a target function withheld inside PATH? | `C17-PROJ-01` and `C17-ATTR-01` retain origin Trajectories while deferring Frame- and Attractor-functions to separate `PROJECT_AS` records. | pass |
| 16 | Is origin type preserved? | No Path or Trajectory becomes □, Α, a new primitive, or a target function by composition or naming. | pass |
| 17 | Is the Claim Ceiling calibrated? | Bounded positive mappings, Claim Reductions, neutral resolution, and failed occurrences remain claim-specific. | pass |
| 18 | Is a Stop condition declared? | Every record preserves Optional Stop and prospective `mandatory_stop` triggers without converting Stop into the current result unless warranted. | pass |
| 19 | Does Non-Capture remain possible? | Every record assesses Non-Capture; weaker available formulations prevent its misuse as protection for failed claims. | pass |
| 20 | Does PATH create no additional authority? | Every record prohibits authority inheritance and withholds diagnosis, ranking, legitimacy, intervention, and application authority. | pass |

The audit passes all twenty controls. Passage does not mean that every source field yields a Path, that every Path yields a Trajectory, or that every future case is capturable. It means that the local discipline can preserve successful objects, reduce excessive claims, fail inadmissible operations, stop misuse, and leave Non-Capture available without changing operation identity.

#### Loss, alternatives, Stop, and Non-Capture integration

The four remaining corpus-wide duties are satisfied as follows:

- `C17-LOSS-01`: all thirteen records contain the canonical five-part Loss structure; empty categories are explicit and do not imply losslessness;
- `C17-ALT-01`: every record carries rival compositions, rival operations, No-Transformation, or unresolved alternatives as appropriate;
- `C17-STOP-01`: Optional Stop remains available when further differentiation repeats the same result, while `mandatory_stop` remains reserved for prohibited continuation or use;
- `C17-NC-01`: Non-Capture remains a possible output where no bounded single object survives, but it is not selected where chronology, a reduced Path, competing constructions, or a failed occurrence can be stated more precisely.

```text
available weaker claim
≠ non_capture automatically

failed transformation
≠ mandatory_stop automatically

optional stop
≠ canonical Output Class
```

---

### 17.16 PATH Output Classes

PATH uses local object descriptions for explanation and maps them only to the ten canonical STRATA Output Classes. A local label such as “admissible Path,” “bounded Trajectory,” or “competing construction” is not an eleventh class.

The thirteen Chapter-17 records instantiate five canonical classes:

| Canonical Output Class | Cases | PATH meaning in this corpus |
|---|---|---|
| `admissible` | `C17-LINEAR-01`, `C17-BRANCH-01`, `C17-LAMBDA-01` | the tested bounded Path or Trajectory is retained as claimed |
| `admissible_with_bounded_claim` | `C17-HISTORY-01`, `C17-WEAKPD-01`, `C17-PROJ-01`, `C17-ATTR-01` | the source object survives only after dependence, function, identity, or reach is materially narrowed |
| `resolution_neutral` | `C17-RES-01` | a valid finer reconstruction changes no warranted PATH finding and creates no stronger truth claim |
| `claim_reduction_required` | `C17-CHRON-01`, `C17-FALSEL-01` | chronology or positive event field remains while the stronger Path, Trajectory, or central-`Λ` claim is withdrawn |
| `failed_transformation` | `C17-MACRO-01`, `C17-TEL-01`, `C17-OMEGA-01` | the declared `COMPOSE` occurrence fails while source materials and separately testable weaker routes remain |

The remaining canonical classes are preserved without artificial instantiation:

- `admissible_but_provisional` remains available where a bounded object survives with material unresolved support; the chapter-production status itself is provisional but is not a substitute case record;
- `partially_admissible` remains available for materially separable supported and unsupported claim segments;
- `analogy_only` remains available principally at projection and cross-domain boundaries and is not manufactured inside PATH;
- `mandatory_stop` remains available when prohibited continuation or use is current, not merely prospective;
- `non_capture` remains available when no responsible bounded single capture survives.

```text
class not selected in this corpus
≠ class unavailable
≠ missing quota case
```

Output mapping remains a reasoned result of the full applicable record pattern. Counts, first-match logic, scoring, class ranking, or one local Rule cannot select the route automatically.

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

This chapter-level result records completed local production with the Part-I lock still pending the integrated WP4 synchronization. It does not overwrite any case result and does not create a fourteenth transformation record.

### 17.17 PATH Closing Statement

PATH specifies how temporally ordered configurations, transitions, events, Non-Events, Branches, alternatives, asymmetries, bindings, and residual structures may be selected and composed into bounded Sequences, Paths, Trajectories, and dimension-specific Path-Dependence claims.

PATH requires that:

- temporal order produce praxeological purchase rather than chronology alone;
- `COMPOSE` disclose selection, ordering, Formation Rule, constitutive relations, and five-part Loss;
- Path, Trajectory, and Path Dependence remain distinct;
- central `Λ` and `Ω` loads remain source-supported and reconstructible;
- direction remain separate from teleology;
- macro-abstraction remain below the Traceability Ceiling;
- later contextual function remain outside PATH until a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence is declared;
- finer resolution receive no truth priority and no automatic SUB status;
- Claim Reduction, failure, Stop, and Non-Capture remain available without erasing source fields;
- no successful temporal reconstruction inherit application or normative authority.

```text
PATH closes the temporal-composition question.
It does not close the source object against later revision.
It does not execute DECOMPOSE merely by exposing detail.
It does not assign a contextual target function.
It does not grant additional authority.
```

The thirteen cases demonstrate that the discipline can discriminate rather than merely assimilate:

```text
chronology may remain without Path
Path may remain without Trajectory
Trajectory may remain without strong Path Dependence
history may remain relevant without total determination
repetition may remain without Attractor identity
source material may remain when COMPOSE fails
projection may remain pending without being pre-authorized
```

#### Part I — PATH provisional-lock boundary

Part I is provisionally locked after integrated audit of Chapters 9–17, the thirteen standalone Chapter-17 case records, the Chapter-17 Local Audit, the PATH Gate, the Reference Layer, the Formal Model mirror, and the release package. The lock closes the present PATH production cycle; it does not make PATH universally sufficient, empirically validated, ontologically final, or immune from reopening under a concrete conflict.

The integrated ownership chain is preserved:

| Chapter | Canonical owner contribution | Lock finding |
|---|---|---|
| 9 | temporal position, order, duration, delay, persistence, bounded irreversibility, transition gate | retained |
| 10 | Path threshold, actual traversal, selection, status, comparison, Minimal Path Record | retained |
| 11 | Trajectory threshold, sedimentation, historical load, direction without teleology | retained |
| 12 | Path Dependence as separately tested property, current-state sufficiency, reduction and re-entry | retained |
| 13 | Branches, aborts, delays, alternatives, bounded counterfactual paths, alternative-space loss | retained |
| 14 | source-supported Non-Events, expectation frame, window, non-realization, false-`Λ` control | retained |
| 15 | `COMPOSE` selection, ordering, Formation Rule, constitutive relations, five-part Loss, failure | retained |
| 16 | PATH-local Relevance Floor and Traceability Ceiling, compression, teleology, Stop, Non-Capture | retained |
| 17 | positive, counter, and confusion cases, canonical mappings, Local Audit, closing statement | retained |

The Part-I completion tests pass:

1. chronology, Sequence, Transition set, Path, Trajectory, and Path Dependence remain distinct;
2. `Θ`, duration, repetition, visual direction, or source volume alone establish none of the stronger PATH claims;
3. `COMPOSE` preserves source typing, selection, ordering, constitutive relations, alternatives, central `Λ` and `Ω` load, complete Loss, uncertainty, and Claim Ceiling;
4. Path Dependence remains dimension-specific, current-state pressured, non-deterministic, and separately testable from Trajectory;
5. directionality remains distinct from teleology, and endpoint-conditioned selection remains inadmissible;
6. a failed Path, Trajectory, or `COMPOSE` occurrence does not erase chronology, source fields, weaker objects, or separately testable operations;
7. finer resolution has no truth priority and does not constitute `DECOMPOSE` without a declared source composite and new operation record;
8. PATH assigns no Frame-, Attractor-, Macro-Event-, or other target function; any later function requires a separate `PROJECT_AS` occurrence;
9. all thirteen Chapter-17 records are schema-valid, contain twelve completed audit stages, five-part Loss, alternatives, Stop/Non-Capture assessment, canonical mapping, and prohibited authority inheritance;
10. all three lock-critical artifact sets are complete: `C17-LAMBDA-01`, `C17-MACRO-01`, and `C17-PROJ-01`;
11. the twenty-control PATH Local Audit passes, and all six integrated Chapter-17 duties are discharged;
12. the ten canonical Output Classes remain closed; selected-class counts are descriptive rather than quotas or routing rules;
13. Optional Stop, `mandatory_stop`, failed transformation, Claim Reduction, and `non_capture` remain distinct;
14. no frame, level, granularity, graph, SUB, RETYPE, or label change retroactively repairs a failed claim;
15. no PATH result establishes empirical truth, causal necessity, intention, diagnosis, person rank, legitimacy, sanction, recommendation, or application authority.

```text
Part I — PATH provisionally locked
≠ final PMS-STRATA lock
≠ universal PATH validity
≠ empirical validation
≠ completed DECOMPOSE
≠ PROJECT_AS authorization
≠ authority increase
```

The Part-I result is:

```text
admissible_but_provisional
```

This result records a complete and internally audited PATH corpus under the present Contracts and source authority. It does not overwrite the thirteen case mappings and does not create a fourteenth Transformation Record.

Reopening requires a concrete conflict, including at least one of the following:

- a contradiction with `PMS.yaml`, the minified kernel, or a Chapter/Block Contract;
- collapse of an operator type, occurrence, composite, Frame, granularity, relative level, origin type, or target function distinction;
- a broken Chapter-17 artifact, schema failure, incorrect canonical mapping, or incomplete Loss/alternatives/audit/governance record;
- a source- or claim-boundary defect that changes a locked PATH result;
- a later SUB, RETYPE, LIMITS, integrated-case, or Corpus Audit finding that exposes an actual unresolved PATH dependency;
- a broken fingerprint, reference, link, package, or roundtrip condition relevant to the locked state.

Preference for more detail, a cleaner graph, a stronger class, fewer visible losses, one dominant periodization, or a more decisive narrative is not a reopening ground.

The controlled handoff is:

```text
Part I — PATH provisional lock
→ Chapter 18 Preparation
→ The Provisionally Compressed Object
→ SUB without inherited truth or authority
```
